# 0.0.241 — AI provider atomic result commit closure

- Add `AI_PROVIDER_RESULT_COMMIT_AUTHORITY_V1`: successful provider output is now finalized through one Store operation that creates/replays the durable `ai_run` and transitions its matching `ai_execution_claims` row from `DISPATCHED` to `COMPLETED` atomically.
- PostgreSQL performs run insert, audit/outbox emission and claim terminalization inside one serializable transaction; FileStore performs one persisted snapshot mutation. The canonical Operator diagnosis and model-backed Marketplace paths no longer call `CreateAIRun` and `CompleteAIExecution` as two independent durable writes.
- Add MemoryStore and scripted PostgreSQL owner tests proving the run and terminal claim commit together, and strengthen the black-box AI control-plane smoke to verify a successful FileStore run is already bound to a `COMPLETED` claim while the intentionally crash-unknown provider request remains `DISPATCHED` and is never redispatched.
- Preserve certification truth: local package networking was unavailable, so a real PostgreSQL server could not be installed in this session. `AI_RUN_POSTGRES_DURABILITY_RUNTIME_CERTIFICATION_PENDING` remains open and Exact-SHA Physical Runtime is not inferred from source/FileStore/scripted-SQL evidence.

# 0.0.240 — AI provider durable dispatch idempotency closure

- Close a real external-AI idempotency race: Operator diagnosis and canonical Marketplace model advisory now create a durable `ai_execution_claims` dispatch fence **before** provider egress instead of relying only on the `ai_runs` row written after a successful model response. `(project_id,idempotency_key)` is unique across PostgreSQL, FileStore and MemoryStore authority.
- Add `AI_PROVIDER_DISPATCH_AUTHORITY_V1` and rolling-safe migration `0054_ai_execution_dispatch_authority.sql`. Concurrent duplicate requests with the same request digest observe the existing `DISPATCHED` claim and cannot spend a second provider call; request-digest/purpose reuse conflicts fail closed.
- Make uncertain outcomes explicit and safe: successful dispatch claims bind to the durable `ai_run`; rejected/provider-failed calls become terminal `FAILED`; a process crash after durable dispatch but before run persistence leaves `DISPATCHED` and the same key is never automatically redispatched. Explicit retry requires a new Idempotency-Key, preferring at-most-once provider dispatch over hidden duplicate cost when remote outcome is uncertain.
- Extend exact runtime smoke semantics: the API is intentionally terminated while a mock provider call is in flight, the FileStore is reopened from disk, and the same key must return `AI_EXECUTION_ALREADY_DISPATCHED` without a second provider request. Unit negative controls also cover concurrent Operator and Marketplace calls.
- Preserve certification truth: this materially strengthens Source/Generated-Runtime failure semantics but does not close `AI_UNIFIED_RUNTIME_EXTERNAL_PROVIDER_CERTIFICATION_PENDING` or `AI_RUN_POSTGRES_DURABILITY_RUNTIME_CERTIFICATION_PENDING`; no real approved external provider or physical PostgreSQL campaign was executed here.

# 0.0.239 — AI provider transport and JSON ambiguity closure

- Close a real external-provider trust-boundary defect: AI provider endpoints now require HTTPS unless the endpoint host is explicit loopback (`localhost`, `127.0.0.0/8`, or `::1`), preventing an API-key-bearing request from being configured over remote plaintext HTTP. URL credentials/fragments and non-HTTP(S) schemes fail closed.
- Add `AI_PROVIDER_TRANSPORT_AUTHORITY_V1` to live AI policy/program evidence. Provider redirects are validated against the same transport rules and must remain on the exact same origin, preventing cross-origin credential egress or HTTPS-to-HTTP downgrade through a redirect.
- Reject duplicate JSON object keys both in OpenAI Responses/OpenAI-compatible provider envelopes and in the model's structured JSON output before normalization/persistence, closing last-key-wins ambiguity across provider/proxy/parser boundaries.
- Preserve release truth: this closes Source/Generated-Runtime transport and parsing semantics only. `AI_UNIFIED_RUNTIME_EXTERNAL_PROVIDER_CERTIFICATION_PENDING` remains open until a real approved external provider is exercised with exact-release evidence; Exact-SHA Physical Runtime is still not inferred.

# 0.0.238 — OCI producer interoperability and bounded admission closure

- Close a real producer-compatibility gap in `internal/ociarchive`: standard OCI Image Index metadata such as index `mediaType`/`annotations` and descriptor `platform` is now admitted instead of being rejected as unknown JSON. This keeps strict duplicate/unknown-field parsing while explicitly modeling the OCI fields real build/export tooling commonly emits.

# 0.0.235 — Workload OCI import-addressability authority closure

- Close an air-gap/runtime boundary where the management workload OCI archive could prove every image manifest/config/layer byte while failing to prove that containerd would import those bytes under the exact digest-pinned image references used by Kubernetes manifests. Top-level OCI descriptors must now bind each inventory reference through both `io.containerd.image.name` and `org.opencontainers.image.ref.name`; missing or mismatched name bindings fail closed in both Go bundle/bootstrap admission and the independent Lab runner.
- Upgrade the embedded archive contract to `MANAGEMENT_WORKLOAD_OCI_ARCHIVE_INVENTORY_V2` and record `MANAGEMENT_WORKLOAD_OCI_IMPORT_ADDRESSABILITY_V1` inside the inventory itself. Inventory manifest digests must be unique so repository/reference ownership cannot be ambiguous.
- Rebind every deterministic OCI fixture used by Bundle Builder, Bootstrap, Lifecycle, Host Deployment and Installer smoke paths to emit import-addressable descriptors, and add independent Go/Python negative controls that strip the descriptor annotations and require rejection before build/install.
- Phase C remains truthfully blocked on the same four runtime/source blockers; source-lock completeness remains `2 resolved / 2 partial / 1 missing + 1 derived`. This source/runtime-semantics closure does not claim that RKE2/containerd has physically imported the exact release archive; that remains part of physical Lab certification.

# 0.0.234 — Workload OCI archive content authority closure

- Close an air-gap authority gap where bundle admission verified the workload archive file digest and the declared `requiredImages` independently but did not prove the archive actually contained those images. Add `internal/ociarchive` and require a real OCI Image Layout with verified `oci-layout`, top-level index, content-addressed manifest/config/layer blobs and the embedded `MANAGEMENT_WORKLOAD_OCI_ARCHIVE_INVENTORY_V1`; unreferenced/tampered blobs and image-set mismatch fail closed in both Bundle Builder and Bootstrap admission.
- Upgrade Lab automatic acquisition to `LAB_APPLIANCE_BUNDLE_ACQUISITION_LOCK_V6`: the source universe is now five source authorities plus one derived authority. `digest-pinned-core-workload-images` is derived from the cryptographically verified management workload OCI archive instead of being a separately supplied product-owned truth, removing a duplicated authority that could drift from the archive bytes.
- Rebind Installer HTTP/host/remote smoke fixtures and lifecycle/host-deployment owner fixtures to deterministic real OCI-layout archives whose image references come from their actual manifest digests. Add OCI parser negative controls for blob tamper/unreferenced content and Lab V6 source/derived contract coverage.
- Source-lock truth improves structurally from `2 resolved / 2 partial / 2 missing` to `2 resolved / 2 partial / 1 missing + 1 derived`; Phase C remains blocked on the same four runtime/source blockers because the real product-owned management workload OCI archive is not produced in this environment and RKE2/Argo CD remain partial. No Generated/Installed Runtime or Exact-SHA Physical PASS is inferred.

An OCM-free build omits the manifest from `bundle.json`, the artifact lock and the air-gap image/index authority instead of synthesizing an empty or required dependency.

OCM remains available only when explicitly supplied; automatic Lab acquisition continues to reject unowned OCM input until a source authority exists.

# 0.0.232 — Bundle input-pack canonicality closure

- Upgrade automatic acquisition to `LAB_APPLIANCE_BUNDLE_ACQUISITION_LOCK_V5` without changing source-completeness truth (`2 resolved / 2 partial / 2 missing`). V5 preserves exact source-byte/build-role binding and adds a canonical input-pack boundary: files-only single-root ZIPs, no duplicate paths, no encryption/symlink/special entries, bounded file count and bounded total uncompressed bytes.
- Reject ambiguous JSON before any build: acquisition locks, external build specs and core-image inventories now reject duplicate keys. Malformed/non-object external build specs raise structured runtime errors instead of escaping the acquisition path through `SystemExit`.
- Require a ready input pack to contain exactly the canonical build spec plus authority-owned staged files. Extra/unowned files are rejected even when no build-spec field references them, closing hidden-payload and pack-ambiguity paths before `platformctl appliance-bundle build`.
- Add owner negative controls for duplicate lock keys, non-object/duplicate-key build specs, duplicate ZIP paths, decompression-size limits, unowned pack files and a positive exact-owned-file-set control. Phase C remains truthfully blocked on the same four runtime/source blockers; no Generated/Installed Runtime or Exact-SHA Physical PASS is inferred.

# 0.0.231 — Bundle source lock V4 input-pack source binding

- Correct a false RKE2 pending-source provenance carried by 0.0.230: official tag `v1.34.10+rke2r1` resolves to commit `d419f09226d50a4777d348e5c53ea1bce3849b77`, and tagged `install.sh` resolves to Git blob `88c5f55bdfde94f2277465ece2b749c52d86c69b`. The script remains partial until independent SHA-256/byte-size admission; provenance is not promoted to a byte lock.

Every locked/pending source now owns a unique canonical `stagingPath`; ready-pack extraction re-hashes every resolved artifact against the authority SHA-256/size before `platformctl appliance-bundle build` can run, preventing a digest-locked input pack from substituting bytes that differ from the per-source authority.
- Bind all currently consumed build-spec source roles to their exact owner authorities: RKE2 installer/install artifacts/offline images, management workload OCI archive, Argo CD, CloudNativePG and replicated-storage manifests. The core-image authority must contain exactly one inventory whose eight digest-pinned references equal the eight direct workload image references. Automatic `ocmManifest` is fail-closed until a canonical source authority owns it.
- Add owner negative controls for source-byte tamper, build-spec path substitution, core-image semantic drift and unowned OCM input, including an integration control proving tampered authoritative bytes block automatic acquisition before the first build command. The shipped production lock remains truthfully `2 resolved / 2 partial / 2 missing`; Phase C blocker count remains four and no Generated/Installed Runtime or Exact-SHA Physical PASS is inferred.

# 0.0.230 — Bundle source lock V3 multi-artifact upstream pinning

# 0.0.226 — Lab immutable bundle auto-acquisition authority closure

## 0.0.273 — Operator Console Assurance IA / Program Phase V6

## 0.0.227 — appliance bundle schema/runtime parity closure

- Fixed the appliance bundle JSON Schema/runtime drift: build specs now admit and require `metadata.sourceReleaseDigest`, both bundle schemas model the required replicated-storage manifest, and OCM is optional exactly as the builder/bootstrap runtime already implements it.
- Added a permanent owner-layer schema contract regression test and made the checked-in build example release-neutral instead of carrying a stale historical release number.
- Preserved Phase C truth: canonical production bundle source locks remain incomplete, so no Physical PASS or source-lock closure is inferred from this contract fix.

- replace the Phase-C manual-bundle-only gap with `LAB_APPLIANCE_BUNDLE_ACQUISITION_LOCK_V1`, shipped inside the exact release at `lab/appliance-bundle-acquisition-lock.json`. A Lab run without `bundleDirectory` no longer accepts caller-selected sources: it resolves only this product-owned lock, and an incomplete lock blocks before any network access with the missing authorities preserved as structured `missingAuthorities` evidence as well as operator-readable output.
- implement the ready-lock path without creating a release/bundle digest cycle: the release lock pins one bundle-input-pack ZIP by public HTTPS URL(s), exact byte size and SHA-256. The pack contains a release-version-bound appliance build spec with a canonical zero `sourceReleaseDigest` placeholder plus staged artifacts; the runner replaces only that placeholder with the exact release ZIP digest, builds with `platformctl` extracted from the same release, then independently verifies the sealed bundle before remote install.
- add fail-closed acquisition controls for URL credentials/query/fragment, non-HTTPS sources, oversized/size-mismatched/digest-mismatched packs, unsafe ZIP paths and non-regular entries, unexpected lock fields, version drift, unsafe build/staging paths and non-zero pre-bound source-release digests. Unit coverage proves an incomplete lock performs no download and a ready lock binds the generated build spec to the exact release artifact.
- tighten both provided-bundle and auto-acquired-bundle preflight so a bundle that verifies structurally but belongs to another product release is rejected before any server mutation: `platformctl appliance-bundle verify` output must bind `version`, `sourceReleaseDigest`, `bundleDigest` and `lockDigest` to the exact release ZIP. Also make warning/prerequisite/success primitives wrap-safe so long canonical blocker identifiers cannot regress mobile horizontal-overflow quality gates.
- keep release truth explicit: the acquisition **mechanism** is now implemented, but the shipped production lock remains intentionally `incomplete` because canonical RKE2/offline artifacts, management workload OCI archive, Argo CD/CloudNativePG/replicated-storage manifests and digest-pinned core workload images are not yet fully source-locked. Phase C therefore replaces the old mechanism blocker with `LAB_CANONICAL_BUNDLE_SOURCE_LOCKS_PENDING`; no Physical PASS is inferred.

# 0.0.225 — MCP 2026-07-28 external-client interoperability closure

- close a real Phase-C MCP wire-contract defect against the current 2026-07-28 protocol: requests now require the per-request protocol-version/client-capabilities envelope, transport headers are checked against the JSON-RPC body with `HeaderMismatch` (`-32020`), unsupported revisions return `UnsupportedProtocolVersion` (`-32022`), unsafe `Mcp-Name` values support the standard base64 sentinel, and every successful response carries `resultType=complete` plus server identity metadata. Trailing JSON values are rejected instead of being silently ignored.
- add `scripts/mcp_external_client_certify.py`, a dependency-free black-box client that runs in a separate process over real TCP and validates `server/discover`, deterministic/cacheable `tools/list`, canonical read-only tool authority and project-scoped `operation_status`. The owner integration test also presents an operation from another project and requires the product handler to deny it with the project-scope MCP error.
- add `MCP_EXTERNAL_CLIENT_INTEROPERABILITY_V1` to the Lab/program authorities and remove only `AI_MCP_SCOPED_EXTERNAL_CLIENT_INTEROPERABILITY_PENDING` from Phase C after the TCP black-box and negative authorization controls pass. The server/runtime, immutable-bundle, external-AI, PostgreSQL-physical and canonical-GitHub blockers remain independent.
- preserve release truth: this interoperability closure is source/runtime-test evidence for MCP only. It is not Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS, does not certify an unavailable external AI provider, and does not imply canonical GitHub parity.

# 0.0.224 — Operator Experience full viewport/accessibility closure

- close the Phase-C Operator Experience source-certification gap with `OPERATOR_EXPERIENCE_VIEWPORT_ACCESSIBILITY_V1`: every one of the 16 Console routes and 6 Installer routes is checked at 320/390/768/1024/1440 widths; DOM naming, form labels, dialog naming, effective target sizing and horizontal overflow run in both LTR and RTL, while contrast runs on every route in both light and dark themes. Keyboard drawer traps/return-focus, data-table focus visibility, durable toast semantics and reduced-motion remain independent owner checks.
- fix explicit-theme correctness rather than weakening the gate: system-dark selectors are now scoped to the no-override state, explicit dark receives the complete product token/component overrides, and dark primary/secondary controls use contrast-safe backgrounds/foregrounds. A cross-preference negative control proves explicit light overrides OS dark and explicit dark overrides OS light.
- make the quality authority deterministic and fast by sampling settled states with transitions disabled only inside the contrast/DOM gate; motion behavior is still separately tested under `prefers-reduced-motion`. The full route coverage now completes without the previous repeated theme-dependent DOM scan.
- close an Exact-ZIP verification omission: `scripts/verify_release.py --full` now executes `scripts/smoke_ui_quality.py` from the extracted artifact after live/headless UI smoke, so source-tree UI success cannot bypass the extracted-source release path.
- remove only `OPERATOR_EXPERIENCE_FULL_VIEWPORT_ACCESSIBILITY_CERTIFICATION_PENDING` from Phase C and add the new authority/evidence references. Phase C remains `blocked` on six independent server/runtime/provider/MCP/GitHub blockers, and this source/extracted-artifact UI certification is not Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS.

# 0.0.223 — Lab M03 PostgreSQL same-runtime certification closure

- correct the M03 authority mismatch: the row now truthfully certifies a PostgreSQL primary restart/failover rather than an API restart, and is bound to the `production-ha` tier whose CloudNativePG runtime can satisfy the secure `verify-full` profile.
- wire M03 to the exact PostgreSQL runtime installed by the same M02 run instead of accepting an unrelated external database: the runner discovers the live CNPG primary, RW service and operator-reported server CA, creates a unique ephemeral certifier role/database inside that cluster, and reaches it only through strict management SSH plus TLS `verify-full`.
- keep certification credentials process-private: generated M03 passwords are never written to spec/state/command output, the private-stage sanitizer explicitly strips `M03_PASSWORD` and CA payloads, and sealed runtime evidence is rejected on digest mismatch or unredacted PostgreSQL credentials. A regression exposed and closed the underscore-form `M03_PASSWORD` redaction gap in the generic redactor path.
- execute fresh/replay migrations, authority/concurrency invariants, real CNPG primary-pod restart/postmaster-identity verification, backup/restore, post-restore immutable invariants and full three-node PostgreSQL/API recovery. Cleanup of every database owned by the unique certifier identity plus the role is mandatory; cleanup failure fails M03.
- promote M03 to `IMPLEMENTED` and make M00-M03 source automation complete, removing only `LAB_PHASE_C_MATRIX_M00_M03_AUTOMATION_PENDING`. Phase C remains `blocked` on physical server-driven runtime certification, immutable bundle acquisition, external AI/MCP/UX certification and canonical GitHub full-source parity. Row-level M03 success never implies the independent four-layer Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS.

# 0.0.222 — Lab M02 HA interruption and recovery closure

- close a real durable-operation ownership defect exposed by Phase-C HA certification design: after `platform-installer` restarted during a durable `RUNNING` bootstrap, the persisted run could remain `RUNNING` with no active worker, while `field-campaign watch` treated that state as live forever. Installer status now carries `bootstrapActive` into the campaign observer; a durable `RUNNING` run with no active worker becomes explicit `INTERRUPTED`, and only an explicit `field-campaign resume --confirmation RESUME` may re-own and continue that exact run/digest set.
- advance the field-campaign authority schema to v4 while retaining schema-v3 runtime-binding and schema-v2 exact-SHA compatibility. `INTERRUPTED` is a first-class durable campaign state with fail-closed transitions and owner-layer tests covering interruption detection and same-run resume.
- complete deterministic M02 source automation: the production-HA Lab runner waits for a replay-safe post-quorum bootstrap step, deliberately restarts the installer service, proves the campaign is `INTERRUPTED/RUNNING`, explicitly resumes it, waits for successful evidence collection, verifies full three-node runtime health, restarts `rke2-server` on one peer, re-verifies full HA, then reboots another peer and requires observed outage, degraded two-node quorum, a changed boot ID, active/enabled RKE2 service and full three-node API/PostgreSQL recovery.
- promote only M02 automation status from `PARTIAL` to `IMPLEMENTED`; M03 remains pending credential-safe remote PostgreSQL harness wiring, so Phase C remains blocked and `LAB_PHASE_C_MATRIX_M00_M03_AUTOMATION_PENDING` remains truthful. No source/local test or individual M02 row may be promoted to independent four-layer Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS.
- enforce the existing developer/source-history contract at the repository boundary: generated release evidence (`ARTIFACT-MANIFEST.json`, `BUILD-PROVENANCE.json`, `SBOM.spdx.json`) is now explicitly ignored in source history alongside generated binaries/releases/smoke evidence. Release ZIPs still generate and carry those files deterministically.

# 0.0.221 — Clean-clone bootstrap status concurrency closure

- close a clean-clone-only bootstrap test defect exposed by GitHub Actions: the status-concurrency owner test no longer combines `Simulation:false` with a simulated system adapter and therefore no longer depends on incidental host port availability on the CI runner.
- make the concurrency proof deterministic by blocking the simulated `prepare-host` filesystem mutation only after the durable run is already `RUNNING`, then asserting `Status()` remains readable while execution is blocked. This preserves the product contract while removing environment-dependent preflight timing/port assumptions.
- restart release identity after the 0.0.220 clean-clone failure; all artifact digests and Git evidence for this version must be bound to 0.0.221 rather than reusing 0.0.220 evidence.

# 0.0.220 — Lab M01 reboot and inventory authority closure

- close a real Lab SSH execution defect: multi-line remote scripts are now shell-quoted as one `sh -c` program before crossing the OpenSSH command-string boundary, with a regression proving spaces/quotes survive the serialization semantics instead of becoming positional parameters.
- make physical topology fail closed by rejecting duplicate physical hosts across Lab roles and bind the canonical role/host topology into a stable `serverInventoryDigest` carried by plan, preflight and run results. This prevents one machine from impersonating a multi-node tier and prevents evidence from being silently moved to another inventory.
- complete deterministic M01 source automation for a single management node: after fresh install and exact-SHA evidence collection, the runner records the pre-reboot boot ID, performs a real host reboot, requires a different boot ID, requires the installer service to return active/enabled, re-runs remote installer verification and re-collects exact-SHA field evidence through the same durable campaign identity. M01 automation is now `IMPLEMENTED`; M02 remains partial and M03 remains pending.
- add the canonical `examples/lab/lab-execution.example.json`, fix the README execution confirmation from the stale `RUN` value to the actual `RUN_LAB` contract, remove release-numbered paths from the example, and document distinct-host/inventory-digest evidence semantics.
- preserve Release Gate truth: M01 automation source coverage and any later physical M01 PASS are not by themselves Generated/Installed Runtime, Runtime-Realism or full Exact-SHA Physical Runtime certification for the product.

# 0.0.219 — AI-native operator experience program rebaseline

- Rebased the canonical roadmap to `PROGRAM_PHASE_MODEL_V3` while retaining eight large delivery phases and adding seven cross-cutting product tracks: Operator Experience/UI/UX, AI-Native, MCP/External Agents, Lab/Certification, Security/RBAC/Evidence, Supply Chain/Disconnected and Developer/Agent Experience.

# 0.0.218 — AI-native Operator Console design foundation

- adopt TailAdmin Community (MIT) as the external visual/layout reference after a CoreUI/AdminMart/TailAdmin fit audit, while retaining the embedded 4SO HTML/CSS/JavaScript runtime and all 4SO information architecture, authority and workflow semantics; no React/Next/MUI/Bootstrap/Tailwind/Alpine runtime migration is introduced solely for appearance.
- establish a 4SO-specific enterprise shell with high-contrast navigation, sticky header, global navigation-only `Ctrl/Cmd+K` command search, explicit persisted light/dark mode, denser operational cards/tables/action surfaces and responsive behavior. Template demo business domains and decorative analytics are not copied.
- close a real Operator Console regression hidden by previous coverage: the AI page used a missing `fillSelect()` helper and could stop rendering durable run history/usage/MCP state after successful API responses; the AI/Lab surfaces are now part of the headless release navigation contract.
- close 320px Lab horizontal overflow caused by long authority identifiers and harden mobile identifier wrapping without making data tables silently non-scrollable.
- harden the UI Release Gate so each viewport owns an isolated Python/Playwright/Chromium lifecycle and dirty-form semantics no longer depend on a presentation-only page title being visible at every responsive breakpoint.
- record the design decision and licensing boundary in `docs/OPERATOR_CONSOLE_DESIGN_FOUNDATION.md`; TailAdmin PRO assets are excluded and direct copied Community source, if ever introduced, must retain the MIT notice.
- preserve the AI-native Phase-C and four-layer Release Gate boundaries from 0.0.217; this UI/source closure is not Exact-SHA Physical Runtime evidence.

# 0.0.217 — AI-native control-plane foundation

- establish `UNIFIED_AI_RUNTIME_V1` as the single provider-neutral AI execution boundary for Operator diagnosis, Marketplace advisory and cloud/local Lab diagnosis. OpenAI Responses and OpenAI-compatible providers share one fail-closed configuration, timeout/input/output budget, mandatory pre-egress redaction and structured-output path; deterministic platform workflows remain available when AI is disabled.
- add durable project-scoped `ai_runs` authority with rolling-safe migration `0053`. Successful advisory calls persist provider/model, prompt/context/request/output digests, redaction count and token usage without persisting raw prompts or credentials. Canonical JSON output digests are recomputed on write/read/restore so PostgreSQL `jsonb` formatting cannot create false drift and tampered output cannot be accepted as durable evidence.
- close duplicate-token spending on idempotent Marketplace/Operator retries: replay is resolved from durable AI-run/request authority before a second provider call is admitted. Linked operation/managed-cluster evidence is revalidated against the same project in Memory/File/PostgreSQL.
- add dedicated API-token capability scopes `mcp.read` and `ai.diagnose`. MCP remains stateless/read-only on protocol `2026-07-28`; project-scoped tools re-apply authoritative project access instead of trusting only the outer HTTP boundary. The lab guide/runtime MCP tool lists are contract-tested to prevent UI/external-agent capability drift.
- upgrade the Operator Console AI Control Plane from a thin diagnosis form to an operational surface backed only by live authority: effective provider/model/budgets, explicit advisory/PASS boundaries, aggregate token/cache/redaction usage, MCP endpoint/protocol/scopes/tools, project-filtered durable run history and inspectable structured evidence. AI history is live-refreshed and provider health is never inferred merely from configuration.
- keep Lab AI failure-only and low-token: the deterministic Lab runner owns preflight/install/matrix/evidence/PASS, while AI receives bounded redacted failure packets and can only diagnose or propose checks. Codex/Claude/Antigravity remain optional repair/diagnosis workers rather than certification authorities.
- expand Phase C to `C-ai-native-control-plane-lab-mcp-foundation` while preserving the eight large program phases. Remaining Phase-C blockers are real server-driven M00-M03 execution, immutable automatic open-source acquisition, external provider/PostgreSQL durability/MCP interoperability runtime certification and a physical failure-to-AI-diagnosis run; no source/local test is promoted to Exact-SHA Physical PASS.

# 0.0.216 — Lab certification, AI diagnosis and MCP foundation

- rebaseline `PROGRAM_PHASE_MODEL_V2` into eight large delivery phases instead of many narrow increments: A) architecture rebaseline, B) target capability-contract foundation, C) Lab Certification + AI + MCP Foundation, D) OKD import runtime certification, E) OKD capability/profile/catalog integration, F) imported Day-2 + managed Compact-3, G) disconnected + upgrade/recovery, and H) full Exact-SHA physical certification with chaos/soak/multi-cluster evidence. Phase C is current and remains `blocked` until its physical/runtime exit criteria are evidenced.
- add `LAB_CERTIFICATION_MATRIX_V1` as the machine-readable lab authority with four server tiers and M00-M13 certification rows. Each row declares phase ownership, automation status (`IMPLEMENTED`, `PARTIAL`, `PENDING`, or `PENDING_PHASE`), destructive scope, actions and AI eligibility so roadmap presence is never represented as executable coverage.
- add `scripts/lab_runner.py` and `schemas/lab-execution.schema.json` as the deterministic server-driven lab foundation. The runner validates the exact release artifact/server inventory, performs fail-closed SSH/resource preflight and plans product-owned installation without allowing an AI agent to own PASS/FAIL or the happy path. Missing immutable dependency authority remains `BLOCKED`; the runner does not fetch arbitrary `latest` upstream software.
- add a failure-only bounded AI diagnosis contract with OpenAI-compatible provider support and optional Codex/Claude/Antigravity worker identities. AI receives normalized bounded failure packets only and cannot declare deterministic, runtime-certification or Physical PASS.
- add read-only MCP exposure through `POST /mcp` plus `GET /api/v1/lab/guide`, using the modern 2026-07-28 protocol shape for external clients. MCP currently exposes lab-guide and target-architecture authorities only; no mutating MCP tool is admitted.
- add the Operator Console Lab & Certification view backed directly by the same lab authority, including required server counts/roles, matrix actions, destructive flags, automation truth and AI/MCP policy. No separate hand-maintained UI roadmap is introduced.
- preserve Release Gate truth: source/local closure of the new foundation is not server-driven lab certification, external MCP interoperability, AI runtime diagnosis proof or Exact-SHA Physical Runtime evidence.

# 0.0.215 — Cluster inventory observation epoch authority closure

- close a Phase-C mutation-authority defect where inventory freshness was derived only from Hub receipt time: an authenticated Agent could submit an old observation now, refresh `LastSeenAt` / `InventoryUpdatedAt`, and make stale target capability/identity evidence appear fresh enough for a new task claim.
- persist a distinct `inventoryObservedAt` authority epoch across Memory/File/PostgreSQL, reject zero, stale, excessively future-skewed and backwards observation timestamps at the Store boundary, and require both fresh Hub receipt and fresh target observation for new mutation task claims. Equal observation timestamps remain idempotently reportable but cannot extend authority beyond the original target-observation age.
- add rolling-safe migration `0052` as a nullable/default-free observation epoch. Existing rows intentionally remain NULL and fail closed for new mutation task claims until a fresh inventory report arrives; migration `0050` and `0051` remain byte-for-byte unchanged.
- preserve leased-task completion semantics: claim-time freshness gates new work, while an already-fenced/leased task is still judged by its unchanged authority epoch plus lease/fence so a legitimate long-running task is not invalidated merely because the three-minute claim window expires during execution.
- add Memory/PostgreSQL negative controls for stale/future/rollback observation epochs and update deterministic fixtures so stale-health behavior is created by time advancing after a valid observation rather than by accepting stale evidence at ingestion. Phase C remains blocked on `OKD_IMPORT_RUNTIME_CERTIFICATION_PENDING`; this source/generated-runtime closure is not Exact-SHA Physical Runtime proof.

# 0.0.214 — Cluster import PostgreSQL expiry authority closure

- close a production-backend parity defect where PostgreSQL left expired `PENDING_APPROVAL` / `APPROVED` cluster-import rows logically active: reads could report stale enrollment state and the partial unique name index could prevent a replacement import even after the credential expiry time had passed.
- make PostgreSQL reject already-expired enrollment creation, render effective expiry on `Get/List`, reject revoke after logical expiry, and atomically materialize an expired same-name generation as `EXPIRED` with credential invalidation plus `cluster_import.expired` audit/outbox evidence before creating its replacement.
- preserve Memory/File semantics and Phase C truth: this is a runtime correctness closure for the PostgreSQL authority backend; `OKD_IMPORT_RUNTIME_CERTIFICATION_PENDING` remains open and no Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS is inferred.
- harden the headless UI release gate by restarting Chromium between the Product Console and Installer viewport matrices; this preserves all 10 viewport checks while preventing cumulative renderer/process stalls from turning an otherwise passing gate into an unbounded timeout.

# 0.0.213 — Target RBAC upgrade read-only parity closure

- preserve migration `0050` byte-for-byte and add rolling-safe migration `0051` instead of rewriting applied history; this closes a PostgreSQL/FileStore upgrade-state parity gap where `0050` removed unsafe legacy successor mutation authority but PostgreSQL did not expose the same explicit `target-read-only-admission` capability truth as FileStore.
- scope the parity repair to already-inventoried successor generations that have neither active nor issued mutation RBAC and that predate (or still await) their revoked predecessor acknowledgement, so a genuinely new same-UID re-enrollment created after acknowledgement is not prematurely marked admitted before its first authoritative inventory.
- add migration-contract regression coverage for the narrow successor guards and explicit rolling-safe compatibility classification; Phase C remains blocked on `OKD_IMPORT_RUNTIME_CERTIFICATION_PENDING`, and this source/upgrade repair is not Exact-SHA Physical Runtime proof.

# 0.0.212 — Target RBAC revocation acknowledgement/history upgrade closure

- enforce generation ordering for physical-cluster re-enrollment: after Hub revocation, a new ClusterImport claiming the same `kube-system` UID is rejected until the revoked predecessor's target-side RBAC fence is acknowledged. The acknowledgement must carry the exact canonical `targetRBACRevocationFenceDigest`, is revisioned/audited/outboxed across Memory/File/PostgreSQL, and is explicitly operator acknowledgement rather than Physical Runtime proof.
- add migration `0050` as `QUIESCED_REQUIRED`. It backfills sticky `target-mutation-rbac-ever-issued` history from immutable `cluster.mutation_rbac_activation.authorized` audit events and repairs legacy same-UID successors that earlier versions allowed before predecessor fencing by removing current mutation issuance/active authority and forcing them read-only until acknowledgement. FileStore snapshot restore performs the equivalent audit backfill and legacy successor repair.
- close revoke-after-drift history loss: server-owned `target-mutation-rbac-ever-issued` survives current authority invalidation, so a revoke still emits the full target-side mutation RBAC fence whenever activation was ever issued/proven. Issued-but-not-yet-proven activation is conservatively treated as potentially applied.
- keep mutation-RBAC authorization basis scoped to stable target identity/distribution authority. Unrelated OLM/API/CRD/OpenAPI discovery churn changes the full authenticated inventory digest used by planning/task safety but does not revoke Kubernetes RBAC; Kubernetes/distribution/identity authority drift still fails closed.
- extend Fleet generated-runtime coverage to `revoke -> blocked same-UID re-enrollment -> exact-digest acknowledgement -> re-enrollment with a new import-scoped principal`. Phase C remains blocked on `OKD_IMPORT_RUNTIME_CERTIFICATION_PENDING`; acknowledgement is not target-side apply evidence and does not constitute Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS.

# 0.0.211 — Mutation RBAC discovery-stability and revocation-history closure

- remove unrelated API/CRD/OpenAPI discovery catalog churn from the mutation-RBAC authorization basis while retaining it in the full inventory digest used by planning and task compatibility. This prevents ordinary OLM/operator installation from needlessly revoking platform mutation RBAC.
- add sticky server-owned `target-mutation-rbac-ever-issued` history so later authority drift cannot erase evidence that mutation RoleBindings may remain on the target. Revocation uses this durable history to fence all target-side mutation bindings even when current active/issued authority has already been invalidated.
- make full target-side revocation fencing idempotent for issued-but-never-proven activation by ensuring managed namespaces before empty-subject RoleBindings, while read-only targets that never received mutation issuance retain the minimal two-binding fence.
- keep Phase C blocked on real OKD runtime certification; no source/generated-runtime result is promoted to Physical PASS.

# 0.0.210 — Mutation RBAC safe-HTTP and stable-authority-basis closure

- make mutation RBAC issuance an explicit state-changing API operation. `POST /api/v1/clusters/{id}/mutation-rbac-manifest` now issues/re-issues digest-bound authorization; `GET` is read-only and returns only an already-current issuance. A cache, prefetch, health probe or ordinary manifest lookup can no longer create authorization state, audit entries or outbox events as a GET side effect.
- narrow `mutationRbacBasisDigest` from the entire non-mutation inventory to stable authorization facts: distribution/evidence, Kubernetes version, canonical API/CRD surface, schema authority, physical-identity continuity and enrollment-principal isolation. Volatile node readiness, capacity, certificate lifetimes, add-on health, storage inventory, networking telemetry and observability capabilities no longer force RBAC re-issuance during an incident.
- retain fail-closed behavior for real authority drift: Kubernetes/distribution/API/CRD/schema or identity/principal changes still invalidate `mutationRbacIssuedForDigest` and `target-mutation-rbac-active` until an explicit POST re-issues activation for the new basis. API/CRD lists are canonicalized before hashing so harmless discovery ordering does not churn authorization.
- keep task-level safety separate from RBAC authority. Full authenticated inventory digests continue to bind plans/tasks/revalidation, so telemetry or add-on drift can force plan revalidation without unnecessarily revoking the stable Kubernetes authorization principal.
- make cluster-detail truth explicit with `mutationRBACActivationIssued` and `mutationRBACProofPending` in addition to `mutationRBACActivationRequired`/`mutationEnabled`, so an issued-but-not-yet-proven activation is not represented as an unexplained disabled state.
- add source and generated-runtime regressions proving GET-before-issuance is side-effect free, POST issuance is idempotently retrievable, telemetry churn preserves current RBAC authority, task planning still revalidates on full inventory drift, and Kubernetes authority drift invalidates old issuance.
- keep Phase C blocked only on `OKD_IMPORT_RUNTIME_CERTIFICATION_PENDING`; these source/generated-runtime corrections do not constitute real OKD target application or Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS.

# 0.0.209 — Mutation RBAC inventory-basis authority closure

- bind server-owned mutation RBAC activation to the exact non-mutation semantic inventory basis instead of retaining a boolean issuance marker across later topology/capability drift. `ManagedCluster` now persists both the current `mutationRbacBasisDigest` and `mutationRbacIssuedForDigest`; task admission requires a current issued marker whose issued-for digest exactly matches the current basis.
- compute the mutation basis from a normalized read-only inventory so applying mutation RBAC does not invalidate its own issuance, while any non-mutation semantic change such as Kubernetes/distribution/topology/capability drift changes the basis, strips `target-mutation-rbac-active` and server issuance, and returns the target to read-only until an explicit new activation is issued.
- add migration `0049` as a rolling-safe additive/defaulted authority for the two mutation-RBAC digest fields. Upgrades from pre-0049 state fail closed: a legacy `target-mutation-rbac-active` capability without digest-bound issuance cannot claim new tasks until a fresh inventory establishes the basis and the control plane re-issues activation.
- make the activation API render `issuedFromInventoryDigest` from the persisted issued-for basis rather than the post-activation inventory digest, and expose the current basis/issued-for digests in the response for operator evidence. Revocation fences reuse the issued-for digest when present.
- add Agent defense-in-depth: before any representative `SelfSubjectAccessReview`, the mutation activation ConfigMap must match the exact basis digest computed from the inventory the Agent is about to report, in addition to the existing managed-cluster ID, physical `kube-system` UID and revoked-marker checks.
- add MemoryStore, PostgreSQL and Agent negative controls proving semantic inventory drift invalidates old mutation issuance, re-issuance binds to the new basis, stale activation ConfigMaps fail before permission proof, and pre-0049 active state is read-only after upgrade.
- keep Phase C blocked only on `OKD_IMPORT_RUNTIME_CERTIFICATION_PENDING`; this closes a source/generated-runtime authorization replay gap but is not real OKD import/reconnect/revocation evidence and does not constitute Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS.

# 0.0.208 — Target RBAC revocation fence and OKD restricted-runtime closure

- distinguish immediate Hub credential revocation from target-local Kubernetes authorization. ManagedCluster revoke now truthfully reports `hubAgentCredentialRevoked=true` and `targetRBACRevocationStatus=APPLY_REQUIRED` instead of implying that target RBAC disappears when the Hub credential is invalidated.
- return an idempotent target RBAC revocation fence with the revoke response and expose it again at `GET /api/v1/clusters/{id}/revocation-rbac-manifest` for revoked clusters. The fence marks the mutation activation proof revoked and clears all subjects from the enrollment read-only/credential bindings; mutation bindings are cleared only when the last authoritative inventory proved mutation RBAC active, so a never-activated read-only target does not fail revocation on namespaces that never existed.
- make `platform-agent` reject a mutation activation ConfigMap carrying `revoked: "true"` before UID or SelfSubjectAccessReview permission proof. Re-enrollment still uses a new import-scoped ServiceAccount generation; applying the new enrollment manifest re-binds only read-only access until a fresh mutation activation is admitted.
- make mutation RBAC issuance a server-owned authority instead of trusting an Agent capability claim. `target-mutation-rbac-activation-issued` is stripped from Agent inventory and can only be added by `AuthorizeClusterMutationRBACActivation`; `target-mutation-rbac-active` is ignored until that issuance exists, so a compromised/stale Agent cannot self-authorize mutation by reporting an active capability before the control plane has issued the activation manifest. Memory/File/PostgreSQL stores enforce the same two-stage invariant and smoke harnesses now exercise the real read-only → issuance → permission-proof sequence.
- make the fleet-agent Deployment explicit about a restricted runtime without pinning OKD-assigned UID/GID: pod `runAsNonRoot`, `RuntimeDefault` seccomp, container `allowPrivilegeEscalation: false`, read-only root filesystem and drop `ALL` capabilities. Writable credential state remains isolated to the existing `emptyDir`.
- add owner regressions for six-binding mutation-active revocation fencing, two-binding read-only fencing, missing inventory-digest normalization, revoked activation rejection before any permission proof, exact revoke-response truth, and post-revoke fence retrieval.
- make repository validation deterministic across developer workspaces and packaged artifacts by excluding `.pytest_cache` from the source-file inventory, matching the release builder exclusion set instead of allowing four local pytest cache files to inflate the validation count.
- keep Phase C blocked only on `OKD_IMPORT_RUNTIME_CERTIFICATION_PENDING`; this source/generated-runtime closure is not target-side apply evidence and does not constitute Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS.

# 0.0.207 — Cluster re-enrollment principal isolation closure

- close physical-cluster re-enrollment parity across Memory/File/PostgreSQL. A `kube-system` UID may have only one non-revoked ManagedCluster authority at a time, while a fully revoked historical registration no longer permanently blocks an explicit re-enrollment of that same physical cluster. Memory snapshot restore independently rejects multiple active authorities for one physical UID.
- replace the global PostgreSQL `external_uid` uniqueness constraint with an active-authority partial unique index in migration `0047`. This migration is deliberately `quiesced-required`: old writers still use the legacy fixed agent ServiceAccount and must be stopped before post-revocation re-enrollment becomes possible.
- close a Kubernetes-authorization gap in revoke -> re-enroll. New ClusterImports persist a deterministic import-scoped agent ServiceAccount and enrollment/activation manifests bind read-only and mutation RBAC to that generation-specific principal, so a new enrollment cannot inherit mutation RoleBindings from a revoked generation. Existing imports keep the empty principal authority and therefore preserve the legacy `4so-platform-agent` ServiceAccount for rolling compatibility.
- make mutation activation consume the persisted ClusterImport principal authority rather than inferring it from an Agent capability. Modern isolated imports must both match the canonical import-scoped principal and attest `target-enrollment-principal-isolated`; legacy imports remain on the fixed principal until an explicit new enrollment. Agent inventory reports isolation only when the running ServiceAccount matches the principal derived from its import ID.
- add migration `0048` as rolling-safe additive authority for `cluster_imports.agent_service_account`, plus owner regressions proving concurrent duplicate physical authority is rejected, revoked history permits re-enrollment, the new enrollment receives a distinct principal, invalid PostgreSQL physical identity is rejected before mutation, and legacy/historical state remains readable.
- keep Phase C blocked only on `OKD_IMPORT_RUNTIME_CERTIFICATION_PENDING`. These source/database/RBAC corrections do not constitute real OKD import/reconnect/revocation evidence or Exact-SHA Physical Runtime certification.

# 0.0.206 — Cluster identity store authority closure

- move imported-cluster physical identity continuity into the authoritative Store boundary instead of relying on the HTTP handler as the only enforcement point. `UpsertClusterInventory` and `HeartbeatCluster` now receive the observed `kube-system` UID and Memory/File/PostgreSQL stores independently compare it with the UID bound at import claim.
- keep mixed-version behavior fail-closed at the owner layer: an inventory without `externalUid` remains observable but is normalized to read-only and cannot retain `target-cluster-uid-attested` or mutation RBAC authority; an identity-unattested heartbeat is authenticated but does not refresh connection liveness. A non-empty mismatched UID is rejected before inventory, digest, heartbeat or connection state can change.
- preserve the API-level conflict response as defense-in-depth while eliminating the possibility that a future in-process Store consumer bypasses the identity invariant. Add MemoryStore and scripted PostgreSQL owner regressions proving mismatch rejection and no liveness refresh for legacy heartbeats.
- keep Phase C truth unchanged: OKD is still preview/read-only and `OKD_IMPORT_RUNTIME_CERTIFICATION_PENDING` remains the sole phase blocker. No real OKD import/reconnect/revocation or Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS is inferred from this source closure.

# 0.0.205 — OKD/OpenShift identity continuity and activation binding closure

- close a distribution-authority correctness gap where any real `config.openshift.io/v1` `ClusterVersion/version` object was previously classified as `okd`. The Agent now distinguishes OKD release versions from Red Hat OpenShift/OCP, the target vocabulary keeps `openshift` as a separate recognized-but-not-admitted identity, and the control plane rejects OKD evidence whose release version is not actually an OKD build.
- add an independent server-side contradiction check: a native `ClusterVersion` API surface can no longer be persisted under `kubernetes` or `rke2`. This prevents a stale/buggy classifier from bypassing the preview/read-only fence by claiming a supported distribution while OpenShift authority is visible in inventory.
- bind imported-cluster continuity to the physical Kubernetes cluster identity. The Agent re-attests the `kube-system` UID on every inventory and fallback heartbeat; a non-empty mismatch with the UID captured at import claim is rejected before state/liveness can be refreshed. Pre-0.0.205 Agents that omit the UID remain observable but their inventory is server-normalized to read-only.
- make `target-cluster-uid-attested` a server-owned inventory authority marker. Agent-supplied copies of that capability are removed and reconstructed only after the control plane verifies physical identity continuity; mutation RBAC activation is unavailable until this marker exists.
- close cross-cluster RBAC activation leakage. The mutation activation manifest now carries a dedicated `4so-platform-mutation-activation` ConfigMap bound to both the managed-cluster ID and claimed external UID; the Agent verifies that binding against its live `kube-system` UID before running any `SelfSubjectAccessReview`. Applying cluster A's activation manifest to cluster B therefore cannot activate B's mutation capability.
- keep activation inventory references truthful as issuance provenance (`issuedFromInventoryDigest`) rather than pretending the RBAC grant is cryptographically tied to all future inventory digests. Existing operation/task planning still fences execution on the current authoritative inventory digest.
- bound **new task claims** to a server-timestamped three-minute inventory-authority freshness window while keeping result acceptance on the already-issued lease/fence and unchanged authority epoch. A disconnected target can no longer retain mutation claim authority indefinitely, but a legitimate long-running task is not invalidated solely because the inventory freshness window elapsed after it was leased.
- restore Memory/PostgreSQL claim parity for baseline work: `NextBaselineTask` now consumes the common fresh-cluster admission helper, and a recursive helper-call defect introduced during the freshness split is removed. Move the generic PostgreSQL Agent admission validators out of the baseline owner into the fleet/cluster authority owner and rename them `validateClusterAgentTx` / `validateFreshClusterAgentTx`, removing the misleading cross-subsystem baseline coupling that enabled the defect. A scripted PostgreSQL regression proves stale inventory is rejected before task selection, while owner tests prove a still-valid leased result remains reportable after claim freshness expires.
- add negative controls for OCP-vs-OKD classification, native-ClusterVersion/distribution contradiction, non-OKD release versions, legacy read-only inventory, inventory/heartbeat identity mismatch, and cross-cluster activation manifests. Phase C remains blocked only on `OKD_IMPORT_RUNTIME_CERTIFICATION_PENDING`; no runtime or Physical PASS is inferred.

# 0.0.204 — OKD import authority and read-only admission source closure

- close the source-level OKD distribution-identity gap without promoting OKD to a mutation-capable target: the Agent now reads the real `config.openshift.io/v1` `ClusterVersion/version` singleton, binds UID/version evidence, requires complete API/CRD discovery, and rejects a same-name CRD as ambiguous authority; the control plane independently validates that evidence before persisting canonical `okd`.
- stop trusting Agent-supplied inventory digests. Inventory identity evidence is included in the canonical digest and both memory/PostgreSQL stores recompute it before binding the managed cluster, preventing a caller-selected digest from becoming target authority.
- make imported-cluster mutation authorization explicitly two-stage. Enrollment now installs only read-only inventory/credential RBAC; a separate inventory-bound mutation activation manifest is available only to supported distributions. The Agent proves the representative mutation permissions with `SelfSubjectAccessReview` and reports `target-mutation-rbac-active` before any task queue can run.
- enforce the mutation fence on both task claim and result acceptance across baseline, tenant, provider, runtime verification/certification, drift/upgrade and cluster-maintenance paths, including PostgreSQL production semantics. Preview/unsupported targets have mutation claims stripped and receive `target-read-only-admission`; OKD therefore remains read-only even if an Agent attempts to advertise mutation capabilities.
- add a server-owned `inventory_updated_at` authority epoch. A successful inventory report synchronizes inventory and heartbeat authority; a later heartbeat without a fresh inventory breaks mutation admission, so liveness alone cannot preserve stale target identity/RBAC authority. Long-running already-leased work is not invalidated by an arbitrary wall-clock TTL.
- add migration `0046` as rolling-safe additive state for distribution evidence and inventory authority, plus negative controls for OKD evidence incompleteness/CRD spoofing, forged digest rejection, read-only capability stripping, staged RBAC activation and heartbeat-only epoch breakage.
- update `PROGRAM_PHASE_MODEL_V2` truthfully: Phase C remains `blocked`, but its source blockers for authoritative detection and read-only fencing are closed. The only remaining Phase C blocker is `OKD_IMPORT_RUNTIME_CERTIFICATION_PENDING`; no Generated/Installed Runtime, Runtime-Realism, Exact-SHA Physical Runtime or OKD mutation PASS is inferred.

# 0.0.203 — OKD goal phase dependency rebaseline

- rebase the product roadmap after a deep goal-to-code audit instead of advancing implementation on a misleading linear sequence. `PROGRAM_PHASE_MODEL_V2` separates authoritative OKD import detection/read-only admission, inventory-bound capability/health ownership, and desired+observed target-profile/catalog compilation.
- expose the real current blockers in Phase C: the Agent still has no authoritative OpenShift/OKD API detector, imported preview/unsupported targets are not yet fenced from generic mutation claims, and the OKD import path has no runtime certification evidence. No OKD admission or Physical PASS is invented.
- remove the false dependency that made imported OKD Day-2 wait for managed bare-metal installation. Imported Day-2 now branches from observed capability ownership and may proceed in parallel with managed Compact-3 work.
- prevent a pre-install circular dependency by requiring the target-profile compiler to support both desired state before a cluster exists and observed state after import/install. Managed Compact-3 consumes that compiler; it does not require inventory from a cluster that has not been created.
- make managed Compact-3 exit criteria explicit around pinned `openshift-install`/FCOS/Agent ISO inputs and one real `BootMediaProvider`; make disconnected delivery explicitly `oc-mirror` v2 + zot; make upgrades consume CVO-supported update edges and ClusterVersion/ClusterOperator evidence without exposing synthetic rollback.
- add phase-local exit criteria, explicit dependency/parallel metadata, and keep Exact-SHA physical certification as the final independent not-evaluated phase. The Operator Console now labels the roadmap item as the current product phase rather than the next phase.

# 0.0.202 — program phase authority and target capability foundation closure

- correct the product roadmap around the target-architecture goal instead of treating the current 18-component generic blueprint as the universal prerequisite for every future target. `PROGRAM_PHASE_MODEL_V1` now makes the canonical order explicit: architecture rebaseline -> target/capability foundation -> first-class OKD import -> OKD-aware catalog/profile -> managed Compact-3 -> Day-2 -> disconnected -> CVO upgrade -> Exact-SHA physical certification. No percentage or physical success is inferred from source implementation.
- add `TARGET_CAPABILITY_RESOLVER_V1` as the source-level resolver boundary. Kubernetes and RKE2 remain admitted; OKD stays preview-only/fail-closed. The OKD preview suppresses default duplicate Cilium, Capsule and VictoriaMetrics selections, makes Kyverno/MetalLB/snapshot-controller/Tetragon conditional on target capabilities/dependencies, and never converts that preview into OKD admission.
- expose the resolver descriptor and canonical program roadmap through the existing target-architecture API and embed the same roadmap in `platformctl release-readiness`, separating product-program sequencing from the existing selected-blueprint source/runtime/deployment gate counts.
- make the Provider Operator Console consume the target-architecture authority so admitted/preview distributions, resolver authority and the next product phase are live API data instead of hard-coded UI prose. This closes the source-semantics foundation phase while keeping `C-okd-import-first-class` as the next blocked objective.
- preserve the existing 76 selected-blueprint blockers and `Exact-SHA Physical Runtime=not-evaluated`; source acquisition/runtime certification are still truthful deployment gates, but they are no longer presented as the architectural phase ordering for first-class OKD support.

# 0.0.201 — disaster recovery preflight archive admission closure

- close a destructive DR admission gap left after 0.0.200: restore preflight proved object existence and SHA-256 before workload quiesce, but structural archive safety was only checked later inside each restore Job. A checksum-valid unsafe archive could therefore pass preflight, quiesce all platform workloads, then fail before extraction and unnecessarily force the appliance into recovery-required state.
- make the pre-quiesce restore preflight run the same canonical tar safety contract over every downloaded PostgreSQL, platform-secrets, agent-PKI, Forgejo and zot payload after digest verification and before deleting its staged copy.
- preserve in-restore archive verification as defense-in-depth; no payload is trusted solely because preflight passed, and no source/runtime certification or Exact-SHA Physical Runtime evidence is promoted.
- add owner regression assertions proving preflight orders digest verification -> archive structural admission -> cleanup for the component loop.

# 0.0.200 — lifecycle restore archive command safety closure

- close a real restore-command inversion in the archive safety gate: the previous `! tar -tvzf | awk ...` pipeline inverted the intended result, so safe archives could be rejected while archives containing rejected link types could continue into extraction.
- make archive admission positively require GNU tar member types to be regular files or directories only; reject symlinks, hard links, FIFOs, block/character devices and other special entries before extraction.
- reject setuid/setgid regular-file modes before extraction so a crafted but checksum-valid backup cannot introduce privileged executables into restored application data.
- add executable owner regressions using real tar archives for safe regular/directory content, FIFO entries and setuid files, plus preserve the existing path-traversal and restore-order checks.
- keep checksum/authenticity, restore staging, lifecycle fencing, source acquisition and Exact-SHA Physical Runtime semantics unchanged; no certification or physical evidence is synthesized.

# 0.0.199 — lifecycle restore archive safety closure

- Hardened disaster-recovery restore archive admission against absolute/parent-traversal members and symlink/hardlink entries before extraction.
- Applied the same archive safety boundary to PostgreSQL, platform-secrets, agent-PKI, Forgejo, and zot restore paths.
- Restore remains fail-closed before destructive extraction/mutation when archive structure is unsafe.

## 0.0.198 — Lifecycle restore Job payload staging integrity closure

- close a destructive restore time-of-check/time-of-use gap left after 0.0.197: lifecycle restore admission verified backup metadata and payload before quiesce and again after quiesce, but the Kubernetes restore Job later reopened the mutable hostPath directly for `pg_restore` and PVC extraction without re-verifying the bytes it was about to consume. A payload changed after host-side validation could therefore become the destructive restore input.
- add an in-Job `verify-backup` init container that re-checks every metadata-authorized payload for regular/non-symlink shape, exact byte size and SHA-256 on the execution node, copies the verified bytes into a pod-local `emptyDir`, and verifies the staged copy again before any database or filesystem mutation starts.
- make PostgreSQL restore and PVC extraction consume only the verified pod-local staging volume (`/verified/database.dump` and `/verified/data.tar.gz`); the mutable backup hostPath is mounted read-only only into the verification init container. This preserves retry/recovery semantics while removing the hostPath TOCTOU boundary from destructive mutation.
- add owner regression coverage proving Forgejo restore manifests contain the verification/staging contract and that both database and data restore paths consume only staged bytes. Exact-SHA Physical Runtime remains an independent gate.

## 0.0.197 — Lifecycle backup restore admission integrity closure

- close a destructive-restore admission gap where lifecycle backup metadata with zero payload files could pass `validateBackupForRestore`, allowing a restore to quiesce a workload before the fixed restore Job discovered that the required payload was absent and forced the operation into recovery-required state.
- make backup payload authority service-specific and exact before destructive mutation: Forgejo requires `data.tar.gz` + `database.dump`, Keycloak requires `database.dump` + `marker`, and zot requires `data.tar.gz`; metadata must contain exactly that set with unique canonical names, valid SHA-256 digests, positive sizes and byte-for-byte size/digest agreement.
- reject symlink/special-file backup payloads and symlinked metadata/directories, bind opened files to the pre-open inode, and reject path-escape/extra entries so metadata cannot make restore admission hash or trust bytes outside the product-owned backup directory.
- make lifecycle simulation generate the same payload filenames as the real backup Jobs instead of the former `database.sql`/generic data shape, so restore/recovery tests exercise the production payload contract rather than a parallel simulated format. Add owner regressions for empty metadata, path escape, matching-digest symlink payload and size-authority mismatch. Exact-SHA Physical Runtime remains a separate gate.

## 0.0.196 — Planner immutable Git revision authority closure

- close a planner-authority bypass where `revisionType=commit` was treated as sufficient proof of immutability even when `spec.delivery.revision` was `main`, a tag-like value, malformed hex or a shortened object ID; `BuildWithCertificationAuthority` could therefore manufacture `execution-ready` when called with already-authorized catalog evidence outside the normal blueprint validator path.
- make the deployment planner independently require a full immutable Git object ID using the same 40-character SHA-1 / 64-character SHA-256 contract as blueprint validation and Git delivery; caller validation is now defense-in-depth rather than a prerequisite for planner safety.
- add owner regressions proving invalid commit-typed revisions remain planning-only even with certification authority, while full 64-character SHA-256 Git object IDs remain execution-eligible. No source lock, runtime certification or Exact-SHA Physical Runtime evidence is fabricated.

## 0.0.195 — OCI mirror transport and upload-location closure

- close an OCI mirror transport-floor gap below the authenticated/control-plane redirect closure: an HTTPS registry could return an absolute HTTP blob-upload `Location`, causing Platform Factory to transmit immutable image-layer bytes over plaintext even though the configured registry authority was TLS-protected. Registry redirects and upload locations now fail closed on HTTPS→HTTP downgrade while preserving OCI-compatible HTTPS offload/CDN locations.
- preserve opaque registry upload-session authority exactly: critical query parameters from the registry `Location` are no longer parsed, reordered and re-encoded before the final PUT. Platform Factory now leaves the existing raw query byte-for-byte intact and appends only the required `digest` parameter, rejecting a conflicting pre-existing digest instead of silently rewriting it.
- resolve relative upload locations against the final successful POST response URL after any permitted redirect, rather than the original upload-initiation URL, and reject registry base URLs decorated with embedded credentials, path, query or fragment so the configured registry authority has one unambiguous origin contract.
- add stable owner regressions reproducing plaintext upload downgrade, signed-query mutation and post-redirect relative-location misresolution. No catalog source, runtime certification or Exact-SHA Physical Runtime state is promoted by this release.

## 0.0.194 — Upstream transport authority and redirect closure

- close an immutable Helm acquisition integrity-anchor gap: an admitted HTTPS chart repository index could previously follow a redirect to HTTP (or another non-HTTPS scheme), allowing the upstream index that supplies the expected chart digest to cross a TLS downgrade boundary before the downloaded chart was verified against it. The acquisition owner now validates the effective response URL and fails closed on any non-HTTPS final transport while still allowing HTTPS CDN redirects.
- close the equivalent authenticated control-plane redirect boundary in `platformctl`: evidence downloads, installer field campaigns, diagnostics, token/access operations and zero-to-HA flows all share the canonical HTTP client, which now refuses redirects instead of allowing endpoint authority to change after an HTTPS/loopback base URL was validated. This prevents Bearer/bootstrap credentials from being forwarded through redirect chains and makes the configured endpoint the only network authority.
- add stable owner regressions for HTTPS index downgrade denial and authenticated redirect refusal before credential forwarding; preserve all existing upstream admission, source acquisition, runtime certification and Exact-SHA Physical Runtime truthfulness without promoting any component or fabricating evidence.

## 0.0.193 — Release readiness upstream authority parity closure

- close a release-phase authority drift where the Go `release-readiness` path trusted a much weaker interpretation of embedded `upstream-admission.json` than the product-owned acquisition validator; a stale admission row could therefore report the upstream-admission phase as complete even when its constraint/version no longer matched the live Catalog.
- add one Go authority validator shared by ReleaseReadiness that fail-closes on policy drift, unresolved-Helm coverage mismatch, unsupported admission status, chart/source mismatch, missing rationale, invalid selected/upstream version relationships, review-constraint drift, ready-candidate Catalog pin drift and ready-candidate version-policy drift.
- preserve phase truthfulness: the enterprise baseline remains 16 admission rows with 9 ready / 7 review-required and the existing immutable-source/runtime/physical blockers remain unchanged; this release does not synthesize acquisition, certification or Exact-SHA Physical Runtime evidence.
- add stable owner regressions proving the embedded Catalog and embedded upstream admission authority remain mutually valid and that stale constraint or ready-pin drift is rejected before a ReleaseReadiness report is emitted.

## 0.0.192 — Release phase authority and Autopilot convergence closure

- add `platformctl release-readiness -f <blueprint>` as the canonical machine-readable release-phase authority. It derives product blockers, deployment-context blockers, upstream-admission state, immutable source-acquisition state, runtime-certification state and control-plane certification-authority reachability from the same live catalog/plan contracts instead of creating a parallel roadmap/state document.
- make unknown future plan blockers fail closed as product-release blockers, keep only immutable Git revision as the current post-publication deployment-context blocker, and make the Exact-SHA Physical Runtime phase explicitly `not-evaluated`; this command cannot manufacture or infer Physical PASS from source/build/runtime metadata.
- remove Autopilot's private release-blocker classifier and make release/real-test decisions consume the canonical CLI readiness authority, preventing planner/Autopilot phase drift while preserving the distinction between local code correctness, external supply-chain closure and physical execution.
- close a Planner authority-suppression edge case: when component source/runtime gates were fully satisfied but the selected Git revision was still mutable, the deployment-context blocker previously prevented `CERTIFICATION_AUTHORITY_UNVERIFIED` from being emitted. Certification authority is now evaluated once component readiness is complete, independently of the post-publication Git immutability gate.
- embed and strictly parse `catalog/upstream-admission.json` in the product catalog authority so phase reporting is bound to the same admitted upstream review rows as acquisition. Unresolved enabled components without an admission authority now fail closed in readiness evaluation.
- correct the Loki upstream review after the 2026 OSS chart ownership migration: the OSS chart now belongs to `grafana-community/helm-charts` with a changed chart-version line, while the shipped 7.3.x path is maintained for Grafana Enterprise Logs. Loki therefore remains architecture-review-required until the product-owned OSS delivery contract is deliberately rebaselined; no source-resolution or runtime-certification evidence is fabricated.
- close a release-gate liveness defect exposed by the UI smoke lifecycle: teardown through implicit Playwright context/finalization could leave gate completion ambiguous in this environment, while hard process exit could sever the driver pipe and emit EPIPE. Headless and live UI smoke now own Playwright start/stop explicitly, close browser/context first, persist evidence, and return the real exit code without orphaning the driver; live smoke also guarantees platform-api cleanup if driver shutdown fails.
- preserve truthful phase state for the enterprise baseline: current external acquisition/runtime-certification blockers remain blockers until real immutable acquisition and target-runtime evidence exist, and Exact-SHA Physical Runtime remains a separate gate.

## 0.0.191 — Catalog install durable crash residue closure

- close a crash-recovery liveness gap in repair of an already-created immutable catalog runtime bundle: a process death inside `durablefile.Replace` could leave its reserved `.durable-*` temp beside a missing payload, causing every retry to reject the otherwise recoverable runtime directory as containing an unexpected file.
- while holding the repository catalog-install transaction fence, remove only real regular product-owned `.durable-*` residue from the final runtime bundle and from the catalog/component authority directories; special-file or symlink lookalikes remain fail-closed, and directory synchronization makes cleanup durable before mutation continues.
- prevent the same crash residue from silently entering a release artifact: the canonical repository validator reports stale durable temps outside excluded runtime state, and the deterministic release builder independently refuses to package them even when invoked directly.
- add release-neutral owner regressions reproducing the 0.0.190 retry deadlock, catalog/admission metadata residue and hostile symlink cleanup case, plus a release-source boundary regression. Exact-SHA Physical Runtime remains a separate gate.

## 0.0.190 — Catalog install runtime file integrity closure

- close the remaining existing-runtime path-integrity gap in catalog install: an expected immutable runtime payload name such as `artifact.bin`, `provenance.json` or `source-lock.json` could be a symlink to a matching file outside the repository and 0.0.189 would follow it during idempotent replay, treating externally-owned bytes as a valid repository runtime bundle.
- require every already-present runtime bundle payload to be a real regular file before comparison/repair; expected symlinks and other special files now fail before the component contract or Helm admission authority can be mutated, while genuinely missing files in an otherwise matching partial bundle retain the existing crash-repair behavior.
- harden repository-owned regular-file reads with a no-follow/nonblocking final open plus post-open `fstat`, removing the final-path check/open substitution window for catalog component/admission reads and preventing a substituted FIFO from turning an install into an unbounded read.
- add a release-neutral owner regression that reproduces the 0.0.189 symlink acceptance and proves rejection occurs before catalog/admission mutation. Exact-SHA Physical Runtime remains a separate gate.

## 0.0.189 — Catalog install authority path and crash hygiene closure

- close a canonical-authority path-integrity bypass in Helm upstream admission: acquisition/validation/install no longer follow a symlinked `catalog/upstream-admission.json` (or symlinked catalog/components authority path) as though it were repository-owned truth; the canonical execution boundary must now be a real in-repository regular file beneath real repository directories before any tool/network use or catalog mutation.
- close catalog install filesystem escape surfaces by rejecting symlinked component contracts and symlinked runtime component/destination directories before immutable bundle writes, including non-Helm external tagged-source installs that do not traverse the Helm admission coverage path.
- close the remaining pre-rename crash hygiene gap in immutable runtime installation: stale product-owned `.<version>.partial-*` staging directories left by a dead process are removed under the repository install fence before retry, with the runtime parent synchronized before the transaction continues.
- add release-neutral owner regressions for symlinked canonical admission, symlinked external component authority, symlinked runtime parents and stale crash staging; retain strict global admission parity, repository-wide install serialization and 0.0.188 post-component/pre-retirement recovery semantics. Exact-SHA Physical Runtime remains a separate gate.

## 0.0.188 — Catalog install crash recovery closure

- close the post-component/pre-admission-retirement crash deadlock in Helm catalog import: 0.0.187 could durably commit the immutable runtime bundle and resolved component contract, crash before retiring the consumed admission row, then reject every retry because strict global coverage observed one stale authority row for a component that was no longer unresolved.
- add a narrowly-scoped resolved-import recovery check that accepts only the exact interrupted transaction shape: the current component must already equal the verified resolved bundle contract, the stale target row must still exactly match the admitted chart/source/version/revision, and removing only that row must make the remaining canonical authority strictly cover every unresolved Helm component. Any unrelated malformed/missing/extra authority state still fails before mutation.
- preserve repository-scoped install fencing, immutable runtime replay and global admission parity; a retry of the interrupted import now validates/repairs the runtime bundle, replays the identical component write and retires the stale row so the repository returns to canonical validity without manual authority editing.
- add a crash-state owner regression that fails on 0.0.187 with `authority=1 unresolved-helm=0` and proves idempotent recovery plus post-recovery strict admission validation on 0.0.188. Exact-SHA Physical Runtime remains a separate gate.

## 0.0.187 — Catalog install global admission parity closure

- close a validator/runtime trust-boundary gap in `catalog-bundle install`: 0.0.186 verified only the target Helm admission row, so a target-valid bundle could mutate runtime/component state even while another row in the shared canonical `catalog/upstream-admission.json` was malformed or the authority no longer exactly covered the unresolved Helm catalog.
