- make the Go install owner validate the complete canonical admission authority against every unresolved Helm component before any filesystem mutation, including exact coverage, component identity, status, release constraint, chart, HTTPS/OCI source shape, rationale, selected/upstream version parity, exact ready-component catalog pin and version-policy semantics.
- fail closed on malformed/symlinked component contracts while constructing the admission coverage authority, preventing install from proceeding against a repository state that the canonical release validator would reject.
- add a negative owner regression that reproduces 0.0.186 accepting a valid target row beside an invalid unrelated admission row and proves 0.0.187 rejects the authority before runtime or component mutation. Existing concurrency fencing, idempotent replay and admission retirement semantics remain unchanged; Exact-SHA Physical Runtime remains a separate gate.

## 0.0.186 — Catalog install concurrency transaction fencing closure

- close a repository-authority lost-update race in concurrent `catalog-bundle install` operations: two different Helm imports could both verify against the same `catalog/upstream-admission.json`, commit their independent runtime/component payloads, then race while retiring the shared authority document so the later atomic rename resurrected an already-consumed row and left the repository invalid.
- serialize the complete repository install transaction (`component/admission read → contract verification → immutable runtime commit → component commit → admission retirement`) with a repository-scoped advisory lock under excluded `.state/catalog-bundle-install.lock`; process crash releases the lock, existing crash/retry idempotency remains intact, and the lock never enters deterministic release artifacts.
- fail closed when the transaction state path is not a real directory or the lock path is not a regular file instead of following an unexpected symlink/special-file lock target.
- add an eight-way concurrent install regression that reproduces the stale-admission row on 0.0.185 and proves all independent imports commit while every consumed row is retired under the fenced transaction. No component source/certification state is promoted by this release; Exact-SHA Physical Runtime remains a separate gate.

## 0.0.185 — Catalog install admission transaction closure

- close the remaining Helm admission bypass below `acquire_upstream_helm.py`: `platformctl catalog-bundle install` now binds unresolved Helm imports to the canonical repository `catalog/upstream-admission.json` row and rejects review-required, source/version/revision/chart or policy mismatches before any runtime/component filesystem mutation.
- bind an imported bundle to the repository's existing unresolved component contract. Import may only perform the explicit source-resolution transition (`release` exact-pin, offline repository key, immutable source evidence and candidate certification reset); it can no longer substitute support tier, compatibility, dependencies, namespace, operations or other catalog policy while resolving the same component name.
- make successful Helm import retire its consumed admission row after runtime bundle and component durability are established, so the authority continues to cover exactly the remaining unresolved Helm set and post-install repository validation can PASS. The ordering is retry-safe: a crash before component commit keeps the row; a crash after component commit can idempotently retire the stale row; a completed import can be replayed after the row is already absent.
- add owner regressions for review-required direct bundle install, unresolved component-contract substitution, admission retirement and idempotent replay. No component is promoted in this release; the existing 9 ready / 7 review-required acquisition queue remains unchanged until real upstream artifacts are acquired. Exact-SHA Physical Runtime remains a separate gate.

## 0.0.184 — Fresh artifact deterministic Go gate closure

- close the remaining release-gate orchestration defect reproduced only from a freshly extracted exact artifact: both `go test -p=2 ./...` and `go test -p=1 ./...` can stall between packages even though every owner package passes in an isolated Go invocation.
- run Go unit, vet and race gates package-isolated in both the canonical Makefile and `verify_release.py --full`; the package set is still derived from the complete `go list ./...` authority and unit/race keep `-count=1`.
- preserve the 0.0.183 upstream-acquisition authority closure unchanged; no catalog component is promoted and no runtime certification state is widened.
- Exact-SHA Physical Runtime remains a separate gate and is never inferred from source, packaged-binary or smoke verification.

## 0.0.183 — Exact artifact upstream authority and release gate closure

- materialize the upstream-acquisition authority bypass closure in the actual release source: unresolved Helm acquisition now requires `--from-admission`, rejects non-canonical `--authority` files before Helm/Crane/network use, and treats version/source flags only as consistency assertions against the canonical `catalog/upstream-admission.json` row.
- add owner regressions proving both direct version/source acquisition and alternate-authority acquisition fail before external tool or network access.
- use bounded Go scheduler concurrency (`-p=2`) for unit/vet gates after reproducing that unrestricted cross-package execution can stall while fully isolated execution adds excessive process/link overhead; race remains package-isolated because the race build still reproduces cross-package stalls even at `-p=2`. The full `go list ./...` package set and fresh `-count=1` test execution are retained.
- this release changes no runtime catalog certification state and does not infer Exact-SHA Physical Runtime from source, generated-artifact or smoke gates.

## 0.0.182 — Release gate serialization and upstream authority closure

- preserve the 0.0.181 upstream-acquisition authority bypass closure and additionally close a release-gate orchestration defect reproduced from a freshly extracted exact release artifact: parallel cross-package `go test ./...` / `go test -race ./...` could stall under shared runtime/filesystem pressure even though every owner package passes independently.
- serialize the canonical source Go unit, vet and race package scheduler one package per process; unit/race execution uses `-count=1` so the gate exercises fresh test executions without reducing the package set or weakening assertions.
- make the fresh extracted-artifact verifier use the exact same one-package-per-process strategy for unit, vet and race phases; the exact artifact verifier therefore covers the full `go list ./...` set without cross-package resource contention becoming an unbounded false blocker.
- no product runtime behavior, catalog resolution status, certification state or component scope is widened. Exact-SHA Physical Runtime remains a separate gate and is not inferred from source/smoke success.

## 0.0.181 — Upstream acquisition authority bypass closure

- make the product-owned `CatalogUpstreamAdmission` a mandatory execution boundary for unresolved Helm acquisition: direct `--version/--source` acquisition without `--from-admission` now fails before Helm, Crane or network use instead of bypassing the authority introduced in 0.0.180.
- reject alternate `--authority` files during acquisition so a release engineer cannot point `--from-admission` at an arbitrary external JSON document that self-approves an untrusted chart source; acquisition is bound to the canonical repository authority at `catalog/upstream-admission.json`.
- retain `--version`, `--upstream-version` and `--source` only as optional consistency assertions against the canonical admitted row; mismatches continue to fail closed and review-required components remain non-executable.
- add owner regressions for both bypass paths and preserve the existing separation between exact version admission, immutable source acquisition and runtime certification. No component is promoted to source-resolved or runtime-certified by this closure.

## 0.0.180 — Catalog upstream admission and acquisition authority foundation

- begin PHASE 2 Catalog Execution Closure with one machine-readable `CatalogUpstreamAdmission` authority covering all 16 unresolved Helm components; it separates official-source/exact-version selection from immutable source acquisition and runtime certification instead of allowing ad-hoc release-engineer URL/version choices.
- exact-pin the nine components whose reviewed upstream candidate is currently admitted for acquisition (`alloy`, `argocd`, `capsule`, `cert-manager`, `external-dns`, `external-secrets`, `tetragon`, `velero`, `victoria-metrics`) while deliberately keeping `source.resolved=false`, source-lock/digest fields empty and runtime certification at `candidate`; this removes nine real `COMPONENT_VERSION_NOT_PINNED` blockers without fabricating supply-chain or runtime evidence.
- keep seven components fail-closed behind explicit review states rather than silently widening or modernizing the baseline: Ceph-CSI requires a product architecture decision because upstream recommends the Operator path for new deployments; Cilium and kgateway require Gateway API dependency alignment; Grafana, Loki, Kyverno and MetalLB still require an exact admitted patch selection.
- add `scripts/catalog_upstream_admission.py`, repository invariants and owner regressions that require exact coverage, official HTTPS/OCI sources, exact non-prerelease versions for ready rows, unchanged constraints for review rows, and strict separation between version admission, source resolution and certification evidence.
- extend `scripts/acquire_upstream_helm.py` with `--from-admission`: release engineering can acquire/install a ready component without repeating version/source arguments, while overrides and review-required components are rejected before Helm/Crane or network use. Add Make targets for authority validation/planning and an admission-aware acquisition preflight.
- preserve truthful planning semantics: the enterprise Blueprint remains `planning-only`; its canonical blocker count moves from 85 to 76 solely because nine versions are now exact-selected. The remaining unresolved-source/source-lock/runtime-certification evidence and immutable Git revision blockers remain authoritative.

## 0.0.179 — Target architecture model rebaseline foundation

- introduce `TARGET_ARCHITECTURE_MODEL_V1` as the product-owned boundary that separates Kubernetes distribution identity, provisioning mode, infrastructure provider and provisioning adapter instead of overloading one `distribution/provider` pair with unrelated meanings; the internal RKE2 management appliance remains an explicit management-plane boundary rather than a generic target abstraction.
- canonicalize distribution identities to `kubernetes` and `rke2` for currently admitted targets; legacy `generic-imported` and `kubespray` values remain accepted and normalize to `kubernetes`, while `okd` is only recognized and explicitly not admitted until the dedicated OKD import/capability-resolution phases exist.
- expose `GET /api/v1/target-architecture-model`, enrich imported-cluster API responses with an explicit target model, and expose canonical target metadata on Provider Profile/Provider Cluster surfaces; Cluster API is now represented as `provisioningMode=cluster-api` rather than a distribution or infrastructure identity.
- migrate the shipped component catalog and enterprise Blueprint to canonical distribution identities, add rolling-safe migration 45 to canonicalize mutable Provider Profile aliases/defaults, and preserve legacy immutable release compatibility through compatibility-engine normalization rather than history rewriting.
- update the Operator Console terminology so operators choose a Distribution identity separately from the Provisioning adapter; add release-neutral regressions for legacy normalization, the target-model API, provider target metadata and migration fencing.

## 0.0.178 — Agent task deterministic ordering parity closure

- close a backend-parity defect across six durable Agent/worker queues where Memory/FileStore selected equal-time candidates from Go map iteration while PostgreSQL used a stable ID tie-break: Baseline Deployment, Runtime Verification, Runtime Certification, Provider Profile verification, Provider Cluster execution, and Drift Scan; each queue now uses the same canonical PostgreSQL tuple (`createdAt ASC, id ASC`, or `updatedAt ASC, id ASC` for Provider Cluster execution).
- align the corresponding operator-facing Memory/FileStore collections for Baseline Deployment, Runtime Verification, Runtime Certification, Provider Profile, Provider Cluster and Drift Scan with PostgreSQL equal-time ordering, removing backend-dependent list chronology without changing lifecycle, approval, recovery, task lease or fence-token semantics.
- centralize stable ResourceMeta comparison so equal timestamps cannot fall back to map iteration; FileStore inherits the same ordering after durable snapshot restore.
- add release-neutral regressions that repeatedly fail on 0.0.177 for all six first-task claims, verify deterministic list ordering, and prove Baseline task selection remains canonical after FileStore restart.

## 0.0.177 — Outbox availability ordering and lease-fencing closure

- close a scheduler-parity defect where Memory/FileStore claimed eligible outbox rows by random resource ID while PostgreSQL selected by `available_at,id`; an event that became available later could therefore jump ahead of older available work. All built-in authority adapters now expose the same `AvailableAt ASC, ID ASC` claim order, including equal-time ties and FileStore restart.
- normalize PostgreSQL claim return order after `UPDATE ... RETURNING`: candidate membership was already selected by `available_at,id`, but PostgreSQL does not guarantee RETURNING row order, so the store boundary now returns the claimed batch in the same deterministic scheduler order as Memory/FileStore.
- close an outbox lease-fencing defect in the notification dispatcher where `MarkOutboxPublished` reused the timestamp captured before claiming the batch. Publication now checks lease ownership using the actual completion time, so work that outlives the 30-second claim lease fails with `ErrStaleFence` instead of being published under an expired lease.
- add regressions that fail on 0.0.176 for older-available-work selection and expired-lease publication, plus equal-availability, FileStore restart and PostgreSQL unordered-RETURNING regressions. No notification routing policy or delivery lifecycle behavior is widened.

## 0.0.176 — Tenant deterministic task ordering parity closure

- close a backend-parity defect where Tenant collections sharing the same `createdAt` were ordered by Go map iteration in Memory/FileStore while PostgreSQL used `created_at,id`; equal-time tenant lists are now canonical `createdAt ASC, id ASC` across all built-in authority backends;
- close the same deterministic-selection gap in the tenant Agent queue: `NextTenantTask` now uses the PostgreSQL claim tuple (`createdAt ASC`, `id ASC`) so equally old runnable tenants converge on the same first task across Memory, FileStore, PostgreSQL and restarts;
- keep tenant lifecycle, leases/fence tokens, protected delete, runtime-contract reconciliation, approval and recovery semantics unchanged; the closure only removes map-order authority from list/claim chronology;
- add regressions that fail on 0.0.175 for equal-time collection ordering and first task claim, plus a FileStore restart regression proving deterministic chronology survives durable restore.

## 0.0.175 — Cluster maintenance deterministic claim ordering parity closure

- close a backend-parity defect where multiple Cluster Maintenance runs created in the same clock tick shared `createdAt`, but Memory/FileStore sorted only by creation time while PostgreSQL used `created_at,id`; equal-time operator-facing run lists could therefore change order across requests/restarts based on Go map iteration;
- close the same tie defect in maintenance worker claiming: Memory/FileStore now use the PostgreSQL queue tuple (`createdAt ASC`, `id ASC`) so two equally old queued runs deterministically select the same first task on every built-in authority backend;
- keep maintenance window, inventory digest/node-UID fencing, approval separation, operation leases and failure recovery semantics unchanged; this closure only removes map-order authority from list/claim chronology;
- add regressions that fail on 0.0.174 for equal-time list ordering and first task selection, plus a FileStore restart regression proving deterministic chronology survives durable snapshot restore.

## 0.0.174 — Notification delivery deterministic ordering parity closure

- close a backend-parity and bounded-page stability defect where notification deliveries created by one routed event share the same `createdAt`, but Memory/FileStore used no ID tie-break and therefore returned map-order-dependent subsets for `limit=N` while PostgreSQL used `created_at DESC,id DESC`; built-in authorities now return the same deterministic operator-facing delivery chronology;
- close the same tie defect in worker claiming: Memory/FileStore now match PostgreSQL claim order exactly (`next_attempt_at ASC`, `created_at ASC`, `id ASC`) so equal-time eligible deliveries are selected deterministically across restarts/backends rather than from Go map iteration order;
- keep scoped notification event/delivery authorization-before-limit behavior unchanged; the closure only canonicalizes equal-timestamp ordering and does not widen scope or reintroduce global scans/N+1 lookups;
- add regressions that repeatedly reproduce the unstable bounded subset and non-canonical first claim on the previous implementation, then pass only with the stable ID tie-breakers.


## 0.0.173 — Operator collection chronology parity closure

- close bounded Operations chronology drift where PostgreSQL returned the operator-facing page newest-first while Memory/FileStore fell back to the legacy full-list order and returned the same page oldest-first; add the same bounded pager contract to Memory/FileStore with authorization-before-limit and the PostgreSQL ordering tuple (`updatedAt DESC`, `createdAt DESC`, stable ID tie-break);
- keep legacy unbounded `ListOperations` semantics unchanged for internal reconciliation/snapshot callers, while making the interactive `limit` path canonical across all built-in authority adapters;
- close Security Audit backend parity in the opposite direction: PostgreSQL still selects the latest bounded tail efficiently with `sequence DESC LIMIT`, but now returns that tail in sequence-ascending hash-chain order like Memory/FileStore so `ValidateSecurityAuditChain` consumers see the same valid chronology on every backend;
- add regressions that fail on 0.0.172 for Memory bounded-operation ordering and PostgreSQL Security Audit chain ordering, then pass only after the owner-store fixes.

## 0.0.172 — Audit authorized bounded ordering parity closure

- close a high-load scoped Audit defect where `/api/v1/audit-events` materialized the complete control-plane Snapshot and filtered it in the API for every non-platform-admin request; scoped Audit now uses an authority-aware store page contract so unrelated tenant state is not cloned merely to resolve the requested history page;
- add Memory/FileStore scoped Audit paging that resolves the same Organization/Project resource families as the API scope model without taking a global Snapshot, and add a PostgreSQL CTE that resolves scoped resource ownership plus metadata scope inside SQL before ordering and `LIMIT`;
- normalize Audit ordering to newest-first (`occurred_at DESC`, stable ID tie-break) across Memory, FileStore and PostgreSQL so the same endpoint no longer changes chronology with the authority backend;
- add regressions proving scoped API requests use the bounded pager rather than Snapshot, Memory ordering matches the PostgreSQL contract, and the production PostgreSQL query applies authorization before `LIMIT`.

## 0.0.171 — PostgreSQL notification NULL-project scope parity closure

- Fix scoped PostgreSQL notification event pagination to treat organization-wide rows using the schema-authoritative `project_id IS NULL` representation instead of the impossible `project_id=''` predicate.
- Apply the same NULL-project scope correction to notification delivery pagination through the joined event scope, restoring Memory/File/PostgreSQL parity without reintroducing global scans or per-row event lookups.
- Keep authorization-before-limit semantics intact for project-scoped and organization-wide notification collections.

## 0.0.170 — Notification authorized bounded pagination closure

- close a high-load notification collection defect where interactive scoped principals requested `notification-events`/`notification-deliveries` without an explicit Project filter and the API intentionally disabled the store limit, scanned global notification history, then tenant-filtered in process; authorized data was correct but request cost grew with unrelated tenant traffic;
- eliminate the Notification Delivery N+1 authorization path that loaded global deliveries and then called `GetNotificationEvent` once per row merely to discover each delivery's tenant scope;
- add scope-aware bounded notification page contracts to Memory/FileStore and PostgreSQL so Project-scoped events plus Organization-wide events authorized to the principal are selected inside the authority store before ordering and `LIMIT`; PostgreSQL performs the delivery scope join in one query;
- add regressions proving scoped notification collections use the bounded authority-aware path without global scans/per-row event lookups, plus a production PostgreSQL query-contract regression that pins authorization predicates ahead of `LIMIT`.

## 0.0.169 — Project scope isolation and authorized pagination closure

- close a tenant-isolation defect where an interactive principal with a direct role on one Project inherited visibility of the parent Organization for navigation and that visibility was then incorrectly expanded back into read access for every sibling Project in the Organization; Project collection and operation authority now derive from backend-effective Project roles rather than hierarchy visibility;
- separate parent-Organization navigation visibility from Organization-resource authority so a direct Project grant cannot enumerate sibling Organization-owned notification destinations, private Catalog trust/release resources, entitlement/OEM summary counters or Organization-wide audit events that the corresponding detail endpoints would reject;
- apply operation authorization before collection limiting: scoped principals no longer lose older authorized operations when newer foreign-tenant operations occupy the global `limit`; PostgreSQL now performs the authorized Project-set predicate inside the bounded ordered query before `LIMIT`, while non-paged stores filter before slicing;
- add negative regressions for sibling Project operation/metadata isolation, Organization-resource/audit/summary isolation, authorization-before-pagination semantics, and the production PostgreSQL scoped-page query boundary.

## 0.0.168 — Drift remediation idempotent recovery closure

- close a durable retry gap in drift remediation where `POST /api/v1/drift-scans/{id}/targets/{clusterId}/findings/{fingerprint}/remediate` could persist its idempotent `Operation` in `DRAFT` or `PLANNING` and then, after a lost response/process interruption, replay the same key without ever advancing that operation to `QUEUED`;
- make the remediation workflow treat idempotent replay as a recovery path: the same durable operation is monotonically converged through only the safe pre-queue states (`DRAFT → PLANNING → QUEUED`) and already-advanced/terminal authority is never rewound;
- tolerate concurrent identical retries by refreshing the operation after optimistic-concurrency conflict and converging on the authoritative revision instead of returning a spurious 409 solely because two requests raced on the same idempotency key;
- add regressions reproducing the stranded-operation behavior on the previous implementation and proving lost-response recovery plus concurrent stale-revision convergence on exactly one queued operation.

## 0.0.167 — Governed release atomic revision authority closure

- close Catalog and Blueprint paired-write failure boundaries where API create/draft-update/clone flows persisted an immutable revision before the governed release mutation was known to succeed; stale `If-Match`, duplicate identity, validation or later transaction failures could therefore leave orphan revisions plus audit/outbox noise;
- introduce atomic revision+release owner-store operations for Catalog and Blueprint create/draft update, with MemoryStore committing only after all release validation succeeds, FileStore persisting the pair as one rollback-capable snapshot mutation, and PostgreSQL performing the revision plus release mutation in one serializable database transaction;
- move stale revision/state checks ahead of revision creation while retaining database row locking for race-safe optimistic concurrency; Blueprint clone now uses the same paired authority boundary as normal creation;
- add regressions proving stale Catalog/Blueprint draft updates and duplicate release creation do not persist revisions, audit or outbox side effects, plus a FileStore restart regression and a PostgreSQL transaction-contract regression.

## 0.0.166 — Cluster maintenance atomic request closure

- close a composite-workflow failure boundary where `POST /api/v1/clusters/{id}/maintenance-runs` created and advanced a durable `Operation` before maintenance-window, live-inventory and node-selection validation completed; rejected requests could therefore leave orphan `AWAITING_APPROVAL` operations and consume the operation idempotency authority;
- make maintenance request admission atomic in all three authority adapters: MemoryStore validates the run before creating its linked operation under one lock, FileStore persists/rolls back the combined mutation as one snapshot transaction, and PostgreSQL creates/reuses the operation plus maintenance run in one serializable database transaction;
- preserve operation audit/outbox transitions (`DRAFT → PLANNING → AWAITING_APPROVAL`) inside the atomic boundary and safely reuse a matching legacy partial operation when it is still in a recoverable pre-approval state;
- add an owner API regression proving an invalid node request creates no operation and that a corrected retry with the same idempotency key succeeds with exactly one linked approval-bound operation.

## 0.0.165 — Cluster enrollment expiry authority closure

- make enrollment TTL authoritative on the read surface: unclaimed `PENDING_APPROVAL`/`APPROVED` imports whose `expiresAt` has elapsed are reported as `EXPIRED` immediately instead of remaining visually actionable until a later approve/claim attempt;
- reject approve/claim/revoke transitions after natural expiry without repeatedly mutating revision state on failed requests;
- when a replacement enrollment reuses the same project/name after TTL expiry, durably materialize the old import as `EXPIRED`, invalidate its one-time credential digest, emit audit/outbox authority, and release the unique active-name fence atomically with the successful replacement creation;
- add owner regressions for truthful expired reads, denied expired-token claim/revoke, same-name replacement, emitted expiry audit, and FileStore restart durability of the materialized expiry/credential invalidation;
- keep release regressions version-neutral rather than coupling agent fixtures to the release number.

## 0.0.164 — Cluster enrollment recovery lifecycle closure

- add an explicit revoke lifecycle for unclaimed cluster enrollment requests in `PENDING_APPROVAL` or `APPROVED`, fenced by project write access, optimistic revision matching and `X-Confirm-Revoke: revoke-cluster-import`;
- invalidate the enrollment token digest when an import is revoked, emit audit/outbox authority, reject any subsequent claim, and immediately release the active cluster name for a clean replacement enrollment;
- expose `Cancel enrollment` in the Operator Console to recover safely from lost one-time manifest/token responses without waiting for TTL expiry, while preserving independent `platform-admin` approval for enrollment approval;
- add owner regressions proving revoked tokens cannot claim and the same project/name can be enrolled again after cancellation.

## 0.0.163 — Operator Console effective scope authority closure

- publish backend-authoritative `effectiveOrganizationRoles` and `effectiveProjectRoles` in `/api/v1/access/context`, preserving OIDC direct-role override semantics, durable membership inheritance, global viewer caps and project-scoped API-token organization read-only boundaries;
- make the Console permission surface consume those effective Organization/Project roles instead of treating a global `platform-operator` role as write authority everywhere; fail closed when scoped permission authority is unavailable and recover immediately when a later authoritative Workspace load succeeds;
- bound access-context computation to the principal's accessible organizations/projects and reuse the membership snapshot instead of scanning every tenant/project or repeating the same membership query;
- align workflow-specific UI authority with backend semantics: tenant approval now requires independent `platform-admin`, project operators can cancel maintenance windows where the API permits project write, Marketplace advisory creation is project-write fenced, and dead-letter retry inherits the source event's project/organization write scope;
- close a platform-wide Git authorization gap by requiring `platform-admin` for managed repository creation and signed desired-state revision publication, matching credential/provider, pull-request and rollback authority;
- classify Blueprint release comparison as a pure read-only POST end to end so project viewers can compare releases without a false operator requirement, while durable advisory/Git POSTs remain mutating;
- extend existing backend and Browser owner regressions for effective scoped roles, project-scoped API tokens, direct project overrides, Git operator denial, read-only POST classification and scoped permission transitions.

## 0.0.162 — Operator Console viewer-safe action parity closure

- fix a UI/auth contract mismatch where the read-only permission surface disabled `Create installation plan` even though `/api/v1/installations/plans` is a backend-authorized pure planning POST for `platform-viewer`;
- keep successful runtime-closure `Verify evidence` available to viewers because `/api/v1/runtime-closure-reports/verify` verifies immutable evidence integrity and does not mutate durable authority;
- make viewer-safe control/form metadata authoritative in the Console mutation classifier, so explicit non-mutating POST workflows are not fenced merely because they use a submit button or `*-action` dataset;
- extend the existing owner Browser permission regression and static Console contract to prove mutating controls stay disabled while viewer-safe planning/verification controls remain enabled;
- document viewer-safe POST surface parity as part of the canonical permission-state contract.

## 0.0.161 — Operator Console bidirectional permission-surface closure

- restore mutation controls that were disabled solely by read-only access when a live session authority refresh promotes the operator, instead of leaving the page visually read-only until a full rerender;
- preserve pre-existing contextual disabled state/title when access mode temporarily fences a control, so promotion restores only the permission-owned disablement rather than blindly enabling controls that are unavailable for another reason;
- invalidate and close an already-open destructive confirmation when live session authority demotes the user to read-only, preventing a stale confirmation surface from remaining actionable until backend rejection;
- extend the owner Browser permission-transition regression to prove viewer disablement, operator promotion re-enablement, backend-authoritative demotion, and destructive-dialog invalidation without a full page reload;
- make bidirectional permission-surface restoration and destructive-confirmation invalidation canonical requirements in `DESIGN.md`.

## 0.0.160 — Operator Console session and feedback authority closure

- re-synchronize effective session roles before blocking a read-only mutation, on manual refresh/tab resume, and after backend authorization denial so OIDC group mapping changes cannot leave stale enabled/disabled controls in an open console;
- keep error feedback persistent until explicit dismissal while routine success feedback remains transient;
- replace runtime-generated raw close glyphs in Console and Installer toasts with the canonical SVG action icon;
- extend owner UI quality gates to verify runtime toast action-icon consistency and feedback durability;
- add Browser regression coverage for platform-viewer → platform-operator promotion and platform-operator → platform-viewer demotion without a full page reload.

## 0.0.159 — Operator Console focus and UI quality-gates closure

- Close a real keyboard-accessibility defect in semantic Data Workspaces: `.data-table-shell` referenced an undefined focus token, so the scrollable table region could receive keyboard focus with no visible outline. Introduce canonical light/dark `--focus-ring` and `--focus-halo` tokens and bind global controls, fields and Data Workspace focus treatment to those semantic tokens.
- Replace raw Unicode menu/close action glyphs in the Operator Console and Bootstrap Installer with the existing single-stroke SVG control language. Status text/glyphs remain semantic indicators rather than being converted into decorative iconography.
- Add a stable owner-level rendered UI quality gate, adapted selectively from public UX/UI agent verification practices without importing an external agent harness, design-system library, runtime dependency or aesthetic preset. The gate validates theme references, 280/414 responsive probes, route-level RTL overflow, rendered-DOM names/labels/dialogs/target sizes, keyboard focus trapping, state-aware representative contrast, Data Workspace state behavior and anti-generic action-icon/copy rules.
- Extend `DESIGN.md` so semantic focus tokens, 280/414 narrow-width probes, route-level RTL verification, rendered-DOM accessibility checks, state-aware contrast, Data Workspace state continuity and anti-slop action-icon rules are canonical product requirements.
- Wire the new stable quality gate into the existing `smoke-ui` owner target instead of creating a release-numbered validator or importing the external repository wholesale.

## 0.0.158 — Operator Console cancellation and authority-truth closure

- Treat superseded page-load cancellation as cancellation, not a degradable API failure. `softApi` now propagates `AbortError`, so an aborted route can no longer inject false Partial/Unavailable sources into the successor page or continue rendering fallback state after navigation moved on.
- Fence navigation completion after `loadPage`: a superseded route no longer announces itself as loaded, steals focus, or scrolls the successor workspace after its request was aborted.
- Make Overview summary/readiness authority-aware. Unavailable cluster/baseline/workflow sources render Unknown/Unavailable rather than fabricated zero counts, unknown readiness cannot expose a misleading Continue action, and attention/recent-activity surfaces disclose partial authority instead of claiming there is nothing to act on.
- Invalidate the entire Overview action/summary surface on a hard required-authority failure. Attention, activity and the next-action control can no longer remain stale while only metrics/checklist show an error.
- Extend the existing owner Browser smoke and static Console contract tests to prove abort propagation, superseded-navigation fencing, Overview partial-authority truth and hard-failure stale-state invalidation.
- Canonicalize the accumulated changelog by removing the duplicated embedded second changelog document instead of carrying parallel release truth forward.

## 0.0.157 — Operator Console data-context and permission-fidelity closure

- Preserve the operator's client-side table ordering across authoritative page refreshes and auto-refresh cycles. Sort preference is bound to a stable data-workspace key, reapplied after rerender, and discarded if the table schema no longer supports the saved column instead of silently sorting the wrong data.
- Preserve an active loaded-record filter across a temporary collection-level authority outage so recovery returns the operator to the same working context. Genuine successful small/empty datasets still clear inapplicable filters rather than carrying stale criteria forward indefinitely.
- Centralize semantic table ordering in a reusable owner helper so timestamp/numeric/text comparison, stable ties, missing-value placement and `aria-sort` state remain identical for direct interaction and restored state.
- Close read-only permission drift for runtime-generated Inspector actions. `Requeue dead letter` and future explicit mutation controls inserted into the shared detail dialog now receive the same access-mode enforcement at render time instead of appearing executable until the API rejects them.
- Extend existing Console owner/browser regression coverage to prove sort persistence after rerender, filter-context survival through Unavailable→Recovered authority, and dynamic Inspector mutation disabling for viewer sessions.

## 0.0.156 — Operator Console partial-authority and table-ordering closure

- Separate collection-level **Unavailable** from genuine **Empty** state for all semantic data workspaces. A failed degradable source can no longer render `No records` merely because `softApi` returned an empty fallback; the affected section now names the unavailable authority and offers a page retry while healthy sections remain usable.
- Keep page-level partial-data aggregation while making section truth local and explicit, so operators can distinguish `nothing exists` from `the source could not be read` without interpreting a remote banner. Authentication/session expiry remains non-degradable and still fails at page authority.
- Add accessible client-side ordering for the currently loaded bounded table rows only. Sortable headers expose `aria-sort`, text uses locale-aware natural ordering, numeric values use numeric comparison, and timestamps sort by their raw authority value rather than localized display text. No server-side ordering or pagination authority is fabricated.
- Keep missing values last and equal-value ordering stable in both ascending and descending order so repeated sorts do not create unnecessary row churn.
- Extend the existing owner Browser smoke and Console contract suite to prove numeric ordering, sort direction semantics, filtered loaded rows, read-only mutation intent, and Unavailable-not-Empty rendering.

## 0.0.155 — Operator Console data workspace and read-only authority closure

- Replaced card/list rendering with semantic enterprise data tables for high-cardinality comparable collections: connected/provider clusters, provider profiles, tenant environments, durable operations, audit/security audit, notification destinations/routes/events/deliveries, governed catalog releases and shipped component inventory.
- Preserved all existing row-scoped inspect/approve/retry/recover/revoke/delete and support-bundle actions while adding explicit column hierarchy, numeric/timestamp alignment and internally scrollable narrow-screen behavior.
- Extended the loaded-collection filter so it filters semantic table rows without pretending to provide server-side search or pagination authority.
- Hardened read-only sessions so non-`*-action` mutations such as safe operation cancellation, membership/OIDC revocation, organization editing and Git LKG rollback are disabled before interaction instead of relying on a later API 403.
- Added owner contracts and browser checks for semantic tables, filtered table rows and read-only mutation intent.

## 0.0.154 — Operator Console contextual workflow navigation closure

- Rebuild the Operator Console information architecture around six operator domains — Home, Infrastructure, Delivery, Fleet, Operations and Administration — with contextual second-level destinations instead of exposing every feature as an equal global sidebar item. Preserve every existing route and backend capability while removing backend/package-shaped navigation.
- Move Catalog release governance into Delivery, runtime verification/closure into Fleet Assurance, and organization/tenant/service administration into Administration. Keep Blueprint planning tools available from Blueprint Expert Tools rather than promoting a planning/debug surface to primary navigation.
- Recompose resource-management pages around inventory and status with compact on-demand mutation commands. Cluster connection, maintenance, provider provisioning, assurance execution, fleet configuration, tenant creation, notification routing, catalog signing/release creation and baseline deployment no longer occupy permanent form walls.
- Make Organizations & projects administration list/authority-first: current workspace records lead, while organization/project creation, access grants, OIDC mapping and automation identity creation are explicit contextual actions.
- Add a contextual section navigation band that preserves deep links, route-specific titles, mobile accessibility and keyboard behavior while reducing global navigation load from sixteen destinations to six domains.
- Update the canonical DESIGN.md with the domain/context navigation model, resource-first page anatomy, placement rules and form disclosure contract. The redesign borrows proven interaction patterns from mature infrastructure consoles without cloning their visual identity or importing a new frontend framework.
- Harden owner UI tests for the new information architecture and preserve all existing API, dirty-state, abort/retry, accessibility, responsive and live-authority behavior.

## 0.0.153 — Operator Console final design-system closure

- Establish `DESIGN.md` as the canonical design language for the Operator Console and Bootstrap Installer, and bind contributor guidance to it. The system defines product-specific information architecture, workflow composition, typography, spacing, semantic color, density, status, form, navigation, dialog/drawer, responsive and accessibility rules plus explicit anti-generic patterns.
- Recompose the Operator Console around the operator mental model instead of backend package structure. Preserve all 16 destinations while grouping them into Platform, Infrastructure, Delivery, Operate and System with shorter task-oriented labels and quieter navigation chrome.
- Replace the generic dashboard hero/KPI-card treatment with a runtime-authoritative operator briefing and instrumentation-style status strip. Convert comparable resource collections into dense record/list presentation and reserve cards for genuinely self-contained objects or mutation workspaces.
- Turn Blueprint authoring into a four-stage workflow (Basics, Runtime & delivery, Policy & governance, Scope & publish) with stage-aware validation and safe dirty-state handling. Preserve strict JSON/API parity and overlay capabilities under explicit Expert authoring tools progressive disclosure.
- Make Platform Services observe-first: Git delivery/LKG state leads the page, while provider credential setup, repository creation and signed publishing remain available behind explicit contextual disclosures. Add bounded filtering for large already-loaded operational collections without inventing global/backend search semantics.
- Harden collection filtering across dynamic re-renders, locale changes and small-result transitions; filtered-empty state is recreated from the current DOM rather than retaining stale detached nodes.
- Apply the same final visual language to the Bootstrap Installer: replace the marketing-like bootstrap hero and repeated KPI cards with an installation authority briefing/status strip, flatten installation/health/recovery sections into workflow structure, preserve numbered installer sequencing, and keep cards only for true action workspaces.
- Close final responsive/accessibility findings: mobile Blueprint stages reflow 2×2 instead of leaking document width, expert disclosures use a bounded semantic summary grid, narrow Installer top-bar actions reflow without page overflow, and neutral checklist glyphs meet text contrast.
- Make the browser owner smoke resilient to semantically equivalent HTML attribute ordering when inlining local assets; keep parity/Expert Tools testing on the actual interaction path rather than weakening the product disclosure. Remove temporary pre-design UI copies from the embedded static tree.

## 0.0.152 - UI production hardening closure

- Closed mobile off-canvas focus leaks by making closed navigation inert and hidden from assistive browsing while preserving focus trap, Escape close, and focus return.
- Completed semantic light/dark color tokens for success, warning, danger, neutral, interactive and technical text; Bootstrap Installer now follows system dark mode as well.
- Hardened 320px-1440px reflow with intrinsic-size guards and long technical identifier wrapping without hiding resource authority.
- Added route announcements, document-title updates, named dialogs, aria-busy loading semantics, persistent dismissible error toasts, and 44px interaction targets.
- Added abortable generation-fenced Product Console page loads so stale navigation responses cannot overwrite the current route.
- Replaced Installer overlapping interval polling with visibility-aware, non-overlapping request coordination; repeated transport failures now demote stale Connected state.
- Reduced control-plane polling pressure by making active-work refresh route-local and widening active/idle refresh windows.
- Moved Runtime Certification implementation detail behind progressive disclosure while keeping operator outcome/action prominent.
- Expanded owner UI validation to cover route-by-route reflow and closed-drawer keyboard isolation across production viewport sizes.

## 0.0.151 — Operator Console UI Skills Redesign

- Redesign the production Operator Console around a task/workflow-first interface hierarchy inspired by the selected UI Skills principles: quieter navigation chrome, clearer active state, consistent inline SVG iconography, stronger page/section hierarchy, denser operational lists, purposeful status treatments and fewer decorative visual effects. The redesign reuses the existing vanilla HTML/CSS/JS runtime and adds no UI framework or external runtime dependency.
- Replace the marketing-style gradient overview hero with an operator briefing surface, reduce equal-weight card treatment, normalize panel/resource/form density, make resource details more scan-friendly, and use progressive disclosure for technical detail while preserving every existing authoritative API journey and DOM contract.
- Establish shared accessibility/interaction primitives in the Product Console: 44px primary touch targets, visible focus treatment, skip navigation, logical-property RTL styling, reduced-motion support, high-contrast status semantics, responsive mobile drawer behavior, and consistent control sizing across forms and actions.
- Redesign the Bootstrap Installer with the same production design language while keeping its numbered workflow navigation. Add an explicit mobile navigation scrim, `aria-current`/`aria-expanded` state, Escape-to-close and keyboard focus trapping so the bootstrap workflow remains usable without pointer input.
- Extend the existing Console owner suites rather than adding release-numbered validators. Regression coverage now protects the shared design/accessibility primitives, SVG navigation consistency, responsive/reduced-motion contracts and Installer mobile-navigation semantics. Headless desktop/mobile RTL/LTR console and installer smoke remains authoritative for rendered UI behavior; no synthetic product data was added.

## 0.0.150 — Recovery Authority Identity Binding Closure

- Bind lifecycle destructive-restore recovery to the exact failed restore authority: service, installed profile and backup ID must match. A different backup can no longer bypass a `recoveryRequired` fence or clear an unresolved restore merely because it targets the same service.
- Bind whole-appliance DR recovery to the exact failed backup/profile/object-storage data authority. Endpoint, bucket, prefix, provider and region are immutable across recovery; credential references may rotate without changing backup identity, so operators can recover after credential rotation without redirecting the restore to different data.
- Continue DR recovery from the failed run's durable `CompletedComponents` prefix and original restore-target identities instead of re-capturing authority or replaying components already committed before the failure. Successful recovery clears only blockers with the same exact data authority.
- Validate destructive recovery state fail-closed on startup: reject non-prefix DR restore progress, incomplete recovery target authority and conflicting outstanding recovery authorities instead of selecting the most recent ambiguous blocker.
- Surface backup/profile authority directly in installer run rows so `RECOVERY REQUIRED` has an operator-visible exact restore reference. Add owner regressions for different-backup rejection, object-storage drift rejection, credential rotation, durable progress continuation and conflicting recovery-state rejection.

## 0.0.149 — Destructive Restore Recovery Fencing Closure

- Persist a durable `recoveryRequired` fence when a lifecycle restore or state-aware upgrade recovery fails after destructive restore has begun and the service may intentionally remain quiesced. A terminal `FAILED` run no longer silently releases safety authority and allow an unrelated backup/upgrade to bring partial restored state online.
- Make lifecycle admission recovery-aware per service. Failed generic restore can be remediated only by an explicitly confirmed restore; failed upgrade-recovery can be continued only for the same source upgrade authority. Successful remediation clears the matching durable blockers atomically with terminal success.
- Apply the same invariant to whole-appliance Disaster Recovery. A partial DR restore that remains quiesced is now blocking across process restart, prevents new DR backup/bootstrap/lifecycle mutation, and permits only another explicit restore until a successful restore clears the fence.
- Surface `RECOVERY REQUIRED` in the installer run UI and make malformed durable recovery markers fail closed on load instead of being silently ignored. `recoveryRequired` is valid only on failed destructive restore/recovery runs with the required source authority.
- Add owner regressions proving recovery fences survive restart, reject unrelated mutations, allow the intended remediation path, clear only after successful recovery, and reject corrupted recovery-marker authority.

## 0.0.148 — Upgrade Recovery Admission + Retry Fencing Closure

- Make upgrade-recovery admission atomic with source authority revalidation and acquisition of the per-service lifecycle mutation slot. Runtime preparation may occur before the lock, but the failed source must still be `RECOVERY_REQUIRED` in the same critical section that durably queues the recovery; a concurrently completed recovery can no longer release the active slot and admit a stale second destructive run.
- Preserve the highest destructive-recovery boundary across explicit retry runs. If any exact prior recovery attempt durably reached `RESTORE_COMPLETED`, a new confirmation-bound retry starts from `RESTORE_COMPLETED` and can only restore the previous image/resume the workload; if `RESUME_VERIFIED` was durable, the retry is read-only verification/finalization. The bound backup is never rewound a second time after either boundary.
- Keep explicit retry from `RESTORE_PENDING` available when no durable evidence proves the restore completed, including legacy interrupted recovery attempts. This retains the intentional operator-confirmed recovery path while separating it from post-restore continuation.
- Fail closed when prior recovery history for the same source upgrade disagrees on service, profile, backup ID, previous digest or requested digest, or contains an unsupported durable phase. Corrupt/ambiguous recovery history can no longer be ignored in favor of a fresh destructive restore.
- Add deterministic owner regressions for the source-close admission race, `RESTORE_COMPLETED` continuation, `RESUME_VERIFIED` read-only retry and mismatched prior recovery authority.

## 0.0.147 — Upgrade Recovery Crash Consistency Closure

- Make explicit state-aware upgrade recovery itself crash-consistent with durable `RESTORE_PENDING`, `RESTORE_COMPLETED` and `RESUME_VERIFIED` phases. The destructive backup rewind is durably committed while service writers are still quiesced, so a process crash or final-state persistence failure after resume can never replay the same backup over newer writes.
- Reconcile post-restore recovery only from the already-restored state: re-apply/verify the previous digest and exact replica count without invoking the restore job again. Once resume verification is durable, reconciliation is read-only and fails closed on image/replica drift rather than mutating a completed recovery.
- Atomically close the source failed upgrade as `RECOVERY_COMPLETED` when the recovery run reaches durable success, so the installer console cannot offer the same destructive restore again. Successful 0.0.146 recovery history is repaired on load and duplicate recovery admission is rejected even if legacy source state was left stale.
- Fail closed for interrupted 0.0.146 recovery runs that have no durable recovery phase, because their crash point is ambiguous and automatically replaying a destructive restore cannot be proven safe. A fresh confirmation-bound recovery remains the explicit operator path.
- Add owner regressions proving post-restore replay does not require or touch the backup payload and that ambiguous legacy recovery performs no Kubernetes inspection or mutation.

## 0.0.146 — Lifecycle State-Aware Upgrade Recovery Closure

- Stop automatic image-only rollback for Forgejo, Zot and Keycloak upgrade failures. These managed services own persistent state, so returning only the previous container image cannot prove database/PVC compatibility and may create an old-binary/new-state combination. Rollout or post-rollout image verification failure now persists `RECOVERY_REQUIRED` with the exact pre-upgrade backup ID and previous digest, then fails closed without another automatic mutation.
- Convert legacy `ROLLBACK_PENDING` upgrade runs from 0.0.145-and-earlier to `RECOVERY_REQUIRED` on reconciliation instead of replaying the old unsafe image-only rollback path. Legacy `ROLLBACK_COMPLETED` state is retained for history but no longer described as proof of persistent-state recovery.
- Add a confirmation-bound `POST /api/v1/lifecycle/upgrade-recovery` workflow. Recovery is accepted only for an exact failed `RECOVERY_REQUIRED` upgrade run and copies the source run ID, backup ID, requested digest and previous digest into the new durable recovery run.
- Execute explicit recovery in state-aware order: validate the exact backup, quiesce all service writers, restore the backup while the workload remains stopped, apply the previous digest with UID/resourceVersion fencing, resume the profile's exact replica count, wait for rollout and verify the previous digest. Restore/image/resume failures leave the service fail-closed instead of claiming successful recovery.
- Expose recovery in the installer console only for failed `RECOVERY_REQUIRED` upgrade runs. The confirmation dialog states that persistent-state rewind is destructive and that writes newer than the pre-upgrade backup can be lost; the server independently requires `X-Confirm-Upgrade-Recovery: recover:<upgradeRunId>`.
- Route post-rollout image verification failures into the same durable recovery-required authority. Previously this path could leave the failed/requested image active while the lifecycle run became terminal `FAILED` without rollback/recovery authority.

## 0.0.145 — Migration Session Authority + v27 Mixed-Version Closure

- Hold one PostgreSQL advisory session authority on the dedicated migration connection from compatibility admission through all migration transactions and final schema-history verification, eliminating the runtime check/apply interleave window between concurrently starting 0.0.145+ replicas.
- Explicitly unlock the session authority before returning the connection to the pool; if unlock fails, mark the physical connection bad so a pooled session cannot strand migration authority.
- Reclassify migration 0027 (`owner_destructive_recovery_authority_runtime`) as `QUIESCED_REQUIRED`: its new CHECK constraints are enforced for new writes and legacy writers do not populate `destructive_operation_id` before destructive state transitions.
- Preserve exact, version-bound quiesced approvals; upgrades crossing both historical unsafe boundaries require approvals for both migration 21 and migration 27.
- Keep historical migration bytes/checksums unchanged.

## 0.0.144 — Migration Rollback Schema Authority Closure

- Fail closed when the PostgreSQL `schema_migrations` authority is newer than the migrations embedded in the current `platform-api` binary. A downgraded/rollback binary can no longer silently start on a future schema that it does not understand; startup reports the applied/known migration boundary and refuses the rollback.
- Require every non-empty `schema_migrations` history to be an exact contiguous prefix of the embedded migration sequence before pending schema mutation. Gaps, extra/invalid history rows and future versions are treated as authority corruption/recovery conditions rather than attempting to apply an older missing migration after later schema changes.
- Move the runtime rollback/version fence and mixed-version admission under the same PostgreSQL advisory migration authority used by schema mutation. `platform-api` now calls `ApplyMigrationsWithCompatibility(...)` as the canonical runtime entry point, eliminating the prior check-then-apply race between concurrently starting binaries.
- Preserve Fresh Install and all historical migration bytes/checksums. The existing 0.0.142 live-lock fail-fast policy and 0.0.143 version-bound quiesced migration admission remain unchanged.
- Add owner regressions for future-schema rollback rejection, gapped migration-history rejection and a direct runtime-entry test proving future-schema admission is evaluated after acquiring the migration advisory lock and before any migration DDL.
- This closes a proven rollback/schema-authority defect but does not claim arbitrary binary rollback after data-model changes, Zero-Downtime Upgrade, PostgreSQL Physical Runtime or Exact-SHA Physical PASS. Rollback across future incompatible application/data semantics still requires explicit recovery/restore authority.

## 0.0.143 — Migration Mixed-Version Compatibility Closure

- Add an explicit mixed-version compatibility contract to embedded PostgreSQL migrations. Migration `0021_blueprint_overlay_field_ownership_runtime.sql` is classified `QUIESCED_REQUIRED` because it makes new Blueprint revision columns `NOT NULL` without defaults; an older API writer that does not know those columns can therefore fail immediately after that schema commit. Existing rolling upgrades now detect that pending boundary before applying any pending schema mutation instead of falsely treating arbitrary historical DDL as N/N+1 compatible.
- Keep Fresh Install zero-touch: a database without an existing `schema_migrations` authority, or with an empty migration history, may still apply the complete embedded schema automatically because no old application writer exists. Existing databases default to `rolling` admission.
- Make the exceptional quiesced path explicit and version-bound. `PLATFORM_FACTORY_POSTGRES_MIGRATION_MODE=quiesced` alone is insufficient; `PLATFORM_FACTORY_POSTGRES_QUIESCED_MIGRATIONS` must list the exact pending migration version (for example `21`). Leaving a stale quiesced configuration behind therefore cannot silently authorize a different future incompatible migration.
- Require every future embedded migration to receive an explicit mixed-version compatibility classification before the migration package can load it. This prevents a newly added migration from silently inheriting `rolling-safe` semantics by default.
- Preserve all historical migration bytes/checksums and the 0.0.142 advisory-lock/lock-timeout ownership model. This release does not retrofit migration 21 into an online expand/contract migration and therefore does not claim zero-downtime for upgrades that cross that historical boundary; it converts a previously unsafe rolling path into a truthful fail-closed/quiesced requirement.
- High-Load, Zero-Downtime Upgrade and Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS remain unclaimed. Physical PostgreSQL mixed-version tests under live traffic are still required before certifying upgrade continuity.

## 0.0.142 — Migration Live-Lock Fail-Fast Closure

- Harden PostgreSQL schema migration under live traffic without weakening the existing cross-replica migration advisory authority. A new replica may still wait for the dedicated migration advisory lock while another new replica owns the migration lane, but once it owns that lane every migration transaction now applies PostgreSQL-local `lock_timeout=5000ms` and `statement_timeout=300000ms` before touching schema state.
- Prevent rolling upgrade DDL from waiting indefinitely behind long-lived transactions in the currently serving release. If the required schema lock cannot be acquired within five seconds, the new replica fails startup/rollout rather than holding a queued DDL lock that can amplify into a prolonged control-plane outage. This is deliberately fail-fast availability protection, not a claim that arbitrary historical DDL is online/expand-contract safe.
- Keep migration integrity semantics unchanged: migration bytes/checksums remain untouched, the transaction-scoped advisory lock remains canonical, schema changes and `schema_migrations` recording remain in one owner transaction, and the existing ten-minute startup migration budget still governs legitimate waiting for another new replica to finish its lane.
- Use explicit PostgreSQL millisecond GUC values rather than Go duration syntax and fail closed if the transaction-local migration safety settings cannot be applied. Add owner regressions proving the safety settings are established after advisory ownership and that configuration failure rolls the migration transaction back.
- High-Load, Zero-Downtime Upgrade and Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS remain unclaimed. This release bounds one proven live-upgrade lock-amplification failure mode; full expand/contract migration discipline, real PostgreSQL contention/failover testing and Physical upgrade-under-traffic evidence remain required.

## 0.0.141 — Security Audit Backpressure & Shutdown Drain Closure

- Bound the per-replica PostgreSQL Security Audit admission queue at 4,096 pending events so a slow/unavailable database or hostile request burst cannot grow the in-memory audit backlog without limit and OOM the API process. Queue saturation fails closed before a protected request can continue.
- Preserve canonical micro-batched hash-chain semantics while hardening durable DB failure behavior: after the serializable transaction exhausts its own retry budget, the currently admitted backlog is rejected with the same audit failure instead of keeping thousands of request goroutines pinned behind a persistently unavailable database. New requests may form a fresh batch and observe recovery.
- Add an explicit `DrainSecurityAudit` shutdown boundary. Public and agent HTTP listeners now stop admission concurrently, then the process waits for already-admitted/cancel-retained Security Audit work before deferred PostgreSQL close. Generated API workloads raise `terminationGracePeriodSeconds` to 75 seconds to cover the 45-second HTTP drain plus the bounded audit-drain phase.
- Make authentication/authorization DENY paths surface Security Audit persistence failure as `503 SECURITY_AUDIT_UNAVAILABLE` instead of returning a normal 401/403 while silently losing forensic evidence. Access remains denied; the response now truthfully identifies the unavailable audit authority. Session logout remains best-effort audited because blocking a privilege-reducing logout on audit availability would be a security regression.
- Add owner regressions for bounded queue admission, fail-closed backlog rejection after durable PostgreSQL failure, graceful drain of admitted events, DENY-path audit backpressure, and the extended generated termination budget. The global canonical audit chain remains intentionally serialized across replicas; this release improves overload/restart correctness but does not claim High-Load or Exact-SHA Physical Runtime certification without real PostgreSQL load/termination testing.

## 0.0.140 — Security Audit Batch-Chain Throughput Closure

- Preserve the existing fail-closed, globally ordered tamper-evident Security Audit chain while removing one-advisory-lock-per-event amplification from the PostgreSQL hot path. Concurrent audit appends on each `platform-api` replica are now micro-batched (bounded at 64 events) and committed under one serializable transaction, one global advisory-lock acquisition and one tail-sequence lookup.
- Allocate every event sequence and `previousDigest` inside the locked transaction and compute each digest from the immediately preceding committed candidate, so batching changes throughput cost rather than trust semantics. Cross-replica batches remain serialized by the same PostgreSQL advisory authority and therefore cannot fork the canonical hash chain.
- Keep request handling fail-closed: callers do not receive a successful audit result until their batch transaction commits. If the HTTP/request context is cancelled, the already-observed authentication/authorization decision intentionally remains queued for immutable audit persistence while that caller receives its context error. Batch execution itself has a bounded 15-second database budget.
- Replace the previous per-replica one-at-a-time admission-lane regression with an owner test proving two audit events share one transaction/one advisory lock, receive monotonic sequences, chain `previousDigest` correctly, and pass canonical `ValidateSecurityAuditChain`. This retains the 0.0.139 pool-convoy protection while amortizing the global lock cost across concurrent events.
- This release does **not** claim that the global Security Audit serialization ceiling has disappeared: batches from different replicas still serialize on the canonical chain head. Partitioned/signed checkpoint chains remain a possible future architecture only if Physical load evidence demonstrates the single canonical chain is insufficient. High-Load, Zero-Downtime Upgrade and Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS are not claimed without real load/soak/failover execution.

## 0.0.139 — High-Load Auth, Pool & Runtime Budget Closure

- Remove the PostgreSQL OIDC group-mapping full-table scan from the authentication hot path. Runtime group resolution now normalizes/deduplicates the groups asserted by the current identity and queries only matching `ACTIVE` mappings through the existing group/state index; an identity with no asserted groups performs no mapping query at all.
- Contain Security Audit lock convoys without weakening the existing fail-closed global tamper-evident chain: each `platform-api` replica now admits at most one in-flight PostgreSQL security-audit append, so burst traffic cannot consume that replica's entire DB connection pool while many requests wait behind the cross-replica advisory chain lock. Waiting for the local audit lane is context-cancellable. The global chain remains serialized and is still an explicit High-Load architecture limitation rather than being falsely declared solved.
- Make PostgreSQL connection budgets explicit and fail-closed through `PLATFORM_FACTORY_POSTGRES_MAX_OPEN_CONNS` / `PLATFORM_FACTORY_POSTGRES_MAX_IDLE_CONNS`, with validated bounds and an idle<=open invariant. Bootstrap profiles set a bounded API pool budget instead of implicitly allowing every HA replica to consume the prior default independently.
- Harden generated `platform-api` workloads for termination/load pressure: both bootstrap profiles now carry explicit CPU/memory requests and limits, a 60-second termination grace period, startup and liveness probes in addition to readiness, while the production HA profile explicitly enforces rolling `maxUnavailable: 0`, `maxSurge: 1` and `minReadySeconds: 10`. These are runtime safety defaults, not a substitute for Physical load certification or HPA capacity evidence.
- Fix a real generated-manifest defect in the single-node bootstrap path where the Catalog signing key environment entry emitted a duplicated `secretKeyRef:` mapping. Stable bootstrap regressions now cover the secret binding plus the high-load runtime settings across both single-node and HA manifests.
- Add owner regressions proving OIDC resolution no longer scans all active mappings, empty group assertions avoid PostgreSQL entirely, Security Audit admission waits do not take a DB connection, pool settings reject malformed/oversubscribed configuration, and generated HA rolling availability settings remain present.
- This release does **not** claim High-Load, Zero-Downtime Upgrade, PostgreSQL Physical Runtime or Exact-SHA Physical PASS. The global Security Audit chain is intentionally still a serialization point; service/DR scale-to-zero backup, online expand/contract schema evolution, component-specific certification, Production upgrade certification and real load/soak/failover campaigns remain open.

## 0.0.138 — High-Load Summary, Scope & Agent Jitter Closure

- Remove the PostgreSQL canonical `Snapshot()` from the polled Control Plane summary hot path. Runtime summary counts are now computed by bounded, scope-aware SQL aggregates and never materialize append-only step/audit history or sealed operation evidence payload bytes merely to render dashboard cardinalities; development Memory/File stores retain the canonical snapshot fallback.
- Add a dedicated global aggregate fast path for platform-wide operators and a scoped visible-resource aggregate path for tenant/project views, preserving authorization semantics while avoiding full object hydration.
- Add a bounded PostgreSQL operations collection path and make the Operator Console request only the 200 most recently updated operations. Legacy API callers that omit `limit` retain the existing full-list contract; explicit limits are validated and capped at 500.
- Remove global Project-table scans from project-scoped API-token authorization and from direct OIDC Project-role resolution. Project-bound credentials resolve their immutable Project authority directly, while organization-scoped users enumerate only Projects in organizations they can read.
- Spread Platform Agent polling with deterministic per-cluster jitter: startup contact is distributed over the first 20% of the normal interval and recurring cycles vary within ±10%, reducing synchronized Fleet bursts after mass enrollment/restart without weakening task fencing, certificate rotation or inventory semantics.
- Add stable owner regressions proving the runtime summary path does not fall back to canonical Snapshot materialization, bounded operation reads use the paged store path, oversized requested limits fail closed, and project-scoped API tokens do not trigger a global Project scan.
- This release reduces concrete high-load amplification paths but does **not** claim High-Load, Large-Fleet, Zero-Downtime Upgrade or Exact-SHA Physical Runtime certification. The global synchronous Security Audit hash-chain serialization, online/expand-contract migration discipline, service/DR scale-to-zero backup behavior, component-specific certification and real Physical load/failover campaigns remain open work.

## 0.0.137 — HA Notification, Graceful Shutdown & Migration Atomicity Closure

- Give every notification dispatcher replica a unique lease identity; an unexpired delivery/outbox lease can no longer be reclaimed merely because the caller presents the same worker name.
- Raise notification delivery fencing beyond the maximum webhook timeout and process a bounded batch concurrently, preventing serial batch work from expiring later claims while preserving throughput.
- Add a durable PostgreSQL fleet-health scan lease (with development-store equivalent) so only one API replica performs each fleet-wide notification health scan interval.
- Make platform-api shutdown wait for graceful HTTP drain completion with a budget greater than the server write timeout, so rolling termination cannot let main exit before in-flight drain finishes.
- Preserve historical migration bytes/checksums while making `ApplyMigrations()` the sole runtime owner of the PostgreSQL transaction and advisory lock: legacy files with one outer `BEGIN/COMMIT` wrapper are normalized only at execution time, unbalanced/nested transaction control fails closed, and already-applied migration checksums remain upgrade-compatible.
- Add migration `0044_notification_health_scan_ha_lease.sql` for HA notification scanner coordination.
- This release does not claim high-load, zero-downtime upgrade, PostgreSQL physical runtime, or Exact-SHA Physical PASS; the larger audit/security-audit/DR/summary scaling findings remain separate work.

## 0.0.136 — Component Runtime Authority & Planner Scope Closure

- close a critical certification scope over-claim: `TARGET_RUNTIME_V1` installs the dedicated `secure-namespace-foundation` harness and proves target-cluster DNS/TLS/network/isolation/storage/backup/observability capabilities, but 0.0.135 could reuse that same catalog-wide evidence digest as `target-runtime-certified` metadata for arbitrary components that were never installed from their immutable Catalog source; RUNTIME admission now authorizes the directly installed foundation component only and fails closed for every other component until a component-specific runtime certification authority actually installs and verifies that component;
- close a cross-organization Catalog authority injection boundary: certification evidence used for channel promotion is now owned by the Catalog release's organization regardless of `PLATFORM`/`PRIVATE` visibility, so a consumer organization may run local certification of a shared RENDER but cannot use its Project evidence to authorize another organization's RUNTIME publication; authority validity no longer changes merely because the current viewer can or cannot read the evidence Project;
- close an offline/source-metadata execution-readiness bypass: `plan.Build` and therefore `platformctl plan`, raw `/api/v1/plans`, and Blueprint flows without an authoritative Catalog release can no longer become `execution-ready` solely because component files contain exact versions, source locks and digest-shaped certification metadata. A separate authority-aware planner entry point is used only after the control plane has revalidated a bound RUNTIME/PRODUCTION Catalog release;
- propagate that authority distinction through Blueprint create/update/clone/resolve-preview, keeping unbound/static Catalog planning advisory while allowing an actually admitted authoritative Catalog release to provide execution readiness; preserve the existing 85 blockers for the shipped Enterprise Blueprint today because its current source/pinning/certification blockers already prevent execution, rather than adding a redundant authority blocker before those are closed;
- strengthen negative controls: a valid managed image mirror can no longer mask missing component-specific runtime certification, the Catalog governance owner test proves catalog-wide TARGET_RUNTIME evidence does not certify `gateway-api`, and a foreign-organization TARGET_RUNTIME run cannot authorize a platform-visible Catalog owned by another organization. Operator Console wording now explicitly distinguishes target-capability/foundation evidence from component runtime certification. Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS is not claimed without direct execution of this final artifact.

## 0.0.135 — Catalog Certification Authority Integrity Closure

- close a critical Catalog governance authority bypass: RUNTIME/PRODUCTION admission previously treated component certification metadata plus any digest-shaped `sha256:...` value as sufficient evidence, allowing a manually supplied hash to satisfy the control-plane publish path without a durable certification authority record; RUNTIME admission now requires evidence backed by an active `TARGET_RUNTIME_V1` run bound to the exact published RENDER source release/revision, source-lock set, organization/project scope and canonical profile;
- revalidate certification evidence at the point of use instead of trusting a persisted `SUCCEEDED` label: the control plane reconstructs the install checkpoint and final evidence digest from the retained PASS checks, validates the required INSTALL/VERIFY check shape, requires an unexpired/non-revoked run, re-renders the immutable RENDER source to match the recorded render/source-lock context, requires current cluster inventory/environment fingerprint and required target capabilities to still match, and rejects evidence when the source signing trust is no longer active;
- prevent stale/legacy false authority after upgrade: a previously published RUNTIME release with unsupported certification metadata can no longer be rendered, used as a Runtime Certification source, or bound into a Blueprint merely because its old signature is valid; successful certification reports also fail closed if their retained evidence no longer reconstructs correctly;
- keep PRODUCTION fail-closed: no durable upgrade-certification authority exists in the current product, so `upgrade-certified` metadata is not accepted merely because it carries a digest. This release deliberately does not fabricate that missing authority; Production promotion remains blocked until supported-upgrade evidence has a real owner/store/workflow;
- replace the image-mirror runtime smoke's former `SMOKE_TEST_ONLY`/fixed-hash certification fixture with a real local control-plane TARGET_RUNTIME run over an imported cluster inventory and complete INSTALL/VERIFY result contract before testing RUNTIME mirror admission/rewrite. The catalog-governance smoke retains a fixed hash only as a negative control and proves it is rejected. Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS is not claimed without execution of this final artifact.

## 0.0.134 — Helm Render Provenance & Live Interaction Closure
