- close a real external Helm supply-chain reproducibility gap: an immutable `render-manifest.json` was digest-bound, but the source lock/provenance did not identify the exact Helm/Crane toolchain and render-affecting inputs that produced it; Helm catalog bundles now require one strict `render-generation` contract and fail closed when it is absent, malformed or inconsistent;
- bind the Helm version, Crane version, fixed release name, component namespace, `--include-crds` semantics, exact Kubernetes min/max render versions and every product-owned values file path+SHA-256 into both `source-lock.json` and `provenance.json`; bundle verification requires canonical equality of those generation records and runtime catalog verification independently enforces the same Helm generation semantics;
- prevent ephemeral local values from entering executable catalog authority: acquisition accepts only values files whose resolved targets stay inside the repository, records their repository-relative paths and exact hashes, rejects duplicates, and repository validation re-hashes the retained files before a resolved Helm component is accepted;
- close a separate live-console interaction race exposed by the full UI authority smoke: Baseline polling could replace a resource card while an operator was expanding nested read-only `<details>`, detaching the clicked element and collapsing inspection state; automatic refresh now defers during recent pointer/keyboard interaction and while an expanded detail exists in the active page, with a headless negative control plus the previously failing live journey proving the boundary;
- strengthen stable owner/negative controls for missing generation evidence, namespace/Kubernetes-window mismatch, values provenance, runtime verification, external catalog import and live interaction preservation without creating a release-numbered validator. Real acquisition of the remaining unresolved upstream components is not claimed in this environment because Helm/Crane acquisition tooling cannot be fetched here; existing Enterprise Blueprint deployment blockers therefore remain authoritative until real upstream acquisition and runtime certification are executed. Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS is not claimed without execution of this final artifact.

## 0.0.133 — Panel RBAC, Dirty Scope & Maintenance Race Closure

- close a real read-only authorization parity defect between the Operator Console and backend middleware: the planning-only Blueprint authoring round-trip, Blueprint resolve preview and compatibility evaluator POST endpoints are now explicitly viewer-safe in the server RBAC authority as well as the browser guard, so a `platform-viewer` no longer sees enabled planning controls that fail with HTTP 403 at runtime; mutating Blueprint/catalog actions remain operator/admin-only;
- close unsaved-state loss in the Blueprint authoring journey: importing strict Blueprint JSON into the visual editor now marks the visual form dirty, reset/edit/clone/lifecycle transitions cannot silently replace dirty Blueprint state, and the shared dirty helper correctly clears a dirty root form in addition to descendants;
- close silent editor replacement in notification administration: opening another persisted Destination or Route, or cancelling an edit, now detects unsaved values and requires explicit discard before authoritative data replaces the form; successfully loaded/reset editors are marked clean deterministically;
- close a cross-cluster Cluster Maintenance scope defect where selecting a cluster without a maintenance profile could retain the previous cluster's environment and drain timeout, creating a path to save stale settings into the wrong cluster; authority refresh now resets clean forms to safe defaults for a profile-less cluster while preserving each independently dirty maintenance form;
- fence asynchronous maintenance authority loads with a generation token so a slow response for a previously selected cluster cannot arrive after a newer selection and overwrite the current cluster/profile/window/run state; add runtime headless negative controls for dirty timeout preservation, profile-less cluster reset and stale-response ordering;
- extend stable owner regressions for the viewer-safe planning endpoint set, Blueprint import dirty semantics, maintenance scope reset/race fencing and the notification/Blueprint discard guards. This release does not alter or fabricate upstream acquisition/runtime-certification evidence: the enterprise Blueprint remains planning-only until its real deployment blockers are closed, and Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS is not claimed without execution of this final artifact.

## 0.0.132 — Panel Session, Dirty-State, Compatibility & Diagnostics Closure

- close the Operator Console authentication fault-isolation defect: API 401 responses now preserve structured status/body information, bypass `softApi` degradation, and enter one global session-expiry path for both page reads and direct mutations, so an expired session cannot silently render authoritative resources as empty/partial data; ordinary read failures remain independently degradable;
- replace focus-only refresh suppression with real unsaved-change protection: active forms and the standalone Blueprint JSON editors become dirty on user edits, adaptive polling remains paused after focus leaves the control, navigation/manual refresh/language switching and browser unload guard against discarding edits, and successful form submissions/reset/canonical Blueprint import/export clear dirty state deterministically;
- make Marketplace and Tenant workflows live participants in adaptive polling and active-work detection, covering provisioning/resizing/suspending/resuming/deleting/approval-wait states instead of requiring operator refresh while durable work progresses;
- complete two existing backend-to-panel capability gaps without adding a parallel engine: Operation cards can download the authoritative `operation-diagnostics` support-bundle profile, and Blueprint Tools can execute `/api/v1/compatibility/evaluate` against a concrete Kubernetes/architecture/distribution/provider target and render the server-reconstructed compatibility authority, including blocker responses; the compatibility action remains available to read-only viewers because it is planning-only;
- close the remaining mobile drawer keyboard boundary with a Tab/Shift+Tab focus trap and focus restoration on Escape/scrim close, on top of the existing scrim, scroll lock and ARIA state;
- extend stable owner/headless regressions for 401 propagation through `softApi`, dirty-form persistence after blur, standalone Blueprint editor dirty state, Marketplace/Tenant live refresh, compatibility authority rendering/viewer access and mobile drawer focus trapping. The enterprise Blueprint remains planning-only while its real upstream source/runtime-certification blockers exist; this release does not fabricate acquisition or certification evidence and does not claim Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS.

## 0.0.131 — Panel Operator Readiness, Live State & Integration Closure

- make the main Operator Console operationally live without overwriting active edits: operational pages now use adaptive refresh (4 seconds while durable work is active, 15 seconds otherwise), pause while a form/dialog is being used or the tab is hidden, and resume on visibility; this closes stale RUNNING/UPGRADING/VERIFYING/notification state that previously required manual refresh while keeping the installer and main console semantics aligned;
- isolate read-path failures at the page data-source boundary with `softApi` and a visible degraded-data banner, so a temporary audit/catalog/notification/fleet endpoint failure no longer makes unrelated healthy widgets disappear; authoritative mutations remain fail-closed and are never softened;
- close the main Git onboarding feature gap by exposing the existing durable `/git-credentials` and `/git-providers` authority in System Services: platform administrators can create environment-backed credential references, connect the supported Forgejo provider, inspect bindings, rebind an active credential, rotate and revoke without pasting raw secrets into the product database or browser state;
- complete notification edit journeys by allowing persisted Destination and Route records to be loaded back into their authoritative forms and updated with exact `If-Match` revision fencing; conflict responses now explain that the resource changed and require a refresh rather than presenting an opaque 409/412;
- improve operator session and mobile accessibility semantics: API 401 now surfaces session expiry and returns the user to sign-in, primary navigation publishes `aria-current`, the mobile drawer has `aria-expanded`/scrim/Escape close/body scroll lock, and a keyboard skip-link reaches the main console;
- extend the stable webconsole owner contract to require the Git provisioning routes and the new live/fault-isolation/edit/session contracts. This release improves Panel usability only; it does not claim Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS without direct execution evidence for this final ZIP SHA.

## 0.0.130 — Release Integrity & Certifier Process Lifecycle Closure

- close a release-builder source-boundary defect where repository symlinks were followed by `Path.is_file()`/`shutil.copy2`, allowing content outside the repository tree to be silently copied into the release ZIP; repository validation and release packaging now reject symlinks and other non-regular source entries before reading or copying them;
- make Exact-SHA manifest literals canonical across Go and Python consumers: the Go release inspector no longer trims whitespace around `sha256` or `mode`, and accepts only lowercase 64-hex SHA-256 plus canonical `0oNNN` mode strings; an Artifact cannot now PASS Physical inspection while failing the canonical release verifier solely because one consumer normalizes manifest bytes;
- require canonical Unix ZIP creator metadata in the Go Exact-SHA inspector, matching the release builder/verifier extraction contract instead of accepting DOS/FAT metadata with platform-dependent permission interpretation;
- close a PostgreSQL runtime-certifier process-lifecycle gap discovered by the full regression matrix: partial worker-launch failure now terminates every worker that was already started before failing closed, while the timeout regression asserts process termination directly instead of depending on scheduler-sensitive PID-file side effects;
- close an installer completion-publication race where the durable bootstrap journal could already report `SUCCEEDED` while the server's in-memory `bootstrapActive` flag had not yet been cleared, causing an immediate lifecycle/backup request to receive a transient `MUTATION_CONFLICT`; terminal durable state now overrides that stale publication lag while failed runs still block non-resume mutations;
- add stable owner regressions for whitespace-padded manifest digest/mode, non-Unix ZIP metadata, external source-tree symlink packaging and partial concurrent-worker launch failure. The four release layers remain independent; this release does not claim Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS without direct execution evidence for this final ZIP SHA.

## 0.0.129 — Canonical Release Identity & Path Closure

- bind the release manifest identity to the actual packaged `RELEASE-NAME` bytes and canonical ZIP root (`4so-platform-factory-<version>-<releaseName>`), require the release-name slug contract, and reject archives missing `RELEASE-NAME`; a non-empty self-claimed `manifest.releaseName` can no longer stand in for packaged release identity;
- reject non-canonical archive and manifest paths rather than silently normalizing `.`/`..`, repeated separators, backslashes or surrounding whitespace into trusted paths; this aligns Exact-SHA inspection, release verification and archive extraction on one unambiguous file identity model;
- reject duplicate JSON object keys in the release manifest across Go and Python consumers, eliminating parser last-wins ambiguity at the self-describing supply-chain boundary;
- close a Python path-resolution bypass in the advertised non-symlink Exact-SHA boundary: release verifier, Autopilot physical preflight and compatibility bundle builder now preserve the caller path through `lstat`/`O_NOFOLLOW` instead of resolving a symlink to its target before the check;
- harden `verify_release.py` to validate the full manifest identity/schema, canonical root, `VERSION`/`RELEASE-NAME` parity, and generated provenance/SBOM release identity instead of accepting content-hash-valid metadata with contradictory product/release labels;
- give Codex Autopilot exact-release preflight and the compatibility appliance-bundle builder the same canonical identity rules, with the compatibility builder additionally snapshotting the opened release before hashing/inspection so its `sourceReleaseDigest` cannot be split across separate mutable-file opens;
- add stable owner negative controls for missing/mismatched `RELEASE-NAME`, forged manifest release identity, non-canonical ZIP paths and Autopilot identity mismatch. The four release layers remain independent and this release does not claim Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS without direct execution evidence for this final ZIP SHA.

## 0.0.128 — Exact Artifact Four-Layer Binding & Snapshot Closure

- close the remaining in-place release-artifact TOCTOU after the 0.0.127 pathname/inode fix: Exact-SHA inspection now copies the already-opened release file once into an unlinked mode-0600 private snapshot while hashing it, and both outer digest authority and all ZIP/manifest/content verification operate exclusively on those copied bytes; same-inode overwrite can no longer make the accepted SHA describe bytes different from the files that were inspected;
- give the Python release verifier the same single-snapshot semantics instead of separately opening the archive for executable-mode inspection, extraction and final SHA calculation; the verifier now creates one private snapshot with no-follow/opened-inode checks and reports the digest of the exact bytes it extracted and verified;
- close a four-layer Autopilot binding defect where extracted-artifact Full Verification could run against the locally built `root/release/...zip` while Physical Field Campaign consumed a different `PLATFORM_FACTORY_AUTOPILOT_RELEASE_ARTIFACT`: real-test preflight now validates the physical artifact identity/version/installer bytes first, and `artifact-full-verify` runs directly against that exact same path before any Physical PASS can be considered;
- harden Autopilot's own Exact-SHA preflight against split-open/self-claim behavior by snapshotting the release once, hashing the snapshot and verifying the actual installer entry bytes against the manifest in that same snapshot; stable negative controls cover forged installer self-claims, private-snapshot stability and exact physical-artifact stage selection; this release still does not claim Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS without executing this final SHA on a supported physical/systemd runtime.

## 0.0.127 — Release Artifact Content Integrity & TOCTOU Closure

- Hardened Exact-SHA release inspection so `ARTIFACT-MANIFEST.json` is no longer treated as an authoritative self-claim: every manifested archive file is streaming-hashed and compared with its declared SHA-256 before any embedded binary digest can be trusted.
- Reject release ZIPs with missing, unmanifested, duplicate, size-mismatched, mode-mismatched, or digest-mismatched files, and require the canonical manifest schema/product/version/release identity contract.
- Closed the release-artifact path replacement TOCTOU by binding outer ZIP SHA calculation and inner archive inspection to the same opened inode/file descriptor (`Lstat`/`f.Stat` identity check + `zip.NewReader` on that handle).
- Hardened `verify_release.py` against duplicate archive paths and duplicate manifest paths so release verification and Exact-SHA Physical inspection share the same archive-identity semantics.
- Added owner-level negative controls proving a forged installer digest in the manifest and duplicate archive entries are rejected before Exact-SHA runtime binding can succeed.

## 0.0.126 — Installer Runtime Identity & Autopilot Physical Gate Closure

- close the remaining generated-runtime identity gap in Exact-SHA certification: Field Evidence schema v3 now records the SHA-256 of the actual running `platform-installer` executable derived from `/proc/self/exe`, Field Campaign schema v3 binds that digest alongside the exact release archive digest, and prepare/start/resume fail closed before mutation unless the live installer's `/api/v1/access/status` identity equals `bin/linux-amd64/platform-installer` in the exact release artifact manifest; schema-v1/v2 state stays recovery-readable but cannot satisfy the current Physical gate;
- close an Autopilot real-test consumer defect introduced by the stronger Exact-SHA collection contract: real-test now resolves and validates `PLATFORM_FACTORY_AUTOPILOT_RELEASE_ARTIFACT` before execution, always performs a fresh schema-v3 evidence collection with `--release-artifact` even when local campaign state previously says `evidenceVerified=true`, independently verifies the resulting report against the same ZIP, and refuses `REAL_TEST_PASS` unless release and running-installer digests still match after collection; stale mutable local state can no longer substitute for fresh runtime evidence;
- make artifact verification independently prove the running installer identity: release archive inspection parses the canonical artifact manifest and exposes per-file digests, `field-evidence verify-report --release-artifact` compares the report's running-installer digest with the installer binary pinned inside that exact ZIP, and stable negative controls reject a replaced/wrong installer before Start/Resume mutation as well as schema-v2 campaigns for the new Physical gate;
- repair generated/runtime contract drift by updating Field Campaign and Installer Access Status schemas for the current runtime contracts, and surface product version, exact installer binary digest, exact release digest and running installer digest in the bootstrap UI; preserve the four independent release layers and do not infer Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS from these source/generated/negative-control results.

## 0.0.125 — Field Campaign Exact Evidence Chain Closure

- close a remaining Exact-SHA campaign/evidence substitution gap at collection time: independently verified Field Evidence now exposes its accepted `specDigest`, `bundleDigest` and `preflightDigest`, and `field-campaign collect` requires all three to equal the prepared campaign in addition to the existing run identity/state and `releaseArtifactDigest`; a report from a different appliance bundle assembled from the same release ZIP and same install request can therefore no longer attach to an older campaign merely because the deterministic run ID and release SHA happen to match;
- re-prove the exact release artifact at the evidence-collection boundary for schema-v2 campaigns: `field-campaign collect` now requires `--release-artifact`, independently inspects/hashes that ZIP again and refuses collection when its SHA differs from the campaign binding; schema-v1 state remains readable/collectable for recovery compatibility but remains ineligible for the Exact-SHA Physical gate;
- add stable owner regressions for same-run/same-release/different-bundle evidence substitution, no-mutation on rejected evidence, missing collection-time release artifact and mismatched exact ZIP rejection; keep the four release layers independent and do not claim Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS without executing this final artifact SHA on the supported physical/systemd runtime.

## 0.0.124 — Exact-SHA Physical Evidence Binding Closure

- bind every newly built appliance bundle to one exact 4SO Platform Factory release archive without creating a self-referential archive hash: the bundle build specification carries `metadata.sourceReleaseDigest`, `platformctl appliance-bundle build` independently hashes and validates the supplied `--release-artifact` ZIP/version, and the sealed `bundle.json` plus `bundle.lock.json` retain that source release digest under the existing manifest/lock integrity chain; legacy bundles remain readable for recovery, but new certification workflows require the binding;
- upgrade the Field Campaign contract to schema v2 so prepare records `releaseArtifactDigest`, rejects an Installer bundle whose source-release binding differs from the exact local release ZIP, rechecks the same binding before Start, and requires independently verified field evidence to carry the identical release digest before collection succeeds; schema-v1 campaign state remains verifiable for recovery compatibility but is not eligible for the new Exact-SHA physical gate;
- upgrade field evidence to schema v2 by cryptographically including `releaseArtifactDigest` in the canonical evidence inputs and verification result, while preserving strict verification of legacy schema-v1 reports; `platformctl field-evidence verify-report` now independently hashes `--release-artifact` and refuses to verify a v2 evidence report against any other release ZIP, so an auditor can directly establish Release ZIP SHA → sealed bundle → installation run → field evidence;
- close the Autopilot real-test inference gap: `--real-test` now requires `PLATFORM_FACTORY_AUTOPILOT_RELEASE_ARTIFACT`, requires a schema-v2 campaign whose `releaseArtifactDigest` equals the SHA-256 of that exact file both before destructive execution and before `REAL_TEST_PASS`, and therefore cannot elevate legacy/non-bound runtime evidence into an Exact-SHA Physical PASS; stable owner regressions cover archive identity/version inspection, required release binding, evidence tampering, legacy recovery compatibility and Autopilot exact-SHA preflight without creating a release-numbered validator;
- preserve the four independent release layers: this release adds the missing cryptographic binding needed by the fourth layer, but does not itself claim Exact-SHA Physical Runtime PASS until this exact final ZIP SHA is actually executed on a real supported physical/systemd runtime and the bound evidence is collected.

## 0.0.123 — Canonical Snapshot & Restore Atomicity Closure

- make authoritative Snapshot representation deterministic across MemoryStore, FileStore and PostgreSQL instead of inheriting backend/query/map iteration order: the shared canonicalizer now normalizes OIDC mappings, compensation steps, agent certificates, Git credentials/providers and all existing canonical resource families, and the PostgreSQL Snapshot path applies the same canonical ordering before returning; this prevents semantically identical state from producing backend-dependent or run-dependent serialized/checksummed representations;
- close a restore failure-atomicity defect in the development durable authority: `MemoryStore.Restore` now validates the immutable security-audit hash chain before acquiring/replacing live state, so a corrupted/invalid snapshot returns an error without first clearing the currently valid organizations/resources; this also preserves FileStore rollback semantics if restore is ever asked to reject invalid input;
- add stable owner regressions for canonical ordering of previously map-backed unordered families and for invalid-security-audit restore preserving pre-existing authority; no release-numbered validator, feature expansion or synthetic physical evidence is introduced;
- keep the four-layer release gate independent: Source Semantics, Generated/Installed Runtime Semantics and Runtime-Realism Negative Controls may PASS independently, but Exact-SHA Physical Runtime remains unclaimed unless this exact artifact SHA is executed on a real physical/systemd runtime with direct evidence.

## 0.0.122 — PostgreSQL Snapshot Runtime Scope & Readiness Closure

- close production PostgreSQL Snapshot parity gaps that left newer control-plane resource families invisible to runtime consumers even though Memory/File stores exposed them: notification destinations/routes/events/deliveries/attempts, agent certificates, recovery checkpoints, Git credential/provider/PR/managed-revision authority, cluster-import credential digests, entitlements/OEM profiles, tenants and provider profiles/clusters are now populated from authoritative PostgreSQL read paths with deterministic ordering; notification health therefore sees certificate expiry state and scope-index consumers see the same resource families in production as in development;
- make canonical PostgreSQL audit Snapshot history complete and correctly ordered: security-audit rows are read without the user-facing 1000-row cap in ascending sequence order and validated as a complete hash chain, while normal audit Snapshot rows are read unbounded in canonical chronological order; API-facing `ListAudit`/`ListSecurityAudit` remain bounded latest-first, so presentation pagination is not confused with canonical runtime state;
- preserve tenant audit/support completeness under heavy foreign activity: project support timelines now scope the canonical audit history before applying their 1000-event response cap, and project-scoped notification route writes authorize the actual project rather than incorrectly requiring organization-write authority; moving a project route across projects requires access to the destination project and expanding it to organization-wide scope still requires organization-level write authority;
- remove canonical-Snapshot amplification from high-frequency health paths: `/readyz` uses a constant-size PostgreSQL aggregate query for organization/operation counts (with a store-compatible fallback) instead of materializing the complete Snapshot, and the notification health scanner reads only projects, managed clusters, latest inventories and agent certificates; stable regressions prove both paths remain operational even when canonical Snapshot loading is deliberately unavailable;
- keep release evidence layered: source/runtime/negative-control gates may validate these fixes, but no Physical PASS is inferred without executing this exact release SHA on a real physical/systemd runtime and retaining direct evidence from that SHA.

## 0.0.121 — Audit Scope & Notification Project Isolation Closure

- close scoped audit completeness drift introduced as the control-plane model grew: the canonical audit visibility index now includes project-/organization-owned Blueprint overlays/releases, organization catalog governance resources, operation traces/compensation steps, notification destinations/routes/events/deliveries/attempts, cluster maintenance resources, agent certificates, runtime certification runs, recovery checkpoints and scoped OIDC mappings; lifecycle events whose later audit records omit explicit organization/project metadata are therefore still visible to their rightful tenant without exposing foreign resource ids;
- apply audit pagination only after authorization scope for non-platform-admin callers: `GET /api/v1/audit-events?limit=N` now derives the caller scope from the authoritative Snapshot, filters all visible audit rows, then selects the newest N, preventing unrelated newer tenant activity from starving a caller's own audit trail; platform-admin keeps the bounded direct store path;
- close project-scope bypass in notification reads: project-scoped API tokens and direct project-role identities can no longer list or directly fetch notification events, routes or deliveries owned by a different project in the same organization; project-bound notification resources now authorize through `requireProjectAccess`, while organization-owned resources continue to use organization authority;
- apply notification event/delivery limits after caller scope whenever a global list is requested, while explicit organization/project queries continue to filter in the store before limiting; this removes cross-tenant pagination starvation without weakening sort order, delivery state filters or organization-wide operator behavior; stable negative controls prove foreign project route/event/delivery denial, own-project visibility, tenant-safe limits and the expanded audit index.

## 0.0.120 — Notification Secret, Egress & Daemon CLI Authority Closure

- close organization-admin process-secret exfiltration through notification destinations: credential references are no longer allowed to name arbitrary Manager environment variables, notification secrets must live under a dedicated organization-ID-bound `PLATFORM_FACTORY_NOTIFICATION_SECRET_<ORG_ID>_*` namespace, credential-bearing destination writes additionally require platform-admin authority, and the dispatcher independently rejects legacy/persisted rows that violate the runtime namespace before any environment lookup or network request;
- close server-side notification webhook access to loopback/link-local/metadata-class targets: the production dispatcher resolves the target itself, rejects loopback, link-local, multicast, unspecified and IPv4 0/8 addresses before dialing, dials the validated numeric address to prevent DNS-rebinding between validation and connect, and keeps redirects disabled; private RFC1918/ULA targets remain supported for intentional disconnected/internal notification endpoints;
- make platform daemon CLI parsing fail closed before initialization: `platform-api`, `platform-agent` and `platform-probe` now expose side-effect-free help/version actions and reject unknown/multiple arguments with exit 2 instead of silently starting network/state mutation on operator typos; stable owner regressions cover the parser and notification credential/egress boundaries, and the live notification smoke proves organization-bound credentials, dispatch/retry history and restart persistence;
- remove the remaining fresh-artifact race-verifier stall mode: canonical `--full` verification still derives the exact package set from `go list ./...` and runs every package with `-race -count=1`, but isolates each package in its own invocation so a slow/high-concurrency package cannot stall unrelated coverage; this changes scheduling only, not the package set or race assertions;
- keep the release defect-driven: no target feature, parallel workflow engine, release-numbered validator, synthetic runtime evidence or project-state document was introduced; Git, installer, lifecycle, upgrade, tenant, provider, fleet and UI behavior remain on their existing owners.

## 0.0.119 — Installer Host Authority & Runtime Safety Closure

- close live systemd false-positive admission across local bootstrap, HA peer and host-deployment preflight: a present `systemctl` binary is no longer sufficient; live execution requires the systemd manager to be running/reachable, with `/run/systemd/system` present on the local host, so container/chroot/offline-systemd hosts fail before mutation;
- close fresh-host Kubernetes residue blind spots by evaluating the real systemd unit search path (with a complete fail-safe fallback including `/usr/local/lib/systemd/system`, generated/transient/attached paths) and resolving known conflicting units through `systemctl cat`; RKE2/K3s/kubelet residue in a valid systemd load path can no longer be admitted as fresh;
- fence bootstrap evidence and HA trust inputs against active/interrupted execution races: public preflight cannot overwrite evidence owned by an in-flight or interrupted run, SSH private-key/known-hosts mutation is blocked while bootstrap execution owns the host, interrupted runs still permit credential repair before Resume, and conflict conditions map to stable HTTP 409 semantics instead of generic input/server failures;
- make installer CLI introspection side-effect free: `--help`, `-h`, `help`, `--version` and `version` are parsed before transport/token/state initialization, while unknown arguments fail with exit 2 before filesystem or network mutation; extend stable owner and HTTP simulation coverage for these contracts, align shipped examples/README binary paths with the release layout, and make full release race verification execute the exact `go list ./...` package set in bounded fresh-test batches so the canonical verifier completes reliably without reducing race coverage.

## 0.0.118 — Git PR Canonical Bundle & Base Authority Closure

- close a signed desired-state authority gap before any Git mutation: revision publication now requires exactly the six product-owned revision files, requires the detached signature file, binds the revision id deterministically to the signed spec digest, and rejects rendered managed-state, revision-marker or kustomization content that is not the canonical product rendering of the signed descriptor; incomplete/tampered bundles can no longer create staging side effects or become a recorded managed authority;
- bring pull-request delivery to the same base-authority boundary as direct delivery: before creating a durable PR intent or staging branch, `main` must equal the latest durable ManagedGitRevision and exact managed tree, or—when no prior authority exists—the exact product bootstrap seed; unmanaged content already present on the base therefore cannot be inherited invisibly through a base-to-head allowlist;
- make PR candidate and post-merge validation independently exact-tree authoritative in addition to signed-descriptor, immutable-head and changed-file fencing, and apply the same canonical-bundle validation to last-known-good restore input before mutation; extend stable Git owner/smoke coverage for incomplete-bundle no-side-effect rejection, unsigned revision-id rejection and unmanaged-base PR rejection without adding release-numbered validators or parallel project-state documents.

## 0.0.117 — Git Direct/LKG Branch CAS Authority Closure

- close the remaining direct-delivery and last-known-good Git branch race without depending on a Gitea-only branch-ref update API: DIRECT_COMMIT and LKG restore now stage the exact managed file set on a deterministic isolated branch, bind it to the exact expected base commit, validate the complete base-to-stage changed-file allowlist, then advance the managed branch through a Forgejo-native internal pull request using `fast-forward-only` plus the exact `head_commit_id`; concurrent target movement or staging-head replacement fails closed, while a lost merge response is reconciled only when the target already equals the exact staged commit;
- strengthen normal managed pull-request merge with the same immutable remote head fence by sending the persisted approved head through Forgejo `head_commit_id`, so a head change between pre-merge validation and the merge request is rejected by the Git authority rather than detected only after mutation; keep post-merge complete-diff validation and atomic local PR/ManagedGitRevision commit as a second independent boundary;
- prevent historical or concurrent unmanaged repository content from becoming managed authority: every reconciled/direct/latest/LKG commit that is trusted as product-owned must expose exactly the canonical `README.md` plus the six product-managed revision files through the repository Contents API, with no unexpected file, directory, symlink or submodule; this closes upgrade safety for authorities recorded by older releases that could have inherited unmanaged content;
- make LKG rollback exact-tree authoritative: matching signed revision metadata alone no longer counts as an already-restored response-loss recovery, the live tree must be byte-for-byte equivalent to the confirmed LKG commit; otherwise rollback requires the live branch to match the latest durable ManagedGitRevision and its exact managed tree before mutation, and the post-restore tree must again equal the LKG tree before a new authority is recorded; unmanaged drift therefore cannot be preserved or adopted by rollback;
- close residual Git action availability/compatibility gaps: drift adoption returns `GIT_AUTHORITY_UNAVAILABLE` instead of dereferencing a missing Forgejo integration after a configuration/restart change, deterministic PR/direct/restore staging branch names are sanitized and capped at the API-supported 100-character limit, Forgejo fast-forward-only merge capability is proved/enabled before any staging branch or revision-file mutation so an incompatible API leaves zero partial publication side effects, and stable owner/smoke regressions prove Forgejo-native fast-forward CAS, approved-head merge fencing, pre-existing unmanaged drift rejection, LKG unmanaged-drift rejection, target movement rejection and recovery without any release-numbered validator or synthetic readiness evidence.

## 0.0.116 — Action Authority, Git Reconciliation & Destructive Confirmation Closure

- close Git pull-request authority and crash-recovery gaps end to end: persist a durable REQUESTED PR intent bound to the exact base commit, signed revision/digest/fingerprint and deterministic head branch before any remote mutation; persist the exact candidate head commit after staging, require approval to bind that same immutable head, reject pre-existing/recovered branches or PR history unless the complete base-to-head diff contains only product-managed paths, re-read Forgejo review/head state on retries, and reconcile an already-committed remote approval/merge instead of blindly repeating it;
- make pull-request merge locally atomic and race-safe: verify the approved head immediately before merge, verify the resulting base branch after merge against the persisted pre-merge base and complete managed-file allowlist, then commit the MERGED pull-request state and corresponding ManagedGitRevision in one Memory/File/PostgreSQL authority transaction so crashes cannot leave two local truths; remote-merge crash recovery accepts only the exact already-merged PR and signed revision;
- bind last-known-good rollback to the exact operator-confirmed authority instead of re-resolving a moving LKG: requests now carry the LKG id, revision and immutable commit SHA, server-side confirmation binds that exact target, changed authority is rejected, and a post-mutation retry reconciles an already-restored signed revision before recording a new managed revision rather than blindly restoring again;
- close stale administrative writes for Organization Membership, Entitlement and OEM Profile: creation requires `If-None-Match: *`, update requires the exact `If-Match` revision, and both Memory/File and PostgreSQL stores enforce the same precondition atomically so a stale admin form cannot reactivate revoked membership, restore a superseded entitlement, or overwrite a newer OEM profile;
- make Disaster-Recovery and Lifecycle Restore confirmation server-authoritative rather than browser-only: `X-Confirm-Restore` must bind the exact backup id (and lifecycle service) before the destructive restore owner is entered, while the console sends the same exact confirmation contract;
- close API-token issue/rotate response-loss duplication without persisting raw secret material: require a durable `Idempotency-Key`, persist only that request identity alongside the one-way token digest, enforce per-service-account uniqueness in Memory/File/PostgreSQL, reject committed retries before minting another token, and preserve the key across FileStore restart while redacting it from API/outbox responses; add stable owner/API regressions for stale RBAC/policy writes, PR candidate/merge changed-file authority, historical PR non-adoption, exact LKG confirmation, restore confirmation and token issue/rotate idempotency; no feature, target abstraction, synthetic certification/supply-chain evidence, release-numbered validator or parallel project-state document was added.

## 0.0.115 — PostgreSQL Certification Object Identity & Cleanup Fencing Closure

- close a destructive certification ownership defect left after schema-name isolation: the PostgreSQL runtime certifier now binds the certification schema to the exact PostgreSQL namespace OID immediately after a successful create, injects that OID guard into normal and concurrent certification SQL, and refuses subsequent migration/check/cleanup work if the same schema name resolves to a replacement object; schema identity is evidence-bearing instead of inferred from a random name;
- remove pre-delete behavior from restore-database certification and bind every created restore database to its exact `pg_database.oid`: `createdb` must succeed without deleting a pre-existing same-name database, backup/restore and post-restore checks re-verify the bound database OID, and cleanup refuses to drop a same-name replacement rather than using name-only destructive authority;
- make cleanup ownership explicit and fail closed: a certification schema/database is eligible for cleanup only when this run durably knows that its own create succeeded and has a valid bound OID, so a failed `CREATE SCHEMA` collision can no longer end with `DROP SCHEMA ... CASCADE` against the pre-existing schema and a failed restore-database create can no longer delete an unrelated database; add stable owner regressions for schema-OID guards, concurrent SQL guards, create-collision no-delete, restore-database OID replacement, and zero cleanup of objects not created by the run; no feature, synthetic runtime evidence, supply-chain lock, release-numbered validator or parallel project-state document was added.

## 0.0.114 — PostgreSQL Certification Schema Isolation Closure

- close a destructive physical-certification isolation defect: the PostgreSQL runtime certifier no longer configures `search_path=<certification-schema>,public`, which could let a missing/partially migrated certification relation fall through to production `public` authority tables after a schema/migration failure; runtime certification sessions now expose only the generated certification schema in their explicit search path, so unresolved test relations fail instead of mutating product data;
- prove schema isolation as part of fresh migration execution before and after the migration set, and gate every follow-on authority mutation/contention/restart/backup/restore check on a durably successful isolated migration phase; if schema creation or any migration fails, later destructive checks fail closed without issuing SQL against production authority while cleanup still removes any partial certification schema/artifacts;
- add stable PostgreSQL certifier owner regressions proving there is no `public` fallback, rejecting sessions whose visible search path contains `public` or whose current schema is not the certification schema, and refusing all post-migration authority mutations when isolated migration did not complete; no product feature, distribution abstraction, synthetic runtime evidence, supply-chain lock, release-numbered validator or parallel project-state document was added.

## 0.0.113 — Bootstrap First-Bind Ownership & Restart Fencing Closure

- close the bootstrap control-plane first-bind adoption boundary: `platform-api` and `platform-internal-services` now carry a product bootstrap ownership annotation in both single-node and compact-HA foundation manifests, and every durable bootstrap-object bind rejects an unowned first observation instead of treating the first same-name UID as product authority; durable UID binding still rejects later same-name replacement;
- close the earlier materialization gap before RKE2 static manifests can manufacture apparent ownership on pre-existing objects: the first foundation deployment requires `platform-system` to be absent when no durable target identities exist, successful materialization immediately binds both critical live UIDs to the install run, complete prior identities are re-verified on idempotent replay, and a partial durable identity set fails closed as an uncertain interrupted materialization rather than being adopted or replayed;
- replace the name-only `kubectl rollout restart deployment/platform-api` runtime-persistence probe with an ownership- and identity-fenced JSON Patch that tests the exact Deployment UID/resourceVersion and bootstrap owner marker, writes a unique Pod-template restart annotation, waits for rollout, then re-reads the same durable UID and exact restart postcondition before certification can continue;
- add stable bootstrap owner regressions for unowned first bind, pre-existing namespace rejection, partial materialization identity rejection, safe complete-identity replay, same-name replacement after restart, and single-node/HA ownership markers; stale fake JSON-Patch behavior was corrected without weakening runtime fencing, and no feature, distribution abstraction, synthetic supply-chain/certification evidence, release-numbered validator or parallel project-state document was added.

## 0.0.112 — Upgrade / DR Authority & Workload Postcondition Closure

- make lifecycle upgrade crash recovery phase-authoritative instead of replay-based: persist the pre-upgrade image and explicit apply/rollback phases before external mutation, persist rollback authority before attempting recovery after a failed rollout, reconcile an interrupted rollback only toward the already-authorized previous image, and make a durably completed rollback terminal so a reboot between rollback completion and final FAILED persistence can never re-apply the failed requested image; legacy interrupted upgrades without durable phase authority fail closed rather than inventing replay permission;
- bind disaster-recovery restore of product authority Secrets to durable pre-mutation target identity: before a Restore run is queued, capture the exact namespace/name/UID/resourceVersion plus required type/key/label shape for platform internal credentials, ingress TLS, managed Git credentials and Agent mTLS; restore manifests, installer-authority synchronization and transient Git-signing-key cleanup require those exact UIDs, so a foreign same-name replacement cannot receive backup credentials and legacy interrupted restore runs without the binding cannot be automatically replayed;
- strengthen the shared Kubernetes workload mutation primitive used by Lifecycle and DR: scale/image JSON Patch still tests UID/resourceVersion (plus container/current image for updates), now also validates the API server PATCH response actually contains the requested replicas/image and exposes exact postcondition verification helpers; quiesce/resume and upgrade/rollback re-read those desired replicas/images after pod termination or rollout so a same-UID concurrent drift cannot be certified as success;
- update only the stable owner-suite fakes to model Kubernetes PATCH state transitions and add negative controls for same-UID post-rollout image/replica drift, interrupted rollback non-replay, terminal rollback, durable restore-target capture, foreign Secret replacement and legacy restore rejection; no new product feature, target distribution, synthetic supply-chain lock/evidence, release-numbered validator or parallel project-state document was introduced.

## 0.0.111 — Fresh Install State Authority & Residue Closure

- make fresh management-plane installation fail closed on stopped/partial Kubernetes residue rather than inferring a clean host from a missing RKE2 kubeconfig and free ports: local and HA-peer admission now reject product-owned RKE2 state/binaries/units as well as conflicting K3s, kubelet and kubeadm state/units before the first RKE2 side effect;
- prevent stale 4SO bootstrap authority from being silently inherited by a new installation when the durable bootstrap journal is missing: preflight rejects generated RKE2/PostgreSQL/identity/session/bootstrap credentials, bootstrap PKI/signing material, staged bootstrap bundles, Git handover/object identities, HA state and lifecycle/DR state while preserving legitimate installer-access and operator SSH trust inputs;
- close the configurable-state-root split-brain boundary for real local execution: live LocalSystem bootstrap accepts only the canonical `/var/lib/4so-platform-installer` authority already used by product-owned host credentials/runtime material, while alternate `PLATFORM_INSTALLER_STATE_DIR` roots remain available only to isolated simulation/test adapters; Resume retains its persisted preflight evidence after owned mutation and does not re-run the fresh-host gate;
- add stable owner regressions for stale RKE2 state without kubeconfig, K3s/kubeadm/kubelet residue, generated bootstrap-state reuse and preservation of valid SSH/access inputs; no reset workflow, target feature, synthetic supply-chain lock, physical certification evidence, release-numbered validator or parallel project-state document was introduced.

## 0.0.110 — Bootstrap Credential Durable Revocation & Secrecy Closure

- prevent bootstrap API credentials from leaking into Kubernetes PodSpec/kubectl process arguments during installer-owned in-cluster HTTP probes: dynamic method/endpoint/actor/payload data now travels over stdin, the bootstrap token is resolved only through a Kubernetes `secretKeyRef`, temporary probe Pods disable service-account token automount, run without privilege escalation/capabilities on a read-only root filesystem, and have a hard 120-second active deadline so an interrupted installer cannot leave an unbounded credential-bearing probe;
- make bootstrap credential revocation durable across RKE2 reconciliation and machine reboot: before mutating live objects the installer sanitizes the authoritative `/var/lib/rancher/rke2/server/manifests/4so-platform-foundation.yaml` so neither the Secret data nor the platform-api environment reference can recreate the token, then applies the existing UID/resourceVersion-fenced live revocation, verifies absence on the same object identities, and removes the root-only local bootstrap-token source file;
- fail closed if durable desired-state revocation cannot be persisted: the installer performs no live credential mutation when the authoritative foundation manifest is missing/unusable, preventing a false `revoked` state that a later RKE2 manifest replay could undo; both single-node and compact HA foundation encodings are covered by stable owner regressions;
- keep scope defect-driven: no feature, target-distribution abstraction, synthetic supply-chain lock, physical certification evidence, release-numbered validator or parallel project-state document was introduced; canonical external/runtime blockers remain authoritative.

## 0.0.109 — Bootstrap Crash Recovery & Authority Truth Closure

- close blind replay of interrupted bootstrap side effects after installer process loss or machine reboot: every bootstrap step now has an explicit owner interruption policy, unknown future steps fail closed, and persisted `RUNNING` state can no longer inherit automatic replay authority merely because the worker disappeared;
- make interrupted RKE2 installation recovery verification-only: Resume proves the local kubeconfig and active RKE2 server and, for `production-standard-ha`, the exact three-node Ready management quorum before marking the step recovered; if completion cannot be proven, the journal records `INTERRUPTED_OUTCOME_UNCERTAIN` and the installer shell/HA join is never automatically rerun, while an operator can repair the host and Resume will reconcile the postcondition without incrementing the execution attempt;
- make interrupted initial Git publication authority-reconciled rather than blindly republished: Resume accepts only the exact deterministic revision ID/digest plus a full immutable commit SHA already present in durable local handover evidence or the Control Plane managed-Git revision authority, reconstructs the local handover/Application state from that authority, and refuses automatic republish when the publication outcome is uncertain;
- make executable appliance installation plans truthful about the pre-database authority boundary: evaluation and standard-HA bootstrap plans now expose `bootstrap-authority-gate` backed by the durable local bootstrap journal, spec/bundle digest binding, mutation exclusivity and interruption policy instead of falsely claiming PostgreSQL runtime certification before Kubernetes/PostgreSQL exist; planning-only/existing-authority flows retain the PostgreSQL runtime gate where that authority can actually exist;
- add stable owner regressions proving policy coverage for every bootstrap step, no RKE2 installer replay on uncertain recovery, postcondition-only RKE2 recovery, Git no-republish behavior, local/control-plane Git authority reconciliation and bootstrap-plan authority truth; no supply-chain lock, physical certification evidence, feature, distribution abstraction, release-numbered validator or parallel state document was fabricated.

## 0.0.108 — Physical Certification Truth & Remote Bootstrap Safety Closure

- make remote bootstrap staging operation-isolated and cleanup-authoritative: Prepare/Apply now derive cryptographically unique per-operation stage paths, the staged `platformctl` path is unique per execution rather than shared by binary digest, cleanup is bounded and returned as an operation error instead of being silently discarded, and failed receive/staging paths still attempt removal so concurrent installs cannot delete each other’s control binary or leave sensitive bundle/TLS material behind unnoticed;
- close interrupted-stage residue without cross-operation deletion: remote stage receive uses destination-scoped temporary directories and cleanup removes only the exact operation stage plus its `.previous`/matching `.incoming` family, preserving unrelated concurrent attempts while preventing crash leftovers from retaining bootstrap payloads;
- make PostgreSQL physical restart certification prove a real server transition: capture the live postmaster identity before the configured restart/failover action, require that identity to change before accepting recovery, terminate complete subprocess groups on timeout, and make certification cleanup a mandatory authoritative check so a no-op restart command, orphaned client or retained destructive test schema cannot produce PASS;
- make PostgreSQL TLS certification session-authentic rather than configuration-only: require the profile’s `sslmode=verify-full` contract and verify the current backend in `pg_stat_ssl`, including an active TLS session, protocol and cipher, so `SHOW ssl=on` on a plaintext certification connection can no longer create fake evidence;
- refresh and expiry-bind the PostgreSQL secure-minor baseline to the 2026-08-13 upstream security release (16.15/17.11/18.6), record the authoritative baseline source/publication date, fail closed when that baseline ages beyond its allowed window, and prevent `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` from forging wall-clock timestamps in physical runtime evidence while retaining reproducible timestamps for contract-only evidence;
- add stable owner regressions for concurrent remote-stage/control-binary isolation, authoritative cleanup, attempt-scoped residue removal, postmaster-identity restart proof, process-group timeout cleanup, mandatory artifact cleanup, live TLS-session proof, security-baseline expiry and physical-evidence timestamps; no synthetic upstream lock, physical PASS, distribution feature, release-numbered validator or parallel state document was introduced.

## 0.0.107 — Management-Plane Kubernetes Identity Fencing Closure

- eliminate name-only lifecycle and disaster-recovery Job replay/cleanup: backup/restore Jobs now use operation-scoped names and ownership annotations, are created rather than force-applied, persist the exact Kubernetes UID before observation, reject legacy/foreign Jobs without durable identity, and fail closed if a same-name replacement appears instead of deleting or blindly replaying an uncertain side effect;
- make disaster-recovery authority restoration identity-safe and restart-safe: platform/ingress/agent/Git credential Secrets are restored in place with UID+resourceVersion JSON-Patch preconditions rather than delete/create or unfenced merge-patch, and the transient GitOps signing key is removed only after durable component completion with an idempotent identity-fenced cleanup so a crash cannot make resume impossible;
- bind Lifecycle quiesce/resume/upgrade and Disaster Recovery quiesce/resume to durable workload UID authority before the first scale/image mutation; replica and image changes use JSON Patch UID/resourceVersion tests, container identity/current-image tests where applicable, and post-quiesce/post-rollout UID verification so a restarted operation cannot mutate or certify a same-name replacement;
- harden installer bootstrap-credential revocation: remove the name-only `kubectl set env` path, durably bind both `platform-api` Deployment and `platform-internal-services` Secret identities to the install run, remove credentials through UID/resourceVersion-fenced JSON Patch, and re-read/verify the same identities and absence of the credential before marking the step successful;
- add owner regressions for Job replacement/replay, workload replacement during/after mutation, UID/resourceVersion patch fencing, DR Secret restoration/cleanup ordering, and bootstrap credential same-name replacement; no feature, distribution abstraction, release-numbered validator, synthetic certification evidence or documentation state artifact was added.

## 0.0.106 — Runtime Fencing, Persistence & Resume Closure

- close tenant Kubernetes ownership adoption across ResourceQuota, LimitRange, NetworkPolicy and ConfigMap reconciliation: existing objects are now read and ownership-verified before conditional SSA, absent objects use create-or-verify, and a same-name foreign/concurrent object is never force-adopted;
- tighten Baseline PLAN → APPLY → ROLLBACK state binding: approved ADD/UPDATE/rollback operations now reject managed-label-preserving content drift and only treat retries as idempotent when the exact desired/forward state still matches the approved plan, while replacement and resourceVersion conflicts remain fail-closed;
- extend Cluster Maintenance fencing through the actual execution deadline by carrying the durable lease expiry in the task and deriving the Agent execution context from it; an expired/stale Agent cannot continue cordon/drain mutations after lease authority is lost;
- make Runtime Certification cleanup generation mandatory at the Agent boundary and helper signatures: every network/storage/backup ephemeral resource requires a non-empty durable cleanup token, eliminating empty-token side effects that could not be safely recovered after crash/retry;
- enforce Forgejo atomic-publication capability before any repository mutation for both publish and restore, so an incompatible Forgejo fails before EnsureRepository/first desired-state write instead of failing mid-publication; update the existing stable Git LKG smoke fixture to model the admitted Forgejo API version;
- repair PostgreSQL startup and Serializable retry correctness: the production startup parent deadline now actually covers the independent ping plus ten-minute migration budget, and transaction retry paths reset/replace attempt-local result slices and flags so a rolled-back/failed-commit attempt cannot leak duplicate rows, stale replay state or certification checks into the committed result;
- expose installer worker ownership in status and let the existing UI resume a durable RUNNING journal only when no in-memory worker is active, closing the reboot/process-crash dead-end without automatically replaying uncertain side effects;
- remove unused blind-force SSA and name-only raw Kubernetes delete helpers after confirming all live destructive paths use ownership checks plus UID/resourceVersion preconditions; no feature, distribution, validator family or synthetic certification evidence was added, and canonical supply-chain/physical blockers remain fail-closed.

## 0.0.105 — Runtime Network, Safe Maintenance & Forgejo Capability Closure

- make TARGET_RUNTIME network probe cleanup identity a compile-time contract: network server/client helpers now require a non-empty task cleanup token explicitly, both cross-tenant deny and explicit-allow probes carry the real cleanup token, and the stable owner test executes with a non-empty token and asserts every probe Pod is labeled with that authority so the previous Agent/cleanup mismatch cannot be hidden by an empty-token fixture;
- make Cluster Maintenance drain fail safe before destructive mutation: the Agent now preflights the complete current Pod set before the first eviction, refuses unmanaged Pods that lack a `controller=true` owner, refuses Pods using `emptyDir` unless a future explicit ephemeral-data-loss authority is introduced, and still uncordons the node when admission fails; this prevents partial drain and permanent loss of standalone/local-ephemeral workloads;
- bind every maintenance Pod eviction to the exact Kubernetes Pod identity observed during drain by carrying UID/resourceVersion in the Pod view and sending both through `policy/v1` Eviction `deleteOptions.preconditions`; same-name replacement or concurrent mutation returns conflict instead of evicting the replacement, while PDB `429` remains a distinct retryable drain condition;
- fail Forgejo service readiness when the reported server version cannot provide the atomic multi-file repository API required by managed Git publication; accept Forgejo 1.20+ and current major releases, reject older/invalid versions, and make the existing system-service/bootstrap health boundary surface this incompatibility before the first desired-state publish rather than failing later at runtime;
- keep the release scope defect-driven: no new distribution/OKD feature, validator family or synthetic certification evidence is introduced, and the existing canonical release-plan blockers remain fail-closed.

## 0.0.104 — Git Atomic Publication & Commit Authority Closure

- make managed desired-state publication to Forgejo atomic: `PublishRevision` now resolves current file identities and submits all changed files through one repository multi-file commit instead of committing files one-by-one directly to `main`, preventing Argo CD from reconciling a partially published platform revision; idempotent publish produces no extra commit, and Git rollback uses the same atomic publication primitive;
- require full immutable Git commit identity end-to-end wherever a managed/LKG/source commit becomes control-plane authority: only 40-hex SHA-1 or 64-hex SHA-256 commit IDs are accepted by blueprint/control-plane/PostgreSQL delivery and drift state, Forgejo publication/branch responses, and rollback source validation; abbreviated SHAs fail closed;
- bind bootstrap GitOps handover evidence to the exact full commit returned by publication: the Application keeps managed `main` branch tracking so later product-owned revisions are not frozen, but bootstrap records the published full commit and requires Argo `status.sync.revision` to equal that exact commit before handover is accepted;
- separate product release blockers from deployment-context Git identity in Codex Autopilot while still delegating all blocker discovery to the canonical planner: the current default plan remains 85-blocked, with 84 product-owned acquisition/pinning/certification/source-lock blockers and one runtime deployment-context `GIT_REVISION_NOT_IMMUTABLE` blocker that can only be resolved after the real Forgejo publish/observe boundary; no placeholder commit or synthetic release evidence is introduced;
- update stable fake-Forgejo integration/smoke fixtures to model the supported multi-file repository commit endpoint, including exact per-file SHA preconditions for updates, and add regressions proving one revision produces one commit and an unchanged re-publish produces none.

## 0.0.103 — Codex Autopilot Durable Resume & Canonical Release Readiness Closure

- make deterministic Codex/Autopilot stage execution crash-safe: each in-progress run now persists an atomic checkpoint containing the selected stage-graph signature, workspace fingerprint, forward/convergence cursor, repair count and failure fingerprints; an interrupted runner resumes only the proven prefix when the workspace is unchanged, while any source/stage-graph change invalidates the checkpoint and restarts from a safe boundary; completed PASS/FAIL/BLOCKED runs remove the checkpoint so a later intentional test run never reuses stale success;
- make stage timeout/restart process-safe: every canonical stage and Codex repair runs in an isolated process group, timeout terminates the complete group with TERM→KILL escalation, and the checkpoint records the active PID plus Linux process start identity so a subsequent runner invocation can terminate a still-running process group left behind by an externally killed Autopilot before resuming; add owner self-tests for descendant-process termination, valid-prefix resume, stale-workspace reset and stranded-stage recovery;
- replace the Autopilot release-readiness shortcut that inspected only `source.resolved` with the product's canonical `platformctl plan -f blueprints/enterprise-private-cloud.json` authority; `autopilot-release-test`/`autopilot-real-test` now require `executable=true` and zero deployment-plan blockers, while normal local correctness still returns `LOCAL_CODE_PASS` and reports the full plan blocker count/codes without asking Codex to patch external prerequisites;
- verify the current baseline honestly remains planning-only with 85 canonical blockers (18 runtime certification, 18 certification evidence, 16 unresolved source, 16 unpinned versions, 16 missing source locks and 1 non-immutable Git revision); no placeholder commit, fabricated source lock, fake certification evidence or release-numbered validator was added to turn those external/evidence requirements green.

## 0.0.102 — Codex Autopilot, Crash Recovery & Test Readiness Closure

- make Codex Autopilot a first-class bounded test/repair entry point: preflight now checks Go/Make, C compiler, libpq development availability, Python test modules, a usable system or Playwright-managed Chromium, and the actual Codex command executable before a repair run; the default non-interactive repair invocation uses an explicit `workspace-write` sandbox while preserving `PLATFORM_FACTORY_CODEX_COMMAND` override support;
- separate deterministic local-code correctness from external release prerequisites: normal `autopilot-test`/repair runs can report `LOCAL_CODE_PASS` without treating unresolved third-party supply-chain inputs as code defects, while `autopilot-release-test` and `autopilot-real-test` remain fail-closed on unresolved source bundles and live-test prerequisites; document stable Autopilot entry points and add pinned Python test requirements plus Playwright-managed Chromium fallback so a disposable Codex test host does not depend on a preinstalled system browser;
- close the recurring disaster-recovery owner-test flake by waiting for the intentionally asynchronous second backup operation to reach durable success before `testing.TempDir` teardown, preventing a background persistence write from racing directory cleanup while leaving production backup semantics unchanged;
- close TARGET_RUNTIME crash-orphan recovery without unsafe name/label garbage collection: every Runtime Certification task claim now durably records an attempt/phase cleanup generation and cryptographic cleanup token in Memory/PostgreSQL authority before Agent execution; ephemeral network/storage/backup resources carry that token, and a later lease holder cleans deterministic prior VERIFY-attempt resources only after owner+token verification and current UID/resourceVersion fencing; foreign/replacement objects are never deleted;
- add PostgreSQL migration 0042, API/task chaining coverage and direct Agent negative controls proving prior-attempt cleanup succeeds only for the durable token, while keeping unresolved upstream acquisition and physical PostgreSQL/HA/Air-gap/DR certification explicitly external rather than fabricating release evidence.

## 0.0.101 — Runtime Cleanup Failure Semantics Closure

- make Runtime Verification probe-Job cleanup authoritative: a successful probe can no longer report PASS when UID/resourceVersion-fenced Job deletion fails, times out, or observes a replacement; cleanup failure now emits explicit `probe-job-cleanup` FAIL evidence and makes the verification result fail closed;
- make TARGET_RUNTIME Network/Tenant-Isolation cleanup authoritative instead of discarding deferred cleanup errors: failure to remove any exact tracked Namespace, NetworkPolicy or probe Pod now appends `target-runtime/network-cleanup` failure evidence and prevents certification success;
- make TARGET_RUNTIME Storage/Backup cleanup authoritative for tracked PVC, VolumeSnapshot, probe Pod/ConfigMap and Velero Backup/Restore resources; a cleanup conflict/API failure can no longer leave side effects while the certification is recorded successful;
- update the stable Runtime Verification owner fixture to model Kubernetes deletion disappearance and add a negative control proving a completed probe with `409 Conflict` during cleanup is rejected; keep crash-orphan garbage collection explicitly open because safe post-crash deletion still requires durable pre-crash UID authority rather than name/label-only cleanup.

## 0.0.100 — Runtime Probe Termination Evidence & RBAC Closure

- remove the real-cluster `pods/log` permission mismatch from TARGET_RUNTIME network certification without widening the Agent ClusterRole: the digest-pinned `platform-probe` now writes its compact JSON result to Kubernetes' termination-message file, and Network/Tenant Isolation certification reads the authoritative result from `Pod.status.containerStatuses[].state.terminated.message` instead of the log subresource;
- move Runtime Verification probe-result collection to the same Pod termination-status contract, preserving Job-UID ownership selection while eliminating the baseline `pods/log` Role permission entirely;
- add probe termination-message encoding coverage, current-Job-UID status-selection regressions, network deny-proof fixtures based on real termination status, and a generated-manifest regression that forbids reintroducing `pods/log` access;
- keep prior-attempt TARGET_RUNTIME orphan cleanup explicitly open: automatic deletion based only on deterministic name/label was rejected because the exact pre-crash Kubernetes UID is not durable, so this release does not introduce an unsafe garbage collector or claim that crash-orphan cleanup is solved.

## 0.0.99 — Runtime Certification Resource Identity & Contract Closure

- align the real Agent Foundation INSTALL result with the Control Plane certification contract by emitting an authoritative `apply/Namespace/...` check for the separately created fresh namespace; add a cross-layer regression that passes the actual Agent-generated result directly through `ValidateRuntimeCertificationResultShape` so synthetic smoke fixtures can no longer hide apply-count drift;
- make Foundation VERIFY compare the live Kubernetes objects against all rendered desired fields instead of certifying existence/name alone, so drifted ResourceQuota, NetworkPolicy, ConfigMap or other managed state produces explicit failed verification evidence; make JSON numeric comparison semantic across in-memory integers and Kubernetes JSON float decoding;
- replace blind force-SSA for Runtime Certification network/storage/backup ephemeral resources with create-or-verify ownership semantics, bind cleanup to the exact `platform.4so.io/runtime-certification` owner and Kubernetes UID, and delete using fresh UID+resourceVersion preconditions so concurrent foreign/replacement resources are never adopted or removed by stale cleanup;
- fence Runtime Verification probe Job pre-cleanup and deferred cleanup to the verification ownership label and exact Job UID/resourceVersion, preventing a stale attempt from deleting a foreign or replacement Job with the same deterministic name;
- fence Tenant Suspend Pod deletion to the exact controller-managed Pod UID after a live ownership re-read and use UID+resourceVersion delete preconditions, so a same-name replacement or loss of controller ownership fails closed rather than being deleted;
- add negative controls for concurrent foreign certification objects, replacement cleanup, foreign/replacement Runtime Verification Jobs, same-name Tenant Pod replacement, real Agent INSTALL contract parity and post-install Foundation drift, without adding release-numbered validators or broadening product scope.

## 0.0.98 — Baseline Plan/Apply/Rollback ResourceVersion Fencing Closure

- bind Baseline planning to the actual live managed fields instead of trusting the `platform.4so.io/desired-digest` annotation alone, so a stale annotation can no longer hide post-deployment drift and produce a false `NOOP`; tolerate unrelated server/default fields while requiring every desired field to match;
- bind approved Baseline plan rollback evidence to the observed Kubernetes UID and normalized object digest for `NOOP`/`UPDATE`, then make APPLY re-read that exact identity/state before mutation: planned ADD uses create-or-verify, planned UPDATE rejects replacement or unapproved post-plan drift, and mutations use the current `resourceVersion` as an optimistic-concurrency precondition rather than blind force-SSA;
- fence `RESTORE_PREIMAGE` rollback to the exact object UID captured during PLAN and restore through conditional `PUT` with the current `resourceVersion`; same-name replacement or a concurrent mutation now fails closed with conflict instead of being overwritten, while an already-restored pre-image remains a safe no-op for resumed rollback;
- close the remaining existing-object ownership TOCTOU in Tenant Namespace/Velero Schedule reconciliation, Provider Cluster APPLY and Runtime Certification namespace retry by using conditional server-side apply after ownership verification; a replacement between GET and PATCH can no longer be force-adopted;
- add negative controls for stale desired-digest drift, foreign concurrent Baseline ADD, post-plan drift, resourceVersion conflicts and conditional owned-resource mutation, and update the stable Plan Safety smoke to carry the new PLAN-bound rollback identity evidence without weakening validators or adding release-numbered gates.

## 0.0.97 — Maintenance Node UID, Baseline Rollback & Certification Ownership Closure

- bind every Cluster Maintenance run durably to the Kubernetes Node UIDs observed in the exact requested inventory; approval and claim re-verify the same name→UID identities, the Agent verifies the live UID before cordon/drain, and cordon/uncordon use an atomic JSON Patch UID test so same-name replacement cannot receive an approved maintenance action; persist the binding in PostgreSQL migration 0041 rather than deriving identity after task claim;
- close the destructive Baseline rollback replacement race: `DELETE_CREATED_RESOURCE` now re-reads the live object, proves the baseline deployment/desired-digest ownership annotations, captures its Kubernetes UID and deletes with `DeleteOptions.preconditions.uid`, so a foreign or same-name replacement is never removed by stale rollback intent;
- close the Runtime Certification fresh-namespace create race by replacing absent-namespace force-SSA with create-or-verify semantics: `POST` the Namespace, re-read on `409 Conflict`, and reject a concurrent foreign namespace without any `force=true` patch; interrupted retries may reconcile only a namespace that proves ownership by the same catalog release/component;
- make the Maintenance node identity part of the durable Run/API contract, fail old/incomplete unfinished runs closed when no approved UID map exists, add negative controls for same-digest node replacement, missing UID inventories, foreign certification namespace creation and UID-fenced rollback deletion, and extend the stable Maintenance smoke to prove persisted/task `nodeUids` without adding release-numbered validators.

## 0.0.96 — PostgreSQL HA Migration Serialization & Startup Budget Closure

- serialize schema bootstrap and every migration decision across concurrently starting `platform-api` replicas with PostgreSQL transaction-scoped advisory locking; each waiter re-reads `schema_migrations` only after acquiring the lock, eliminating duplicate DDL/record races during three-replica HA startup;
- keep the migration lock crash-safe by using `pg_advisory_xact_lock` inside each schema/migration transaction rather than a session lock that could be stranded in a pooled connection;
- separate the 10-second PostgreSQL connectivity probe from schema migration execution so migrations and replicas legitimately waiting on the HA migration lock receive an independent 10-minute startup budget instead of inheriting the ping deadline;
- preserve per-migration transactional rollback and checksum-drift fail-closed behavior, and extend the stable PostgreSQL owner tests to prove advisory-lock ordering before schema creation/check/apply without adding release-numbered validators.

## 0.0.95 — Agent Resource UID Fencing & Provider Ownership Closure

- make Tenant Suspend classify a Pod as durably controller-managed only when an ownerReference explicitly carries `controller=true`; a mere owner reference no longer authorizes destructive suspension, preventing Pods with non-controller owners from being deleted under a false resume guarantee;
- close the Provider Cluster APPLY ownership boundary in the shared `4so-provider-system` namespace: existing Clusters are ownership-verified before SSA, missing Clusters are created with `POST`, and `409 Conflict` is re-read and rejected when a concurrent foreign Cluster took the name instead of being force-adopted;
- fence ownership-sensitive Tenant Namespace, Tenant Velero Schedule, legacy Schedule and Provider Cluster deletion with Kubernetes `DeleteOptions.preconditions.uid`, so a same-name resource replacement between ownership read and DELETE returns conflict instead of deleting the replacement object;
- add negative-control coverage for non-controller Pod owners, concurrent foreign Provider Cluster creation and destructive same-name replacement races while keeping the existing owner suites and validator structure stable;
- correct release SBOM annotation semantics so optional integrations such as Open Cluster Management are described as present only when explicitly bundled instead of being claimed as unconditional appliance contents.

## 0.0.94 — Tenant Atomic Resource Ownership & Legacy Cleanup Closure

- close the Tenant provision TOCTOU boundary for cluster-scoped Namespace and shared Velero Schedule resources: missing protected resources are now created with Kubernetes `POST`, `409 Conflict` is re-read and ownership-verified, and no SSA `force=true` mutation is allowed against a concurrently created foreign resource;
- preserve normal reconciliation for already-owned Namespace/Schedule resources while keeping the remaining tenant-owned resources on the existing server-side-apply path;
- close the direct-delete upgrade edge case for pre-runtime-contract Tenant states by deleting an owned legacy `tenant-platform-backup` Schedule even when the Tenant never passed through ACTIVE reconcile after upgrade;
- strengthen legacy backup ownership before destructive cleanup: both 4SO tenant ownership labels and the Velero `includedNamespaces` target must identify the same Tenant, otherwise deletion fails closed as ambiguous;
- add negative-control coverage for concurrent foreign Namespace/Schedule creation and for legacy Schedule cleanup/ambiguity without introducing release-numbered validators.
