## 0.0.93 — Tenant Lifecycle Runtime Contract Closure

- align the Control Plane evidence contract with the real Agent Suspend workflow: `SUSPEND` now requires the eight normal Tenant policy artifacts plus authoritative `suspension/no-running-pods` evidence, so a successful Agent suspend report is no longer rejected after workloads have already been stopped; extend the stable Tenant owner smoke through Suspend → Resume using the real nine/eight-artifact contracts;
- make protected Tenant deletion cover the full product-owned runtime footprint: verify ownership of both the Tenant Namespace and its deterministic Velero Schedule before any destructive mutation, delete the shared-namespace Schedule first, then delete/observe the Namespace, and report `Deleted=true` only after both are absent; wrong-owner Schedule/Namespace objects fail closed without mutation;
- add durable Tenant runtime resource-contract versioning and PostgreSQL migration 0040 so ACTIVE Tenants loaded from older releases are automatically reconciled exactly once through the normal fenced Agent PROVISION path; after the new per-Tenant Schedule is applied and verified, an owned legacy global `tenant-platform-backup` Schedule is removed without touching a legacy Schedule owned by another Tenant;
- add upgrade, deletion, ownership and real-Agent evidence regressions in the existing owner suites and keep the change fail-closed without release-numbered validators or one-shot migration scripts.

## 0.0.92 — Tenant Backup Identity, RBAC and Namespace Ownership Closure

- make every Tenant Velero Schedule identity tenant-unique instead of reusing the global `tenant-platform-backup` name in the shared `velero` namespace, preventing one tenant from force-applying over another tenant's backup schedule; bind completion evidence to the exact deterministic per-tenant Schedule identity;
- fail closed before PROVISION/RESIZE/SUSPEND/RESUME mutation when a target Namespace already exists but is not labeled as managed by the same Tenant, preventing server-side apply with `force=true` from taking ownership of an unrelated or stale tenant Namespace; allow PROVISION to resume only when an existing Namespace proves the same Tenant ownership;
- align generated Agent RBAC with real runtime mutations by granting the previously missing `serviceaccounts` and Velero `schedules` permissions required by Runtime Certification and Tenant backup policy execution, with manifest regression coverage so fake Kubernetes smoke cannot hide a real-cluster `403 Forbidden`;
- add owner regressions for tenant-unique backup identity, API task identity, wrong-owner Namespace preflight and generated RBAC, while keeping the broader Agent credential blast-radius hardening as a separate non-functional follow-up rather than weakening current runtime workflows.

## 0.0.91 — Optional Fleet Hub and Installer Capability Closure

- Removed Open Cluster Management from the mandatory appliance bootstrap boundary: OCM is now included, air-gap indexed, deployed, and verified only when an explicit digest-locked OCM manifest is present in the bundle.
- Preserved optional OCM integration without making Factory installation depend on an unused fleet-management subsystem.
- Removed the planning-only `existing-kubernetes` option from the executable installer UI so users are no longer sent into a guaranteed planner rejection; API/planner semantics remain available for future target adapters.
- Added regression coverage for OCM-free bundle admission/bootstrap step selection and executable-installer capability exposure.

## 0.0.90 — Unified Agent Execution Fencing and Tenant Suspension Closure

- extend durable Agent task ownership to Baseline Deployment, Runtime Verification and Runtime Certification with monotonic fence tokens and active leases, closing the remaining immediate/multi-Agent double-claim paths left outside the 0.0.89 Tenant/Provider fencing contract;
- make Baseline planning respect active leases, fence stale reports after recovery, and fail an abandoned destructive rollback into explicit owner recovery instead of replaying rollback side effects after lease expiry;
- bind Agent-side Baseline, Runtime Verification, Runtime Certification, Tenant and Provider execution contexts to the authoritative lease deadline so an old worker cannot intentionally continue Kubernetes mutation after its execution lease expires;
- make Tenant Suspend a runtime-observed lifecycle operation: reject standalone Pods before mutation, establish the `pods=0` admission barrier, delete controller-managed Pods, verify zero remaining Pods from the Kubernetes API, and emit authoritative suspension evidence before committing `SUSPENDED`;
- add PostgreSQL migration 0039 and keep Memory/PostgreSQL/API/Agent schemas aligned for the newly fenced task families, including phase-to-phase lease release for Runtime Certification;
- add concurrent two-claimant regressions, stale-fence recovery tests, destructive rollback lease-expiry coverage, Agent lease-deadline coverage and Tenant suspension behavior tests; update HTTP/UI smoke consumers to report the claimed fence token without weakening validation.

## 0.0.89 — Agent Task Execution Lease Fencing Closure

- add durable lease ownership and monotonic task fence tokens to Tenant, Provider Profile and Provider Cluster agent tasks so active work is never immediately reissued to a second agent;
- reject stale or unfenced task reports and allow bounded recovery of idempotent non-destructive work only after lease expiry with a strictly newer fence token;
- fail abandoned destructive DELETE work into its owner recovery authority instead of replaying a potentially duplicated destructive side effect;
- persist the fencing contract in PostgreSQL migration 0038 and align Memory/PostgreSQL/API/Agent task schemas and behavior;
- make the fleet Agent Deployment use `Recreate` as defense in depth against rollout overlap, add a true two-caller ownership regression, exclude `.pytest_cache` from release artifacts, and serialize extracted-artifact race packages so the full verifier cannot deadlock under cross-package resource contention.

## 0.0.88 — Agent Task Recovery and Maintenance Fencing Closure

- make Cluster Maintenance ownership fail-safe across long drains and lost workers: advertise an explicit fenced-report Agent capability, size the lease from the requested node/drain workload instead of a fixed two minutes, reject stale fence/expired-lease reports, and transition abandoned destructive maintenance to `NEEDS_OPERATOR` rather than silently retrying or leaving it `RUNNING` forever;
- align PostgreSQL and in-memory Cluster Maintenance semantics so expired/cancelled windows, inventory drift and stale running leases commit their terminal transitions even when no task is returned, eliminating queued/running operations that could otherwise remain permanently stuck;
- make read-only Provider Profile verification recover from a lost report by reissuing `VERIFYING` tasks with a new attempt/revision and fencing reports from the stale attempt;
- make declarative Provider Cluster APPLY and Tenant provision/suspend/resume/resize recover from Agent restart or lost reports by reissuing in-flight states with incremented attempts/revisions, while retaining conflict rejection for stale reports and the existing observed-delete safety contract for destructive tenant deletion;
- update the Agent inventory/runtime and Cluster Maintenance smoke contract to exercise the fenced-report capability and operation fence token without weakening recovery/inventory-drift gates.

## 0.0.87 — Runtime Evidence Attempt and Lifecycle Identity Closure

- bind Runtime Verification probe evidence to the exact Kubernetes Job UID created for the current attempt, so a Pod left terminating from an earlier same-name Job cannot supply stale logs to a later verification;
- make network/tenant-isolation negative certification authoritative: a generic Failed Pod no longer proves NetworkPolicy denial, and the negative path must return structured probe evidence showing DNS succeeded, TCP did not connect, and the failure was the expected TCP connection failure;
- scope Loki and Alertmanager observability evidence to the current runtime-certification execution attempt, verify the exact pushed probe line rather than merely any matching stream, and reject stale prior-attempt logs/alerts after report-failed retries;
- make Lifecycle run and producer backup identifiers collision-safe when the wall clock repeats or moves backward, use the same uniqueness rule for legacy recovered runs missing a backup ID, and fail closed when durable lifecycle state already contains ambiguous duplicate run/producer-backup identities.

## 0.0.86 — Kubernetes Deletion Observation and Certification Retry Closure

- make tenant destructive deletion authoritative instead of optimistic: the Agent observes Namespace disappearance, the Control Plane keeps the tenant/destructive operation `DELETING/RUNNING` until that proof arrives, and rolling upgrades fail closed by advertising/claiming the new behavior through the `tenant-delete-observed` inventory capability rather than assuming an Agent version;
- make Kubernetes object replacement wait for deletion completion before recreating deterministic Runtime Verification Jobs, eliminating retry/report-failure races with objects still `Terminating`;
- make Baseline rollback observe deletion of forward-created resources before reporting rollback success, so DELETE acceptance is no longer mistaken for completed rollback;
- strengthen target Runtime Certification backup/restore proof by observing the backup probe ConfigMap as absent before Velero Restore begins, eliminating a false PASS where Velero could skip an object that had not actually disappeared;
- isolate Runtime Certification retry resources by task attempt after the first attempt so PVC/Snapshot/Velero/Network probes from a previous report-failed attempt cannot collide with asynchronously terminating objects on retry;
- update destructive smoke fixtures to advertise the actual observed-delete capability and report explicit deletion evidence, preserving stale-recovery and owner-operation gates without weakening product semantics.

## 0.0.85 — Runtime Contract, Crash Consistency and Resume Closure

- align executable installation planning with the bootstrap runner that actually ships: unsupported external Git/registry/database/identity adapters and unimplemented ACME/external-certificate modes now fail closed, provider/credential/TLS/DNS/storage contracts are validated at planning time, HA management nodes must be unique, and the planner's normalized effective request is the exact request persisted and executed;
- make durable bootstrap resume truthful after real host mutation: do not re-run fresh-host preflight once its accepted step is durable, revalidate the current executable contract, SpecDigest and BundleDigest before any resumed side effect, prevent PENDING/RUNNING/FAILED runs from being overwritten by a new Start, fail startup on unreadable journal state, and keep Status responsive while long-running execution owns only the execution guard rather than the status mutex;
- introduce a shared fail-closed durable-file replacement primitive with file sync, atomic rename and parent-directory sync, and apply it to bootstrap side effects/journals, control-plane state, lifecycle/DR state and job manifests, installer-access state, agent credential cache, field-campaign state and platformctl private state so a durable journal cannot outrun the filesystem mutation it records;
- harden lifecycle and DR crash boundaries: fsync service backup payloads before hashing and committing metadata, make DR backup/run identifiers collision-safe even under identical timestamps, refresh agent credentials from the authoritative Kubernetes Secret before trusting a local cache, and preserve fail-closed mutation exclusivity across interrupted bootstrap/lifecycle/DR operations;
- make external Catalog runtime bundle installation crash-repairable and immutable: write and sync a complete staging tree before atomic publication, repair only partial directories whose existing files exactly match verified evidence, and reject any conflicting or unexpected content;
- make Host Deployment and Remote Bootstrap directory swaps durable and recoverable: sync parent directories around bundle/stage renames, keep the previous remote stage until the new stage is durably published, recover an interrupted swap on retry, and use durable private-file writes instead of silently ignoring directory-sync failures;
- remove an Installer smoke race that could mistake the previous terminal bootstrap run for a newly accepted asynchronous run; assurance now waits for a new run identity and expected profile rather than changing correct product timing.

## 0.0.84 — DR Integrity, Encryption and Signing Authority Closure

- bind every whole-appliance DR payload to a SHA-256 sidecar, verify all component objects before quiesce/destructive restore, and re-verify each payload immediately before its component mutation so truncated/corrupt backups fail closed;
- encrypt PostgreSQL, Forgejo and zot backup payloads and all checksum sidecars with the same S3 SSE-AES256 contract already used for platform authority data, eliminating mixed encrypted/unencrypted backup sets;
- version the durable backup format and reset legacy interrupted backup progress before resume, preventing a pre-integrity partial run from skipping old components and later writing a misleading current-format completion marker;
- preserve the installer-local GitOps Ed25519 signing authority across whole-appliance DR without making Kubernetes a new long-term source of truth: backup reads the private key from the verified Manager node, restore mirrors it back to installer-local state, then removes the transient Kubernetes copy;
- persist the Manager authority backup node before side effects and add owner regressions for digest ordering, shell syntax, encryption, legacy-resume migration, GitOps key continuity, Manager-node pinning and transient-secret cleanup.

## 0.0.83 — DR Runtime Authority, Lifecycle HA and Mutation Correctness Closure

- extend whole-appliance DR with off-node backup/restore of runtime authority secrets required to preserve Forgejo admin identity, API session/catalog signing authority and ingress TLS/CA, repair the Argo CD internal-Git credential after restore, and mirror restored authority back into installer-local state without reintroducing the revoked bootstrap credential;
- add a profile-bound completion marker and restore preflight covering every whole-appliance component so incomplete/legacy backup sets fail before quiesce or destructive restore, and make DR/Lifecycle Job replacement synchronously remove any prior immutable Job before apply;
- make service Lifecycle profile-aware and durable: HA Forgejo/Keycloak backups use their actual database-owner credentials, logical restores no longer require database create/drop authority, backup/restore quiesce and observe writer termination, failed restores remain quiesced, and HA replica counts are restored correctly;
- pin Lifecycle hostPath maintenance Jobs to the verified installer/RKE2 execution node and run filesystem maintenance with the authority required to preserve PVC ownership while privilege escalation remains disabled;
- serialize bootstrap, service Lifecycle and whole-appliance DR mutation domains across HTTP admission and restart/reconcile state, while preserving the explicit Resume path for an interrupted durable bootstrap run; add owner regressions for shell syntax, secret recreation, HA credentials, quiesce barriers, Job cleanup, durable profile/node identity and mutation overlap.

## 0.0.82 — Disaster-Recovery PostgreSQL, Shell and Filesystem Correctness Closure

- replace HA `pg_dumpall`/single-app-role whole-cluster backup with three owner-scoped logical database dumps for `platform_factory`, `keycloak` and `forgejo`, using the actual CNPG-managed credentials for each database and retaining a single-node legacy restore fallback while rejecting unsafe legacy HA restores;
- make PostgreSQL dump/restore failure propagation explicit by removing left-side `pg_dump | gzip` and `gunzip | psql` pipelines whose failures could be hidden without `pipefail`; every dump, decompression and `psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1` step is now sequenced with durable `&&` boundaries;
- isolate S3 object keys in a quoted environment variable instead of interpolating ObjectStorage prefix data into shell programs, and strictly validate/YAML-quote the external Secret name as a Kubernetes DNS-1123 subdomain;
- run DR maintenance jobs with the filesystem authority required to read/preserve Forgejo/zot PVC ownership and the host RKE2 kubeconfig used by Agent-PKI restore, while disabling privilege escalation;
- add owner-suite regressions covering HA/single-node PostgreSQL credential contracts, legacy restore behavior, shell-injection boundaries, hidden pipeline failures, strict credential references and maintenance filesystem authority.

## 0.0.81 — Disaster-Recovery Quiesce Barrier and Durable Profile Closure

- make whole-appliance backup/restore wait until every API, Keycloak, Forgejo and zot writer pod has actually terminated after scale-to-zero before any backup or restore job may begin;
- fail the quiesce boundary closed if writer shutdown cannot be observed, and recover every workload already scaled down when that pre-side-effect barrier fails;
- persist the installation profile with every DR run and make it authoritative during restart/reconcile, preventing an HA operation from silently resuming with single-node replica counts when current installer status is unavailable or has drifted;
- reject DR execution when neither durable nor migration-fallback profile identity is available, and add owner-suite regressions for shutdown ordering, observation failure recovery and durable profile migration.
- replace the live Catalog trust smoke's fixed 250 ms timing assumption with a condition-based UI wait so assurance follows real async API completion instead of producing intermittent false failures.
- align full extracted-artifact verification with the canonical smoke environment, preventing false 401 failures while leaving production authentication defaults fail-closed.

## 0.0.80 — Disaster-Recovery Consistency Quiesce Closure

- quiesce platform API, Keycloak, Forgejo and zot writers before whole-appliance backup/restore so PostgreSQL, repository and registry state are not captured or rewritten while authoritative writers remain active;
- persist the pre-quiesce Forgejo/zot node placement before side effects and pin RWO backup jobs to those nodes, preserving restart/reconcile safety without depending on a live service pod;
- restore profile-correct replica counts after backup, keep workloads intentionally quiesced after a failed restore to avoid serving mixed state, and recover already-stopped workloads if quiesce itself fails before backup/restore begins;
- add owner-suite regression coverage for HA/single-node replica restoration, RWO placement persistence, backup failure recovery and fail-closed restore behavior.

## 0.0.79 — OIDC Login Entropy Fail-Closed Closure

- fail closed before OIDC redirect when cryptographic entropy for state, nonce, or PKCE verifier generation is unavailable;
- fail closed if OIDC state/session signing cannot be completed instead of emitting an unusable authentication cookie;
- add owner-suite regression coverage proving entropy failure creates neither a redirect nor authentication cookies.

## 0.0.78 — Security, HA and release correctness closure

- Fail-closed production authentication boundaries and bootstrap credential scope.
- HA installer credential/storage correctness and air-gap/runtime checks.
- OCI/release identity, non-mutating smoke evidence and truthful provenance/SBOM.
- Fail-closed authoritative API/agent error handling and Codex Autopilot coverage closure.

Only the canonical release history is kept in this file. Older repository history remains available from Git.

## 0.0.332 — parallel C7W/S1/S2/H1 V41

- Expanded typed MCP parity for Data Protection, Compliance Scan/Recheck and Fleet Upgrade control/read surfaces while preserving authorization-filtered tools, durable product authority and independent approval.
- Added `MCP_EXTERNAL_CLIENT_INTEROPERABILITY_MATRIX_V2` so source-contract readiness for ChatGPT/Claude/Gemini/Grok is distinct from still-pending real external-client certification.
- Added `COMPONENT_RUNTIME_UPGRADE_MATRIX_V1`; upgrade edges require two distinct exact source locks and cannot be inferred from a current-only runtime executor.
- Added fail-closed release toolchain verifier; development compilation is no longer confusable with admitted production build-toolchain closure.
- Added `BOOT_MEDIA_PROVIDER_AUTHORITY_V1` source contract with secret-reference-only BMC access, immutable media digest validation, fenced transitions and restart-safe attach/boot/power/observe sequencing. Managed bare-metal orchestration remains blocked until wired to durable execution and physical evidence.
